#5223. Tracked Binance wallets record $2.5 billion in outflows as withdrawals reopen after hack

Binance is restoring withdrawals through new wallets, so the tracked outflows cannot be read as a clean measure of customer exits.

Binance's tracked cryptocurrency wallets recorded $2,510 million in net outflows on Nov. 1 after the exchange resumed withdrawals following its $6.3 billion security breach.

According to DeFiLlama data, the outflows included approximately $1,083 million in USDT, $912 million in Bitcoin, $385 million in USDC and $131 million in wrapped Ethereum.

These outflows coincided with Binance's phased reopening of withdrawal services after the Oct. 29 attack, which the FBI attributed to North Korea's TraderTraitor hacking operation.

In a Nov. 1 announcement, Binance said it was gradually restoring withdrawal access across 15 blockchain networks after completing additional security reviews.

The exchange initially reopened withdrawals for Bitcoin, Ethereum, BNB Smart Chain, XRP and Base, then confirmed support for Dogecoin and Optimism.

Other supported networks in the first phase include Solana, Tron, Arbitrum, Avalanche, Polygon, Zcash and Sui, with individual assets becoming available as security checks are completed.

Binance co-founder Yi He said withdrawal processing was proceeding without significant disruption, although increased demand could temporarily affect transaction processing. She wrote on X:

“Things are currently running fairly smoothly. If there are many withdrawal requests, congestion cannot be ruled out, after which it will smooth out again.”

He also urged customers to verify withdrawal addresses and ensure their devices and operating environments were secure before transferring funds.

Meanwhile, Binance has updated its proof-of-reserves wallet address list to include newly generated wallets, which it said would process all subsequent withdrawals.

The infrastructure changes followed the multibillion-dollar breach that forced Binance to temporarily suspend withdrawals while investigators examined its compromised hot-wallet systems.

Binance has maintained that customer funds remain protected and that the company will absorb the financial losses from the attack.

The exchange said it replenished about 95% of the affected assets through corporate resources and market purchases, up from 90% in its previous update.

It also reported that balances in Bitcoin, Ethereum, USDT, USDC, BNB, Solana, XRP, USD1, RLUSD and Zcash were fully backed, while the remaining unreplenished assets consisted primarily of less-liquid tokens.

However, replacing stolen assets does not mean the original funds have been recovered from the attackers. Binance continues working with law enforcement and industry partners to trace and potentially recover those assets.

The extent to which the observed on-chain outflows reflect customers moving assets elsewhere remains uncertain, particularly as Binance transitions withdrawals to newly generated wallets.

The exchange has pledged to publish a detailed post-incident investigation report, while its updated proof-of-reserves disclosures are expected to provide further information about the assets backing customer balances.

#5222. Can Bitcoin outperform gold as AI spending concerns shake equities?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The market is heading into another key macro week. Q3 2026 earnings were a little disappointing.

With costs rising in the AI industry, Amazon saw its free cash flow turn negative at $42.4 billion, causing concern that its huge AI investments may not be providing enough returns to match these valuations. As such, it will be interesting to see how the next set of earnings go.

Apple and Palantir will be reporting on Monday, the 2nd of November.

Notably, the figures are beginning to show just how large the investment gap could be.

According to Bloomberg, this will more than double to roughly $330 billion in FY27, suggesting that AI bubble concerns are more than just short-term market noise. So, with equities under pressure, could this further strengthen Bitcoin’s status as a macro hedge?

Interestingly, this week could offer some clues.

On a macro level, besides earnings reports, The Kobeissi Letter highlights 10 key pieces of data that would provide focus to October’s jobs and inflation figures. This comes at a time when the U.S. midterm elections are looming on the horizon, adding another layer of FUD to an already cautious market.

With the 6.8% drop on the Nasdaq in October and an influx of earnings reports, this could cause a change in market flows. This brings the macro-hedging narrative for Bitcoin back into play.

However, the key question is whether Bitcoin [BTC] can outperform traditional hedges as market uncertainty builds.

Bitcoin’s 2024-2026 setup against gold is gaining attention.

In 2024, Bitcoin outperformed gold by a wide margin. Up 121%, Bitcoin enjoyed a much stronger year than gold (up 27%) due to strong institutional demand and the launch of spot ETFs.

Interestingly, this makes the recent report from The Kobeissi Letter much more interesting given the increasing macro uncertainty that is being seen in the markets.

As shown in the chart below, the largest U.S. physical gold-backed ETF, GLD, saw $1.8 billion in outflows in October, its biggest monthly withdrawal since June. On a year-to-date basis, GLD has lost $3.8 billion, putting it on track for its first annual outflow since 2023.

Meanwhile, investors pulled another $1.5 billion from gold and precious metals funds last week, marking their second weekly outflow in the past seven weeks.

By contrast, Bitcoin ETFs saw a minor $515 million outflow in October.

So technically, GLD recorded nearly 3.5x more outflows than all Bitcoin ETFs combined in October. This is interesting as it comes amid increasing concerns about the AI bubble and pressure on tech stocks, making the coming week particularly important for Bitcoin’s macro hedge narrative.

In this context, Bitcoin could gain more attention as investors reassess traditional safe havens.

Rising AI costs and market uncertainty are putting pressure on equities, bringing Bitcoin’s macro hedge narrative back into focus.

Gold ETFs saw $1.8 billion in outflows in October, compared to just $515 million from Bitcoin ETFs, showing a difference in investor flows.

#5221. 5 Best AI Crypto Trading Bots

Home » Cryptopedia » 5 Best AI Crypto Trading Bots

Compare five AI crypto trading bots by automation features, strategy tools, supported exchanges, ease of use, and key limitations.

AI crypto trading bots help traders automate strategies, analyze market data, and manage trade execution without monitoring charts around the clock. However, not every crypto trading bot marketed as AI-powered uses artificial intelligence to make trading decisions. Some platforms rely primarily on predefined rules, while others use AI to build strategies, interpret market conditions, or configure automated trading systems.

The best AI crypto trading bot depends on what you need: strategy automation, AI-assisted decision-making, beginner-friendly controls, or trading across multiple exchanges. This guide compares five platforms based on their available features, intended use cases, and practical limitations.

We looked at each platform’s automation features, strategy tools, backtesting options, supported exchanges, ease of use, pricing, and risk-management features. We also checked the information against each platform’s official website. Our picks are based on the features each bot offers and who it may suit best, rather than independently tested trading results. Keep in mind that features, fees, and exchange support can change over time.

3Commas is a crypto trading platform that combines automated bots with tools for managing and executing trades. It is widely used for multi-exchange order execution and parameter-driven  3Commas AI Trading Bot setups.

Its features include Dollar-Cost Averaging (DCA) bots, grid bots, signal-based trading, and SmartTrade order management.

The platform also offers an AI trading assistant that lets users describe a strategy in natural language. According to 3Commas, the assistant can configure a bot, run a backtest using historical market data, and prepare the strategy for launch.

Limitation: Bot capacity on the entry plan is small, and higher limits require Pro or Expert. Backtests also cannot fully reproduce future market conditions, slippage, liquidity constraints, or unexpected volatility.

3Commas is worth considering for traders who want to automate established trading strategies while retaining control over entry conditions, position sizing, and exits. It is particularly relevant to users who already understand concepts such as DCA, grid trading, and technical indicators.

Cryptohopper is a cloud-based engine that combines automated crypto trading with strategy-building, backtesting, and AI-assisted tools.

Its AI Strategy Designer helps traders choose strategies, signal providers, and TradingView alerts based on current market conditions. However, access depends on the subscription plan, so it’s worth checking the requirements before signing up.

Cryptohopper also has Cryptohopper.AI, a separate tool that lets users create trading bots and other trading tools simply by describing what they want in plain language. Although both use AI, they serve different purposes and shouldn’t be confused with each other.

Limitation: Access to certain AI features depends on the product and subscription tier. AI-generated strategies still require testing and monitoring before live deployment.

Cryptohopper trading bot suits traders who want to experiment with different strategies and automate their selection or execution. It is also relevant to users interested in building customized crypto trading tools without writing all the code themselves.

Pionex is a cryptocurrency exchange with AI trading bots built directly into its platform. Unlike third-party automation services that require users to connect exchange accounts, Pionex integrates bot functionality into the exchange itself.

Pionex offers 16 bots, including Grid Trading, Infinity Grid, Trailing Buy/Sell, Rebalancing, and Spot-Futures Arbitrage bots. The options also include PionexGPT, an AI-related feature that allows users to write custom trading strategies using natural language prompts.

Limitations:  Funds are held on the Pionex exchange rather than on an exchange you already use, so the AI trading bots cannot be applied to other exchanges.

Pionex is a good option for beginners who want to try automated trading without paying for a separate bot service or connecting to another exchange. It can also be useful for experienced traders who prefer to manage everything on one platform.

Coinrule focuses on rule-based crypto trading automation. Users can create strategies by setting simple rules rather than building a trading system entirely from scratch.

With features like Coinrule AI Cloud, users can also use plain language to create trading strategies, get help setting them up, test them against historical data, and connect with compatible AI assistants.

Limitation: Rule-based automation follows its configured conditions; it does not inherently understand market context or predict future prices. It doesn’t provide the level of mathematical detail and precise order book analysis that professional institutional traders need.

Coinrule is suitable for traders who know what conditions they want to trade but do not want to write the underlying code. It is also useful for users who want to test systematic strategies before connecting them to live trading.

Bitsgap is a crypto trading platform that brings everything together in one place, including automated crypto AI trading bots, portfolio management, analytics, and exchange connections. It offers different trading bots, such as Grid and DCA, to help users automate their strategies. Its AI Assistant also helps users configure their trading setups.

With support for multiple crypto exchanges, Bitsgap lets users manage their trading activities through a single interface.

Limitation: Futures bots are not available on the Basic plan.Exchange support and available features vary.

Bitsgap is a good option for traders who use multiple exchanges and want to manage their trading bots in one place. Its automation tools and analytics also make it easier to track strategies and monitor performance.

The best AI crypto trading bot is the one whose capabilities match your trading approach, not necessarily the one with the most AI features.

Before choosing a platform, compare these factors:

AI crypto trading bots can automate execution and reduce the need for constant manual monitoring, but profitability is never guaranteed. Results depend on strategy design, market conditions, execution costs, liquidity, and risk management.

Historical backtests are useful for evaluating a strategy, but they do not establish that it will perform similarly in live markets. Traders should test bots in simulation where possible, start with limited exposure, and monitor performance after deployment.

An AI label alone is not evidence of a trading advantage. Before selecting a bot, establish what the AI actually does, how trades are triggered, and how losses are managed.

The answer depends on the use case. Pionex requires no API setup or subscription, 3Commas builds bots from plain-English prompts, Bitsgap covers 17+ exchanges, and Cryptohopper offers a strategy marketplace.

No profitability can be assumed. Results depend on settings, fees, and market conditions, and losses are possible.

It depends on the platform. 3Commas states it is non-custodial and connects through APIs, while Pionex operates as an exchange where funds are deposited.

#5220. XRP Ledger Posts 479% Payment Surge as XRP Heads For Recovery

XRP appears to be starting the new month on a stronger foot as its network activity and price movements over the past day have both turned out to be promising.

Latest onchain data from XRPScan shows a substantial increase in the asset's payment volume on the XRP ledger after closing October with weaker figures.

According to the data, the amount of XRP transferred between accounts on the XRP ledger has surged from around 229 million XRP on October 31 to over 1.33 billion XRP on November 1st.

This marks a substantial increase of over 479% in the metric within the 24-hour period of crossing into November, suggesting that the network is starting the new month with stronger momentum.

While the surge in the XRP payment volume has come at a time when the asset is beginning to show signs of a potential recovery after several weeks of high volatility, it appears that the XRP ledger is also seeing a rebound in its network usage.

Furthermore, the data also suggests that the impact of the recent enormous Binance heist on the network is gradually fading, as activities on the XRP ledger slowed following the incident.

The massive surge in the XRP payment volume could sometimes provide a bullish outlook for XRP's price as it typically signals an increase in the amount of XRP transferred from one account to another, which shows that traders are actively engaging.

While this does not guarantee an instant rally and XRP is yet to show any major price recovery as it is still showing mixed price action, market analysts are hopeful that stronger network activity could rebuild momentum and help the asset restore the gaining streak broken in October.

#5219. Cardano’s 2027 budget proposal would let funding stewards seek grants too

Proposed stewards could influence assessments while DReps retain spending votes and a direct funding route awaits its allocation.

Cardano’s proposed 2027 budget process would let three institutions help design how funding requests are assessed while remaining eligible to seek money from the same spending domains.

The proposed Cardano Ecosystem Budget Process, or CEBP, published by Intersect on Oct. 28, would assign stewardship roles to Intersect, PRAGMA and the Cardano Foundation across six areas of spending. Delegated representatives, or DReps, would retain approval of the stewards, budget distribution and every treasury withdrawal. The institutions’ influence would come earlier, through the plans and assessment processes that help shape those votes.

For independent applicants, the balance depends on how conflicts are handled and how much funding remains available through Open Contributions, a proposed route that would let anyone approach DReps without a steward. The published framework leaves that route’s allocation and common procedures for assessing steward-related funding unspecified.

In its Oct. 30 update, Intersect said feedback remained open ahead of a tentative Nov. 10 submission of the overall concept. The process would run as a trial in 2027 only if DReps approve.

Each proposed steward would help develop its domain’s strategy, recommend a dollar-denominated budget cap in coordination with other stewards, and define how applications are collected and assessed. It could submit proposals itself or support others in bringing them to an on-chain vote.

Delivery oversight would also depend on the structure chosen for each proposal. The framework allows different arrangements, from teams retaining their own administration to more coordinated delivery contracts.

These responsibilities could influence which needs are prioritized, how competing requests are compared and what information reaches voters. DReps would still decide whether the resulting proposals receive funding.

Intersect presents the approach as a response to overloaded DReps facing numerous, hard-to-compare requests with insufficient expert guidance. Common presentation standards and domain expertise could make those decisions easier. The same assessment role makes the treatment of an institution’s own funding proposals consequential.

The proposal expressly allows a steward to receive funding from its domain, provided that funding is transparently declared and accompanied by safeguards. An award would still require DRep approval.

The published framework promises a common public code of conduct in the planned information action, a proposal seeking DRep support for the concept. Common recusal, independent assessment and appeals procedures for funding proposals involving stewards remain unspecified in the published text.

There are existing, narrower provisions. Intersect’s Growth & Marketing Committee working terms, which remain subject to ratification and adjustment, require potential conflicts to be disclosed to committee leadership. They specifically address reviews involving members’ working relationships and allow sanctions for code violations.

The Budget Committee grievance process covers conflicts and concerns about process fairness, with escalation through Intersect’s leadership to its board. The procedure applies specifically to that committee’s activities and stakeholder interactions.

Existing payment oversight applies after funding approval. The existing 2026 budget FAQ says the five-member independent Oversight Committee checks administrative disbursements, with funding selection outside its remit.

Open Contributions would be the seventh domain, with no steward. Any proposer could go straight to DReps. The Net Change Limit, or NCL, sets the overall treasury withdrawal limit. The framework says the information action setting it would suggest moving a rejected stewarded domain’s proposed budget into that open route.

Those provisions preserve alternatives to the institutional process. Their practical importance will depend partly on the numbers: domain caps and any guaranteed share for Open Contributions remain unspecified. The framework anticipates an allowance to be developed alongside the other budgets.

In DRepTalk consultation feedback, the participant posting as SIPO proposed giving Open Contributions its own explicit share of the overall withdrawal limit. SIPO also sought disclosure of stewards’ roles and relationships with applicants. Those suggestions remain part of the consultation.

DReps’ own commitments would matter too. Intersect says direct on-chain enforcement of the framework is unavailable. It would rely on supporting DReps making a social commitment to follow the process in the relevant domains, including withholding support from withdrawals outside the agreed process.

That could make the agreed application rules influential without introducing a technical barrier to submission. DReps would remain able to reject a domain plan or move to another model, while independent applicants would still need to persuade them to approve funding.

The tentative Nov. 10 action would seek support for the concept and task stewards with preparing detailed domain plans. DReps’ commitment to the framework for 2027 would be sought through the later domain actions.

Intersect envisages a second round in December 2026 or January 2027 covering domain arrangements and the 2027 NCL. The framework sets a goal of at least 51% DRep support for the concept and at least 60% DRep support for each domain’s stewardship mandate. It describes 51% DRep support for the NCL. Those statements concern the proposed information-action process and social commitments. Individual treasury withdrawals would continue to require their own approvals.

The published versions also leave an action-count discrepancy. Six stewarded domains plus an NCL action imply seven; the blog describes eight. Intersect’s forum copy says seven before referring to an eighth. Neither version consistently explains the total.

Existing contracts are separate from those future choices. On Oct. 17, Intersect reported 25 vendor smart contracts worth 204,639,279 ADA from the 2026 budget cycle.

For applicants, the proposed next round would set out how much money each route could offer and how assessment would work when a steward also seeks funding. DReps would retain the final funding decision; the code of conduct and the size of Open Contributions will help determine how much choice applicants have before that vote.

#5218. Morning Minute: Hedge Funds Left the BTC ETF Trade and Sovereign Wealth Stepped In

Morning Minute: Hedge Funds Left the BTC ETF Trade and Sovereign Wealth Stepped In

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

Every quarter, big institutions have to tell the SEC what they own. Add up those filings from just before Bitcoin’s October 2025 high through the end of June, and the picture is clear: nine hedge funds and banks sold about 68,000 Bitcoin worth of ETF shares over those nine months, roughly $5.4 billion at today’s prices. Millennium, Brevan Howard, Goldman Sachs, D.E. Shaw and five others led the selling. Some trimmed by a third. Some sold everything.

The trade had nothing to do with believing in Bitcoin. You buy the ETF, you simultaneously bet against Bitcoin in the futures market, and you pocket the gap between those two prices. Done right you make money whether Bitcoin rises or falls—as long as the gap beats what you’d earn just parking the cash in Treasury bills.

Before the high, that gap paid 7.9% a year, a full 3.9 points better than T-bills. By late October it paid 4.8%, barely half a point better. After fees and financing, Galaxy’s Alex Thorn figures what’s left is about 1.5 points over cash. Not worth the trouble.

“That arithmetic, not the price, is why the multi-strategy desks left,” he wrote.

You can see it in the futures data too. Both sides of that trade shrank by nearly identical amounts, around 57% each.

Now look at who came in and bought. Morgan Stanley’s wealth management arm. Mubadala. The Abu Dhabi Investment Council. Roughly 2,200 other filers added about 20,000 Bitcoin between them. Hedge funds sold; sovereign funds and wealth managers bought. And the investors too small to file at all—advisers, family offices, regular people—cut their holdings by just 6.6% while the price fell 49%. So Middle America is hodling.

That’s the whole story. The people who were never really Bitcoin investors left. The people who were didn’t flinch.

Since June, the funds have added back 87,000 Bitcoin—this time as straightforward buying, not arbitrage.

The rate picture is turning too. The Fed held Wednesday, but December has quietly softened: a month ago traders put 59% odds on two rate hikes by year-end. Today that’s zero. Expected tightening has been cut roughly in half. Less tightening helps every risk asset.

October’s flows already show the shift. The month was down $907 million at the midpoint. Through Tuesday it’s essentially flat, with $724 million arriving in just the last eight sessions.

The fast money is gone. The believers are left. And that’s the foundation you can build on for a bull market rally…

#5217. Binance net outflows cross $3.64B after withdrawals restart – Can user confidence return?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On Sunday, the 1st of November, at 06:00 UTC, Binance resumed withdrawals, allowing users to withdraw their assets. Immediately upon opening for withdrawals again, there was an increase in the volume of withdrawal transactions.

Within the first two hours of withdrawals reopening, $733 million was withdrawn before gradually declining as the initial rush eased. However, a high volume of withdrawals continued to be initiated throughout the day.

This indicated users were continuing to withdraw their assets from the exchange even though they can do so.

As of 15:30 UTC, withdrawals totaled $4.649 billion, and deposits totaled $1.01 billion, thus resulting in a net withdrawal amount of $3.638 billion. This represents 2.1% of Binance’s stated $171 billion in assets.

Although the net outflow indicates a large number of post-resumption withdrawals were made from the exchange, it may not indicate a loss of confidence. The fact that deposits continue to occur, along with decreasing withdrawal rates, suggests that trading activity appears to be normalizing.

However, if a large volume of additional withdrawals are made, it will decrease the exchange’s reserve levels and liquidity.

Soon after the withdrawals began, some assets experienced outflows in larger volumes. Notably, Tether [USDT] led with the most outflows, with 1.96 billion tokens leaving the exchange.

USD Coin [USDC] trailed closely by 557.65 million tokens. Still, during this time, Binance saw 19,569 Bitcoins [BTC] worth approximately $1.53 billion leave the exchange. Meanwhile, while Ethereum [ETH] outflows totalled 154,147 ETH, valued near $360 million.

This distribution shows that users moved both stablecoins and major cryptocurrencies rather than concentrating on one asset.

Given that many users withdrew both types of assets, many seem to want to maximize their liquidity and ease transferability. Simultaneously, Bitcoin and ETH outflows further reduced assets held on Binance

Continued removals could negatively affect exchanges‘ reserves and delay the end of the examination into the security measures taken by Binance.

Now, instead of focusing solely on the breach itself, Binance is facing pressure to determine what portion of the stolen funds cannot be recovered. Both Tether and Circle have issued freezes on approximately $2.7-$3 billion in stablecoins.

This severely limits attackers’ ability to withdraw any part of the funds. Binance claims that through company funds and SAFU reserve accounts, they have been able to recover over 90% of the impacted assets.

Nonetheless, most of the remaining exposure is comprised of Bitcoin and ETH. These assets are far more difficult to freeze since they are currently controlled by attackers.

Furthermore, independent verification of the amounts reported by Binance regarding recovery is extremely important. Until transaction records clarify the outstanding balance, confidence will depend on evidence rather than reported progress.

Binance recorded $3.64 billion in net outflows after reopening withdrawals following the hack.

Binance recovered over 90% of stolen assets, but the remaining losses threaten user confidence.

#5216. Shiba Inu (SHIB) Is Back to 85 Trillion Token Range: Here's What It Means for Holders

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Shiba Inu (SHIB) exchange reserves have dropped back to about 85.84 trillion tokens in early November, a level that may be healthier for the meme coin's supply. The price, meanwhile, is still under heavy selling pressure.

The latest on-chain data puts SHIB exchange reserves at 85.8432 trillion tokens, up only 0.01% over the past 24 hours. The seven-day chart tells a different story: reserves have fallen noticeably, which suggests a lot of SHIB has left trading platforms.

Lower exchange reserves generally mean fewer tokens are on hand to be sold. When investors pull SHIB into private wallets, there is less supply available to sellers on centralized exchanges. That doesn't guarantee a price recovery, but it can make the supply picture more favorable.

The reserves are currently valued at about $397.45 million, down 1.69% on the day. Exchange netflow is still positive at roughly 10.94 billion SHIB, so short-term selling pressure hasn't gone away.

The supply picture is improving, but the chart is still bearish. On the daily SHIB/USDT chart, the token trades near $0.00000463 after breaking below its ascending support trendline. It is also under several important moving averages, which means sellers still control the broader structure.

Immediate resistance sits around $0.00000500–$0.00000530. The $0.00000440–$0.00000450 region is the next potential support zone.

If demand starts to recover, the drop in exchange-held supply could matter more. For now, the 85 trillion mark is a constructive on-chain sign, not a confirmed bullish reversal. A more convincing case would need continued exchange withdrawals, stronger buying, and a move back above the key resistance levels.

#5215. RLUSD supply fell 29% on XRPL. One XRP trading pool barely changed

Ledger readings showed similar XRP/RLUSD pool reserves and uneven funded offers, while the reported supply decline's cause remains unresolved.

XRPL's reported RLUSD supply fell almost 29% in a day, but a sampled XRP/RLUSD trading pool did not lose a comparable share of its reserves. Across ledger observations from Oct. 29 to Oct. 31, the pool's XRP balance slipped less than 1%, while its RLUSD balance increased slightly.

The contrast matters for XRP holders deciding whether they can trade into Ripple's dollar stablecoin. DefiLlama's Oct. 31 RLUSD chain breakdown showed 707 million tokens on XRPL, down 28.7% over its displayed one-day window. Yet funded RLUSD offers available to XRP sellers increased in the captured order-book sample. The opposite side of that book remained below its earlier level. XRP was trading near $1.25 in the Oct. 31 market snapshot.

The two pool readings showed broadly similar inventory, while the funded book changed differently on each side. A later estimate could still fill a 10,000-RLUSD order in either direction.

NOSHASHI's ledger readings compared the same XRP/RLUSD pool at Oct. 29, 23:59:51 UTC, and Oct. 31, 13:11:11 UTC. The comparison uses quantities of each token, rather than a dollar valuation that can also move with XRP's price.

Source: NOSHASHI's Oct. 29 closing-ledger reading and captured Oct. 31 market reading. All times are UTC in 2026.

Those balances did not mirror the scale of the reported supply reduction. The pool still contained about 1.87 million XRP and 2.34 million RLUSD in the later observation.

An automated market maker lets traders exchange against a pool holding both assets. As the XRPL's AMM documentation explains, a larger pool generally absorbs a given trade with less movement in its exchange rate. Those actual balances therefore matter more to a pool user's execution than RLUSD sitting elsewhere on the ledger. XRP is committed directly when it sits in such a pool; it can also bridge between issued tokens when auto-bridging offers a cheaper route.

The supply dashboard and market samples do not share an exact cutoff. DefiLlama exposed the date and one-day change, but no intraday observation time or ledger for its supply window. The comparison spans the reported contraction's dates; it cannot isolate the market's response to a particular issuance or redemption transaction.

The pool was only one source of trading capacity. The order book contained offers backed by funds their owners could actually provide, and its two sides moved differently.

For someone selling XRP to receive RLUSD, the relevant offers sell RLUSD in exchange for XRP. NOSHASHI's capped sample showed 62,952.98 RLUSD of these funded asks at the Oct. 29 close. That increased to 98,461.88 RLUSD at the Oct. 30 close, then 112,162.55 RLUSD at the Oct. 31 observation.

Someone selling RLUSD to acquire XRP faced the other side. Funded offers to buy RLUSD with XRP, expressed in RLUSD units, declined from 71,292.22 RLUSD to 40,870.63 RLUSD between the two daily closes. They recovered to 59,593.01 RLUSD by the Oct. 31 sample, still below the Oct. 29 level.

This is why the direction of the trade changes the answer. The captured book offered more RLUSD to an XRP seller, while the capacity available to an RLUSD seller had only partly recovered.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

There are limits to those totals. NOSHASHI's book-depth measurement examined up to 100 offers per side within 10% of the contemporaneous reference mid, the midpoint used for comparison. It kept AMM reserves separate. The totals do not mean the entire amount could trade near the best price, and a moving reference price changes the band being measured.

A later fixed-size estimate gives a more concrete example. At ledger 108023839, evaluated Oct. 31 at 13:11:54 UTC, NOSHASHI's 10,000-RLUSD order-book estimate showed buying 10,000 RLUSD would cost 8,033.58 XRP, 0.14% above its reference mid. Selling 10,000 RLUSD would return 8,015.85 XRP, 0.08% below that mid.

Both modeled orders were fully fillable in the captured book. These were resting-offer estimates, excluding the pool, rather than executed trades or guaranteed quotes. Without an equivalent earlier estimate, they cannot show that execution became cheaper or more expensive because RLUSD supply changed.

Why XRPL's RLUSD supply fell remains unresolved. DefiLlama simultaneously showed Ethereum RLUSD supply near 1.71 billion, up 5.73% over its displayed day. Opposing chain changes do not establish that the same funds migrated from XRPL to Ethereum.

On the XRP Ledger, returning tokens to their issuer destroys them and reduces ledger obligations. For a stablecoin, that begins redemption; it does not itself prove dollars have been paid off-chain. XRPL Dashboard's net supply measure records obligation changes rather than enumerating separate gross mint and burn totals. Its supply fields also lack a supply-specific ledger or observation time.

A transaction-level reconciliation would need to identify issuance, returns and any clawbacks, alongside consistent treatment of operational wallets. The issuer-balance API permits those wallets to be excluded, but the dashboards' actual exclusion treatment was not established. The available observations cannot distinguish inventory consolidation, net redemption and measurement treatment as the explanation, or identify XRP selling as a cause.

Ripple's issuer-wide transparency figures are earlier: 2,627 million circulating RLUSD and $2,759 million in reserve funds as of Oct. 29. They cannot be paired with Oct. 31 chain supply as simultaneous observations. Issuer backing is also a separate question from funds available to execute a trade.

For holders, the repeatable test is the cost of the same trade size in both directions at subsequent ledgers, alongside funded offers and pool balances. A sustained reduction in those balances or worse comparable quotes would change the assessment. The captured pool balances did not show a proportionate loss alongside the reported supply decline; the evidence does not yet establish an equivalent conclusion for execution quality across the wider XRP market.

#5213. Zcash (ZEC) Whale Builds $14 Million Long Ahead of 300% Speed Upgrade

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. An unknown investor has built a position in Zcash (ZEC) worth about $14 million, splitting the capital between the spot market and the decentralized derivatives exchange Hyperliquid. The purchases came amid preparations for the Network Upgrade 7 (NU7) hard fork: on November 5, it is set to speed up block processing threefold.

Judging by on-chain data, the address t1JJnA linked to the investor first bought 3,052 ZEC (about $3.8 million) on the spot market at an average price of $1,250. Then, on Hyperliquid, it opened a 2x leveraged long on 8,000 ZEC worth $10.16 million in two different wallets.

So far, 3,000 ZEC of this position can be verified, with an average entry price of $1,353.83.

In late October, ZEC pulled back to the $1,220–$1,280 range, and the derivatives position went into the red. At its peak, the paper loss exceeded $1.8 million; it now stands at $249,388.

But liquidation will only occur if the price falls to $647.67, which is nearly half of current levels. The investor is clearly betting on long-term growth rather than a quick rebound.

The main argument is the NU7 hard fork, whose final tests the developers completed on the testnet in October. The ZIP-218 update will cut block time from 75 to 25 seconds: the network’s throughput will triple, while the issuance schedule will remain the same.

Along with the hard fork, the Network Sustainability Mechanism (a reserve fee fund for miners) will go live, and the outdated Sprout pool will be shut down in favor of the shielded Orchard architecture.

Throughout 2026, each new Zcash low came in higher than the previous one. An ascending triangle has formed on the chart, and time after time the price runs into resistance at $1,396. Traders on the derivatives market have split into two camps.

Some expect a sharp upside breakout amid inflows into the Grayscale Zcash Trust. Others fear that right after the upgrade activates on November 5, the “buy the rumor, sell the news” principle will kick in.

#5212. Treasury dealers’ financing burden can outlast their bond holdings, Fed study finds

Customer financing persisted beyond inventory effects in historical data, leaving Bitcoin spillover dependent on evidence from crypto credit markets.

Federal Reserve staff research published Oct. 30 finds that Treasury dealers can keep financing customers' bonds after their own auction inventory has largely cleared. That lingering lending uses balance-sheet capacity, so falling inventories can give an incomplete picture of dealers' ability to finance Treasury positions.

The study estimates that each dollar of Treasury coupon issuance expanded dealer balance sheets by about 21 cents, with roughly two-thirds attributable to client financing. Its main regression sample covers eight large bank-affiliated dealers from January 2016 through June 2025. These are historical estimates, rather than measurements of October 2026 stress.

The mechanism separates owning a bond from financing its owner. Dealers can sell Treasuries to customers while continuing to supply repo financing, short-term loans secured by the bonds. As the New York Fed explains, dealers also borrow cash to fund their lending to leveraged clients. Moving the security off inventory need not end that intermediary role.

In the Fed study, the effect of coupon issuance on dealers' long positions dissipates within roughly three days after issuance. The increase in reverse repo, the financing dealers provide clients, remains measurable during the following week.

The study associates issuance with greater use of Treasury trading desks' internal risk limits, which can reduce spare intermediation capacity. Its illustrative leverage-ratio calculations, however, put temporary issuance effects well below aggregate regulatory headroom. Available room across dealers does not establish spare capacity at each desk.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

New York Fed observations put the Secured Overnight Financing Rate, or SOFR, between 3.92% and 3.95% across the five business days from Oct. 23 through Oct. 29. The latest reading in that window was 3.94%.

The Federal Home Loan Bank of New York's Oct. 30 update cites a 3.90% interest rate on reserve balances and attributes SOFR trading above it to Treasury supply, month-end and Canadian year-end. Yet it also describes financing conditions as tranquil enough for the Fed's reserve-management purchases to remain paused.

If the same constrained intermediaries fund Bitcoin traders, cuts in their lending could transmit Treasury pressure into crypto. That remains an inference about financing capacity, not an observed result of the Fed study. The Bank for International Settlements' April 2023 Crypto carry research identifies scarce arbitrage capital and leveraged investor demand as drivers of the gap between futures and spot prices. It also finds some segmentation between crypto and traditional markets, with interest-rate differentials explaining little of carry variation.

Treasury repo rates measure secured borrowing costs. Futures basis measures a price gap. Perpetual funding, meanwhile, transfers payments between longs and shorts to anchor the contract to its index.

A stronger spillover case would pair persistent repo pressure with worsening comparable crypto borrowing or rollover terms. Establishing causation would require evidence connecting constrained intermediaries to reduced Bitcoin financing. Stable crypto credit terms would weaken the spillover explanation, even if Treasury financing remained expensive.

#5211. Ripple Partner Reaches One Million People With Safe Water

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple Partner Reaches One Million People With Safe Water

The access to clean water isn’t taken for granted in many jurisdictions across the globe. Water. Org is here to fix it.

Ripple and Water.org are expanding a collaboration that has already helped more than one million people gain access to safe water or sanitation financing across Latin America.

The nonprofit, co-founded by Gary White and Matt Damon, is now layering Ripple Payments, the RLUSD stablecoin, and digital asset custody tools into its operations so capital can move faster and cheaper into emerging markets.

Water.org has used Ripple Payments to move more than $3.2 million across four markets.

Settlement times in Mexico and Brazil dropped from two to three days to same-day, while payment processing became available around the clock instead of being limited by a single daily banking cutoff.

Transaction fees fell by as much as 66 percent, so a larger share of every dollar reaches borrowers rather than intermediaries.

Ripple committed $6 million over three years to support Water.org programs such as WaterCredit, which partners with local financial institutions to offer affordable loans for household taps, toilets, and piped connections.

Finance as a tool to solve the water crisis 💧@Water co-founders Matt Damon and Gary White joined @bgarlinghouse on the Swell main stage to share how Ripple Payments and RLUSD are helping @Water reach more people, faster. pic.twitter.com/VXdttesBK6

Separately, Water.org received a $1.25 million grant in Ripple’s RLUSD through the Stablecoin Impact Fund. That capital is intended to expand stablecoin-enabled WaterCredit disbursements into Indonesia, the Philippines, and Uganda.

Water.org will also begin using Ripple’s Wallet-as-a-Service technology to manage digital assets as the programs scale. Beyond the direct funding, Ripple joined Get Blue, the Water.org movement modeled after the (RED) campaign, as its exclusive digital-asset and fintech partner.

Founding brand partners include Gap, Amazon, Starbucks, and Ecolab. Ripple’s first year of Get Blue support helped reach 400,000 people, with the three-year commitment expected to support 1.2 million as Water.org works toward its broader goal of reaching 200 million people by 2030.

Gary White, co-founder and CEO of Water.org, said the actual water crisis is fundamentally a funding-flow problem rather than a lack of capital, and that Ripple’s infrastructure has shown what efficient movement of money looks like in practice.

Brad Garlinghouse, Ripple’s CEO, noted that the partnership demonstrates the original promise of crypto and blockchain: making money move faster, more efficiently, and globally for real-world needs.

💧🌍 WATER IS LIFE. CRYPTO IS CHANGING THE GAME! 🚀Hollywood superstar Matt Damon in conversation with @bgarlinghouse is helping tackle the global water crisis through https://t.co/AMHUwOVte1, and Ripple is part of a bigger conversation about using financial innovation to… pic.twitter.com/U42l8YvnZM

White and Matt Damon joined Garlinghouse for a fireside chat at Ripple Swell in New York.

The expanded setup combines traditional philanthropic capital with on-chain settlement and custody so that repaid loans can be recycled into new lending through vehicles such as the WaterEquity Everspring Fund.

For Water.org, the practical result is more of each donated or invested dollar reaching families that need household water connections. For Ripple, it is another demonstration of its payments and stablecoin stack operating outside pure trading venues and inside humanitarian finance.

#5210. Justin Sun's HTX Agrees to Leave UK in Settlement With Financial Watchdog

Justin Sun's HTX Agrees to Leave UK in Settlement With Financial Watchdog

Crypto exchange HTX is leaving the UK after settling a lawsuit brought by the Financial Conduct Authority over illegal crypto promotions, the regulator said on Thursday.

The FCA said it had secured undertakings in the High Court that HTX, formerly known as Huobi, will not knowingly promote crypto services unlawfully to UK consumers. Huobi Global S.A., the Panama-registered company named as the first defendant, has undertaken not to communicate financial promotions in breach of UK law across HTX's website, apps and social media, according to the court order.

HTX, which names Tron founder Justin Sun as a global adviser, is no longer active in the UK, the regulator said. UK consumers can no longer make deposits, and new UK users cannot sign up.

"Crypto firms should be under no illusion: being based overseas does not put them beyond the reach of UK law," said Steve Smart, the FCA's executive director of enforcement and market oversight. "The message to other firms is clear: follow the rules or face the consequences."

HTX said it had "proactively implemented measures to align with the UK's financial promotion regulations," the Financial Times reported. On its website, the exchange said existing UK customers are blocked from trading and have 48 hours to withdraw their assets before their accounts are closed.

The terms of the settlement are confidential, and it does not amount to an admission of liability or wrongdoing, the FCA said.

The regulator began proceedings against HTX in October 2025, its first enforcement case against a crypto firm under the UK's financial promotion rules, saying the exchange had repeatedly failed to engage with it. The two sides began settlement talks in March, which were extended in June, according to court orders reported by Reuters.

In May, the UK sanctioned Huobi Global S.A. over alleged support for Russia's financial sector, with the government's notice listing HTX as another name for the designated entity.

Two months later, the European Union named Huobi Global in its 21st package of Russia sanctions, accusing it of "significantly frustrating" the bloc's measures and barring EU transactions with the exchange, though stopping short of a full asset freeze. HTX has said Huobi Global S.A. is "distinct from the online HTX exchange."

October was a reality check for the AI trade.

#5207. Zcash whale buys $3.8M in ZEC – 2 obstacles could slow the recovery

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Zcash [ZEC] reached a local high of $1,284 before easing slightly. At press time, it traded around $1,277, up 2.3% over 24 hours.

The recovery attracted renewed whale interest. However, broader Spot activity suggested that other investors remained cautious.

According to Lookonchain, a whale bought 3,052 ZEC worth $3.8 million on the Spot market. The purchase showed substantial conviction from one buyer, although broader flows painted a different picture.

Zcash recorded $110 million in Spot Inflows compared with $89 million in Outflows. Spot Netflow stood near $20 million, reflecting higher exchange deposits.

These deposits increased the supply available for potential sale. So, while the whale bought into the recovery, broader exchange flows left Zcash exposed to selling pressure.

The same whale also opened a 2x long position on 8,000 ZEC worth $10.16 million. That position added a leveraged bullish bet alongside the Spot purchase.

Meanwhile, participation increased across the derivatives market. Over 24 hours, Open Interest rose 6% to $2.5 billion, while Derivatives Volume climbed 19% to $4.03 billion.

Together, these increases pointed to greater activity and expanding outstanding positions. They did not establish whether traders predominantly favored longs or shorts.

On top of that, Futures Inflows exceeded $954 million, while Futures Netflow rose 1,205% to $11.4 million. More capital was entering the market, although the whale’s bullish positioning alone could not establish a broader directional consensus.

Zcash recovered after falling below $1,200, but its price still faced technical resistance. The MA Cross showed the altcoin trading below its short-term moving averages.

That left the recovery facing another test as exchange deposits increased. If selling pressure persisted, Zcash could retreat toward $1,208.

A stronger recovery could instead bring $1,318 into focus. Sustained Futures Inflows would need support from price strength before suggesting that buyers had regained control.

#5206. Binance's 2.5 Billion XRP Audit Logs Crypto Reserves Fully Backed Above 100%

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. Binance has published its latest Proof of Reserves report as of October 31, 2026. Judging by it, client funds are still fully backed — despite the largest hack in crypto history at the end of the month. Coverage for XRP is 100.99%, and for the Ripple USD (RLUSD) stablecoin, 100.83%.

A comparison of balances as of October 1 and October 31 shows how the exchange's liabilities and reserves changed after the October 29 attack: hackers then withdrew about $6.3 billion from its wallets across ten blockchains.

Immediately after the attack, Binance suspended withdrawals and deposits, so the growth of balances in Ripple assets occurred before the hack. October results:

The main thing in the report is something else: Binance confirmed full coverage of the ten most traded assets, including BTC, ETH, USDT, BNB and SOL — and did so just two days after the hack.

About $3 billion of the stolen funds was quickly frozen directly on the blockchains with the help of partners, including Tether, Circle and Ripple. To cover the losses and return reserves to 1:1, Binance allocated more than $6.34 billion of its own capital. This reserve included the SAFU fund, the Binance Futures insurance fund and retained earnings.

The remaining 5% of losses are in low-liquidity tokens: they are being quietly bought back through over-the-counter (OTC) desks so as not to crash spot prices.

Each client can verify their own balance: the report is built on Merkle trees and zero-knowledge proofs (zk-SNARKs). For the market, this case sets a new bar: an exchange is no longer judged by whether it gets hacked, but by how quickly it stops an attack and whether it has enough of its own money to plug the hole.

#5205. Bitcoin Lightning’s year-old security fixes still missing from 71% of flagged nodes

New research reveals widespread delays in patch adoption as public vulnerability warnings fail to accelerate software upgrades.

Nearly three-quarters of public Bitcoin Lightning nodes identified as exposed in August remained on affected software lines in October.

An Oct. 21 study found that 71% of a group of 550 public LND nodes flagged during an Aug. 11 security disclosure were still operating on affected software release lines as of Oct. 13.

Only about 10% had upgraded during that period, while 108 nodes were no longer classified as live. The findings suggest that security fixes are reaching some Lightning operators well after their release dates.

The delays are particularly striking because patches for six vulnerabilities covered by the August disclosures had been available since LND version 0.19.0, released 446 days before the public warning.

LND is one of the software implementations used to operate nodes on Bitcoin's Lightning Network, which processes payments outside the blockchain's main transaction layer.

Research by Saurabh Kumar of Bitcoin Data Labs found that the network's largest software upgrade waves closely followed releases from Umbrel, a platform that packages Bitcoin node software.

The three biggest waves involved between 262 and 385 nodes each, with at least 87% of participating nodes broadcasting updated software features within 48 hours of an Umbrel release.

However, Umbrel distributed those versions between 19 and 41 days after the corresponding LND releases, introducing another potential delay between developers making fixes available and operators receiving them through their preferred platforms.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The association suggests that software distribution channels can significantly influence how quickly Bitcoin's payment infrastructure adopts security improvements. The study could not identify which platforms individual nodes used, leaving the precise role of automatic updates unresolved.

Public security warnings appeared less effective at changing operator behavior.

Across nine LND vulnerability disclosures, the researcher found no statistically detectable acceleration in nodes moving away from affected software lines. The findings do not rule out smaller responses, but they show that disclosures were not followed by clear, widespread upgrade surges.

The delays also extend beyond security incidents. Nodes operating older software when LND versions 0.19 through 0.21 became available took an estimated median of 88 days to migrate to the newer release lines.

Still, the potential exposure is unevenly distributed across Lightning's payment infrastructure.

At the August disclosure, affected release lines accounted for 14.9% of live LND nodes but only 6.4% of their channel capacity, excluding an identified managed fleet. That disparity suggests that affected nodes controlled a relatively smaller share of payment liquidity.

The researcher analyzed 814 daily snapshots of Lightning's public network graph, using advertised software features to infer release lines. The method cannot identify exact patch versions, and nodes that upgrade without broadcasting new announcements may continue to appear exposed. Actual exploitability also depends on the vulnerability and the node's configuration.

Kumar published the study's code and aggregate data alongside the preprint, allowing further scrutiny of the findings. The research leaves node software developers and platform distributors facing an unresolved operational question: how to reduce the weeks or months between releasing security fixes and getting them installed across Lightning's public network.

#5204. Polygon Extends Its Stablecoin Payments Infrastructure to Solana

Polygon Extends Its Stablecoin Payments Infrastructure to Solana

Polygon Labs is extending its stablecoin payments infrastructure to Solana, giving businesses on the rival network a single connection to pull money in from bank accounts, move crypto across chains, and pay out to customers and suppliers worldwide.

The company said the expansion brings its Open Money Stack, a bundle of payments tools, to Solana for the first time.

The pitch addresses a persistent headache in crypto payments: sending the tokens is the easy part, but getting money from a bank account into a wallet, converting it and landing it back in a recipient’s bank account typically requires stitching together several providers, each with its own integration and contract.

Open Money Stack collapses that into one technical connection and one commercial relationship, with businesses free to adopt the whole thing or mix individual pieces into their existing systems.

Through the platform, a Solana business can connect bank, card, cash, and crypto funding routes, spin up embedded wallets, route and convert funds across networks including Polygon, Ethereum and Tron, and deliver payouts into banks, cards, or wallets. A remittance provider, for instance, could take dollars from a sender, deliver USDC on Solana, and connect the recipient to a bank payout.

“Every bank and fintech that wants to use crypto faces the same problem: connecting them to the accounts and payment systems its customers already use,” said Sandeep Nailwal, co-founder and CEO of the Polygon Foundation, adding that adding Solana lets more businesses build on the network their customers choose.

Cross-border payments firm Kira is among the first to adopt Solana through the stack, using it to help clients turn stablecoin transfers into dollars in their bank accounts to pay overseas suppliers.

The move reflects Solana’s growing pull as a payments venue, having processed more than $5 trillion in stablecoin volume this year, per the Solana Foundation.

The network recently rolled out an institutional settlement standard with input from J.P. Morgan and went live with its Alpenglow upgrade. For Polygon, the expansion builds on its broader payments push, including its acquisition of crypto-cash firm Coinme.

#5201. Polygon brings US banking access to Solana’s $15 billion stablecoin market

Eligible foreign businesses can now move dollars between US banking rails and Solana stablecoins without establishing a US company.

Polygon has extended its US banking and stablecoin payment infrastructure to Solana, including access for eligible overseas businesses.

The Oct. 29 expansion of its Open Money Stack (OMS) allows qualifying foreign companies to access US banking rails in their own legal names without establishing a US entity.

The service connects traditional banking with Solana's stablecoin infrastructure, enabling businesses to accept deposits through bank transfers, cards, cash or crypto, convert funds into stablecoins and process payouts through the same channels.

Businesses can also hold funds in embedded or custodial Solana wallets and move assets across Polygon, Ethereum, TRON and other supported networks.

The integration targets fintech companies, remittance providers and payment applications seeking to offer dollar-denominated services across borders without building separate banking, custody and blockchain integrations.

Polygon Labs CEO Marc Boiron said the expansion reflects the company's ambition to support money movement across different blockchains. He said:

“Moving all money onchain means enabling money to move on the chains people want to use.”

The expansion gives Polygon a foothold in one of the largest stablecoin ecosystems outside its own network.

According to DeFiLlama data, Solana held more than $15 billion in stablecoins, making it the third-largest network behind Ethereum and TRON.

Polygon, meanwhile, ranked eighth with approximately $3 billion, highlighting the difference in the networks' existing stablecoin liquidity.

Solana's payment activity also provides a substantial market for Polygon's infrastructure services. Solana reported that the blockchain had processed more than $5 trillion in stablecoin transfer volume in 2026.

Polygon had already integrated Solana into its Trails routing infrastructure in August, enabling stablecoin and SOL transfers between Solana and more than 20 Ethereum-compatible networks.

The latest expansion adds banking access, customer accounts, stablecoin custody and fiat payouts to that existing routing capability.

Under the arrangement, a remittance provider could receive dollars from a customer, convert them into USDC on Solana, and enable the recipient to withdraw through a supported bank account or other payout channel.

However, access remains subject to regulatory requirements. Businesses must complete know-your-business verification, while customers initiating payments require separate identity checks. Geographic restrictions and supported payment corridors also determine which transactions are available.

The OMS service remains in early access, requiring businesses to apply before using the infrastructure.

#5200. Walmart Blocks Its Stock From OKX-ICE Tokenized Trading Venue

Walmart Blocks Its Stock From OKX-ICE Tokenized Trading Venue

The world’s largest retailer is now keeping its shares off OKX and ICE’s planned tokenized stock venue.

Walmart, Eli Lilly, Cigna Group, Carnival, and Arcos Dorados have objected to having their shares offered on OKXICE TSV, according to an updated notice from the venue dated Wednesday. The five additions bring the total to 38 companies.

That’s up from 33 a day earlier, when JPMorgan Chase, Coca-Cola and Dell were among seven newcomers. The tally has more than quadrupled since the venue’s first list of nine objectors on Oct. 15. It grew to 16 on Oct. 19, with names including Microsoft, and 22 on Oct. 23 when Wells Fargo joined the list.

The latest batch broadens the pushback beyond tech and finance into retail, health care, and travel. Arcos Dorados, the largest McDonald’s franchisee in Latin America, joins McDonald’s itself, which objected earlier this month. Eli Lilly and Cigna join UnitedHealth Group and Moderna among the health care companies on the list.

The objections carry real weight. Under the SEC’s innovation exemption, which the agency unveiled in September after the Clarity Act stalled in the Senate, a venue must notify a company before offering a version of its shares tokenized by an unaffiliated third party.

If the company objects in writing within 30 days, the venue can’t make that stock available for trading, according to the SEC’s order. The objection applies only to that venue, and the notices don’t disclose the companies’ reasons.

OKXICE TSV, a 50-50 joint venture between NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange and OKX’s U.S. holding company, plans to let approved users trade tokenized U.S. stocks around the clock. OKX and ICE first teamed up on tokenized securities earlier this year before announcing the 24/7 venue this month.

Trades would run through permissioned Uniswap v4 pools on OKX’s XLayer blockchain, paired with stablecoins such as USDC. The exemption caps the venue at 75 of the largest, most liquid stocks, with trading limited to 0.25% of each stock’s average daily volume, and up to 250 other stocks at 2.5%.

The list may not be finished. Under the SEC’s order, companies have 30 days from receiving notice to object, and some of the market’s most heavily traded names, including Nvidia, Tesla, Apple and Amazon, haven’t done so.

The venue has also said it can list shares tokenized by the issuers themselves, which would give companies direct control over how their stock appears onchain, though the notices don’t identify any such listings.

#5199. Solana validators told to act immediately as critical Linux vulnerability emerges

Anza and the Solana Foundation have urged operators to secure affected servers after researchers demonstrated an exploit capable of remote command execution.

Solana validators have been urged to secure their servers against a critical Linux vulnerability that Ubuntu has yet to patch.

Anza, a Solana-focused software developer, issued an urgent warning to mainnet-beta validators on Nov. 1, calling for immediate action against CVE-2026-98432, a vulnerability that could allow hackers to remotely take control of affected Linux servers.

Tim Garcia, validator relations lead at the Solana Foundation, said the underlying flaw had already been fixed in the Linux kernel but remained unpatched in Ubuntu, an operating system commonly used by Solana validators.

“Validators! CVE-2026-98432 is patched in the linux kernel but not yet patched on ubuntu.”

He advised affected operators to block incoming TCP packets containing IPv4 options, disable Multipath TCP (MPTCP), and turn off TCP request-socket migration until a security update becomes available.

The warning places Solana's validator infrastructure among systems potentially exposed to a vulnerability with a critical severity score of 9.8 out of 10.

The flaw stems from a memory-handling error during IPv4 TCP connection processing that can allow attackers to exploit a race condition without authentication or user interaction.

In research published Oct. 27, Anthropic disclosed that an unreleased Claude research model developed an exploit capable of executing commands with root privileges on Linux servers running an MPTCP listener.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The attack involved leaking kernel memory information before manipulating the system into executing malicious commands. Anthropic said its demonstration took approximately 53 minutes to achieve remote command execution.

Such access could give attackers control over an affected server, potentially compromising a validator's operations and sensitive credentials stored on the machine.

However, the demonstrated exploit involved a specific MPTCP configuration. No evidence shows Solana validators have been compromised through the flaw, and the research does not establish that every validator is vulnerable to the demonstrated attack.

The Ubuntu security tracker still listed its main Linux kernel package for Ubuntu 24.04 as “Vulnerable work in progress” as of Nov. 2, identifying version 6.8.0-149.149 as the planned fix.

That leaves affected operators dependent on temporary protections, although Red Hat warned that blocking malicious IPv4 traffic cannot completely eliminate exposure and could interfere with some workloads.

Other Linux distributions have moved ahead. Amazon Web Services confirmed that corrected kernel packages were available for Amazon Linux 2 and Amazon Linux 2023, and instructed operators to update and reboot their servers.

Anza's validator requirements specify Ubuntu 24.04 and public IPv4 connectivity, although actual exposure depends on each server's kernel version and network configuration.

The remaining uncertainty is how many Solana validators are running affected configurations. Ubuntu operators also face an outstanding remediation step: installing the corrected kernel once published and confirming that their servers are running it after a reboot.

#5198. Bitcoin catches a break from falling oil prices, but OPEC+ raises new Fed concerns

Brent's 3% decline offers relief from inflation fears, although unchanged oil production targets could complicate Bitcoin's recovery ahead of crucial US economic data.

Bitcoin's recovery faces another inflation test after OPEC+ held December oil production targets steady amid persistent Middle East supply disruptions.

Data from CryptoSlate shows that the cryptocurrency climbed around 1% to approximately $78,724 early Monday, recovering modestly from last week's market turmoil as falling oil prices and a weaker dollar offered some relief to risk assets.

Brent crude declined about 3% to $110 a barrel after trading above $114 on Friday, while the dollar index slipped 0.5% to 101.45. The moves offered Bitcoin a reprieve from two pressures that have complicated its recovery: expensive energy and restrictive US financial conditions.

However, the Nov. 1 OPEC+ decision threatens to prolong that uncertainty.

Seven producers, including Saudi Arabia and Russia, maintained September production targets through December, declining to announce additional increases despite months of disruptions to Middle Eastern exports.

The decision leaves Bitcoin exposed to renewed inflation pressure if oil shipments remain constrained, potentially reinforcing the Federal Reserve's reluctance to loosen monetary conditions.

That risk has become more pronounced after the central bank raised interest rates in September and subsequently held them at 3.75% to 4% on Oct. 28, with two policymakers voting for another increase.

Bitcoin's immediate outlook now partly hinges on whether the latest retreat in crude prices develops into sustained relief for US inflation.

The Bureau of Labor Statistics reported that gasoline prices surged 9.1% in September, accounting for more than half of the month's 0.6% increase in consumer prices. Annual inflation reached 3.7%, while core inflation stood at 2.6%.

Another energy-driven inflation surprise could strengthen expectations that borrowing costs will remain elevated, restricting the liquidity and risk-taking appetite that support cryptocurrency valuations.

An IMF study previously found that US monetary tightening depresses crypto prices through reduced investor risk-taking, a relationship that has strengthened with institutional participation.

Diplomatic negotiations involving Qatari and Iranian officials have continued, although attacks on vessels near the Strait of Hormuz threaten further supply disruptions.

The Energy Information Administration expects global oil inventories to decline by 700,000 barrels daily in the fourth quarter, although improving export routes could gradually restore supply.

Bitcoin's next macroeconomic test arrives Nov. 10, when October US inflation data and the EIA's updated oil outlook are due. Softer fuel inflation could weaken expectations of further Fed tightening, while renewed price pressure would complicate the cryptocurrency's attempt to sustain its recovery.

#5197. Hyperliquid's HYPE Jumps 13% as NYSE Owner ICE Moves to Bring Its Perpetual Futures to the US

Hyperliquid's HYPE Jumps 13% as NYSE Owner ICE Moves to Bring Its Perpetual Futures to the US

HYPE, the token behind the Hyperliquid exchange, jumped about 13% on Thursday to roughly $91 after Intercontinental Exchange, the owner of the New York Stock Exchange, announced a deal to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual futures to the United States.

The token climbed from a daily open of $80.69 to a high of $92.10 on KuCoin, leaving it about 7% below the record near $98 it set in September. Hyperliquid’s token now trades at around a $22.4 billion market capitalization.

ICE and Trade[XYZ], a company that runs markets for real-world assets on Hyperliquid, signed a definitive agreement that makes ICE the exclusive operator of Trade[XYZ]'s regulated markets, and ICE is taking a strategic stake in the company.

ICE will build a dedicated retail market and clearing structure for the product, which still needs regulatory approvals and has no launch date.

We're honored to have your trust and support. https://t.co/1bCLPHbAcy

Perpetual futures are derivatives with no expiry date that let traders take leveraged positions, meaning bets amplified with borrowed money. Trade[XYZ] already offers eligible users outside the U.S. around-the-clock perpetuals on stocks, equity indices, commodities and currencies, while Hyperliquid provides the underlying infrastructure.

Stan Druckenmiller, the macro investor who ran the Duquesne Capital hedge fund for about 30 years and said it never had a losing year, publicly backed the deal as an investor in Trade[XYZ]. “I am excited to be an investor in trade(xyz) as they partner with ICE to bring 24/7 perpetual futures on Hyperliquid to the United States,” Druckenmiller wrote on X. “As a market participant, I am looking forward to using their innovative product to enhance my investment process.

The U.S. markets are meant to comply with know-your-customer and anti-money-laundering rules, which require identity checks, and ICE will seek approvals to support leveraged trading.

Verified customers would connect through futures commission merchants, the brokers that handle customer futures accounts, and ICE plans to distribute the product through its joint venture with OKX, which has its own approvals pending. ICE bought into OKX at a $25 billion valuation in March.

ICE Chair and CEO Jeffrey Sprecher described the perpetuals as “additive to our futures markets, not a replacement for them.” ICE says more than 99% of its participation today is institutional, so retail traders are a new business, and it will run the unit separately from its institutional futures and clearing franchise.

President Donald Trump said in August that the CFTC was working to let Hyperliquid operate legally in the United States, where the exchange is currently geofenced.

#5196. Akash’s Solana plan would end current staking rewards while allowing new AKT issuance

The proposal separates the end of old-chain rewards from future provider and treasury incentives, whose emission amounts remain unresolved.

Akash’s proposed Solana migration would end the inflation-funded staking rewards paid on its current chain while its draft economic framework would allow new AKT issuance for provider incentives and the treasury.

Akash is a decentralized cloud-computing marketplace. Its AKT token helps govern and secure its sovereign Cosmos chain. The proposed migration would move the token and marketplace onto Solana in separate steps, with different consequences for balances, staking income and future supply.

Proposal 346 remained in voting when checked on Nov. 2, with a deadline of Nov. 2 at 15:02:58 UTC. A successful vote would authorize the migration and token portal. It would leave the marketplace’s cutover date for another proposal.

That distinction matters because a promise to exchange one old AKT for one SPL AKT preserves a balance at conversion. It does not, by itself, preserve the income attached to holding or staking that balance.

The proposal separates approval, token launch, marketplace cutover and the old chain’s eventual halt. Old-chain inflation and staking rewards would end at cutover. Validator roles and income would end at halt, 90 days later.

The submitted proposal expects SPL AKT and the one-way portal to launch two to three weeks after passage, once token and portal audits are complete. The marketplace would move only after its audits and migration rehearsals pass and a separate cutover proposal is approved.

The proposal now being voted on describes a portal transaction that starts unbonding, claims rewards and queues migration after 21 days. Earlier descriptions differ: the Oct. 20 technical draft uses seven days to token launch and 14 days for unbonding, while the Oct. 26 blog says two weeks to launch and unbonding for 21 days before using the portal.

Those descriptions do not establish one settled user flow. The current proposal sets the authorization being voted on; final audited instructions would still need to reconcile the older draft and the blog.

In his Oct. 26 explanation, Greg Osuri said new staking mechanisms would be announced before cutover. That establishes a commitment to explain a replacement, rather than a published replacement yield or payout formula.

Akash’s AEP-79 migration design makes a separate provision for replacement emissions: newly issued AKT directed to provider incentives and the community treasury. It describes reduced gross emissions as the intended direction, but leaves the numerical curve for modeling and governance.

Under the proposed design, a holder could lose the present inflation-funded staking income while new tokens continued to be issued for other purposes. Issuance to those accounts would expand the token base without automatically increasing a passive holder’s balance.

The unresolved economic modeling covers the gross rate, decay, split between recipients and cumulative hard-cap amount. Those determine both how much new AKT could enter supply and how its distribution would change over time.

The token-migration draft specifies a cumulative incentive-emissions cap separate from the migration budget. Its numerical amount remains unresolved; once set from the ratified model, the cap could not be raised by governance. The schedule would remain adjustable through the DAO and a timelock within that limit, with a guardian able to pause emissions.
