Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The draft also allows a paused start. If the model has not been ratified before the cutover supply snapshot, the token-issuance program would start with zero scheduled emissions and wait for governance activation. That is a contingency in the design, rather than a decision to end issuance permanently.

These are proposed controls over a future program. They supply no final numerical ceiling, emission rate or date on which replacement incentives would begin.

Compute payments could also affect how much AKT remains available to trade. Their supply implications depend on the accounting model, and the roadmap and proposed Solana design describe different limits.

Akash’s AEP-76 roadmap describes ACT as a nontransferable, dollar-denominated compute credit. Its proposed burn-mint equilibrium (BME) model links funding those credits to AKT removed from liquid circulation.

The roadmap’s detailed vault model uses previously removed AKT and remint credits first. If those are insufficient to meet a payout, it describes minting a shortfall. That describes a possible issuance path within the roadmap’s model; compute usage alone is not enough to establish a permanent net burn.

The proposed Solana architecture specifies a different limit: AKT supplied for ACT stays in a vault as collateral, and ACT redemption is paid from that vault. It expressly forbids the BME program from minting AKT.

The document also leaves current-chain vault payouts versus fresh minting as an implementation question. The roadmap’s shortfall-minting description consequently does not establish a fresh-minting path for the proposed Solana system.

The supply effects need to be separated: tokens represented through migration, new incentive emissions, and AKT moving into and out of compute-credit collateral. A vault balance temporarily removed from trading is not the same thing as tokens permanently destroyed.

The proposed portal would remain open for two years, with migration irreversible. Its 1:1 accounting answers how eligible token balances would be represented on Solana. The future emissions curve and replacement staking terms answer different questions about holders’ income and exposure to issuance.

The proposal says the emissions curve must be published before the later cutover proposal. Alongside the promised staking details and resolved compute-credit accounting, it would give holders the information needed to judge the post-migration model.

#5194. Crypto Biz: Bitcoin’s quantum threat is becoming an economic problem

Bitcoin faces mounting quantum security concerns as Ripple secures a US banking charter, USDC gains ground in Europe and VanEck adds BTC to a new ETF.

The debate over quantum computing has raised serious questions about Bitcoin’s long-term security, but the economic consequences could emerge long before the technology seriously threatens the network.

Muneeb Ali, founder of Bitcoin layer-2 project Stacks, believes quantum computing risks are already discouraging institutional investment, even as US spot Bitcoin ETFs have amassed more than $104 billion in net assets, according to BitBo data. He warns that BTC has only a few years to prepare for the transition to quantum-resistant security, a process that could take years given the network’s history of slow and contentious upgrades.

For institutional investors drawn to Bitcoin as a hedge against currency debasement, doubts about its long-term security could complicate the investment case.

This week’s Crypto Biz explores the quantum threat, alongside new developments surrounding Ripple, VanEck and European stablecoin adoption.

Bitcoin could face a more serious security challenge if developers fail to make meaningful progress toward quantum resistance by 2028, according to Stacks founder Muneeb Ali, who warned that the network’s lengthy upgrade process leaves little room for delays.

Speaking on Cointelegraph’s Chain Reaction podcast, Ali said Bitcoin needs a credible roadmap for becoming quantum-resistant, with meaningful progress required by 2028. He cited 2030 as the target for completing the transition to quantum-resistant security, based on guidance from the National Institute of Standards and Technology, though advances in artificial intelligence could bring that deadline forward.

Even if developers agree on how to protect Bitcoin against quantum attacks, implementing the changes could take years. Holders would then need time to move their Bitcoin to quantum-resistant addresses.

Although some industry participants dispute the urgency of the threat, Ali argued that uncertainty alone could undermine investor confidence. He also warned that a quantum breakthrough capable of compromising Bitcoin would have consequences for the broader cryptocurrency market.

Ripple has received final approval from the US Office of the Comptroller of the Currency to establish a national trust bank, clearing the last regulatory hurdle after securing conditional approval in December 2025.

The newly chartered Ripple National Trust Bank will operate under federal supervision, manage reserves backing the company’s RLUSD stablecoin, serve as a collateral trustee for tokenholders and provide digital asset custody. RLUSD will continue to be issued by Standard Custody & Trust Company, which is regulated by the New York Department of Financial Services.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse welcomed the approval, declaring that “crypto and banks work better together.”

The decision follows similar final approval for Circle in July, while Coinbase, Crypto.com and Stripe-owned Bridge remain at the conditional approval stage. Unlike traditional commercial banks, national trust banks generally cannot accept deposits or issue loans and do not carry Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation coverage.

Circle’s USDC has surpassed Tether’s USDt in stablecoin trading against the euro, as European Union regulations reshape competition between the world’s two largest dollar-backed stablecoins.

According to a new report from Kaiko, USDC now accounts for 56% of euro-denominated stablecoin trading, up from just 10% in January 2024. The shift accelerated after major cryptocurrency exchanges restricted USDt trading for European Economic Area customers to comply with the EU’s Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA).

USDC’s euro order books are now roughly 40% deeper than USDt’s, suggesting stronger liquidity for traders. However, USDT remains the dominant stablecoin globally, with a circulating supply approximately 2.5 times larger than USDC’s.

The findings come after the European Banking Authority designated USDC as the bloc’s first “significant e-money token,” subjecting it to additional regulatory oversight. Kaiko suggested that the US GENIUS Act could produce similar changes in stablecoin trading, depending on how exchanges implement the new requirements.

VanEck, the $244 billion asset manager, has launched an actively managed exchange-traded fund that combines traditional stocks and bonds with real assets and a small allocation to Bitcoin.

The VanEck Wealth Builder ETF (NYSE: WBMO), announced Tuesday, invests in a mix of VanEck and third-party ETFs and targets a 2% Bitcoin allocation, which can range from 1% to 5%. Rather than holding Bitcoin directly, the fund gains exposure through a spot Bitcoin exchange-traded product. The launch expands VanEck’s existing Wealth Builder model portfolios into a single ETF.

David Schassler, VanEck’s head of multi-asset solutions, said the traditional 60/40 stock-and-bond portfolio was designed for a different investment environment. He contended that adding real assets could help investors navigate a broader range of economic conditions, with Bitcoin now forming a small part of that strategy.

#5192. Blockchain Firms Link North Korea to $6.3 Billion Binance Hack

Chainalysis and TRM Labs have linked yesterday’s $6.3 billion Binance hack to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group.

Blockchain analysis firms Chainalysis and TRM Labs have linked the $6.3 billion Binance hack yesterday to TraderTraitor, a unit associated with North Korea. If confirmed at this scale, the theft would be the largest crypto hack on record.

Chainalysis puts the total at $6.4 billion in its own assessment. Chainalysis says funds began leaving Binance hot wallets across multiple blockchains around 4:20 p.m. Eastern Time on October 29.

The firm said the attacker used an operational wallet funded through Tornado Cash to submit signed authorizations that allowed a Binance hot wallet to run attacker-deployed code.

The attacker then swept assets into addresses under its control. Binance has said its initial investigation points to a compromised engineer’s laptop, which gave an attacker access to a service container and enabled unauthorized withdrawals.

The exchange said it has found no evidence of a wallet private key leak.

The attacker reportedly tried to outpace freezes by converting stablecoins into ether and other assets that issuers cannot freeze. Chainalysis said the attacker swapped $325 million in stablecoins into $285 million in ETH over roughly 20 minutes, then sent the remaining $175 million to stopover wallets. Freezes began by 5:14 p.m. Eastern, according to the firm.

Bitcoin dropped toward $76,000 on Friday morning before trading near $77,400. Other crypto followed suit and posted declines of their own. Total market liquidations reached $1.5 billion, with bitcoin accounting for $468 million and ether for $358 million.

Long positions, which bet on higher prices, bore most of the losses. The sell-off coincided with a separate incident at decentralized betting platform Polymarket, which suspended international trading after hackers drained more than $2.6 million from user accounts.

Binance said Thursday that the security incident affected about $6.3 billion in assets, surpassing the $1.5 billion Bybit hack of 2025, which was attributed to North Korean hackers. Former CEO Changpeng Zhao wrote on X, “It does look like a hack.” Zhao no longer runs the exchange but remains a key shareholder.

Stablecoin issuers moved to contain the fallout. Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said the company froze more than $1.567 billion in USDT and that protecting the industry and its partners is its main priority.

Circle co-founder and CEO Jeremy Allaire said Circle froze more than $800 million in USDC in coordination with law enforcement. First Digital CEO Vincent Chok said FDUSD stolen in the attack has been frozen. Binance says about $3 billion has been frozen onchain in total.

Binance says it has reserved more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover the incident. About $5 billion of that reserve is held in the same token assets that were affected, and the company is buying the remainder through market and OTC channels.

The exchange says frozen and protected assets remain subject to verification and plans to publish an updated Proof of Reserves.

Reuters, citing a person familiar with the matter, reported that Binance is working with the FBI and the U.S. Secret Service.

Standard Chartered’s global head of digital assets research, Geoffrey Kendrick, wrote in an email to CoinDesk that the negative price action may have some way to go. He said a new base will form after assets are unfrozen and more details of the hack emerge.

Kendrick argued that the incident could accelerate a shift toward decentralized exchanges. Chainalysis has warned that scammers are impersonating the firm, including those offering fund recovery and recruiting services.

Victims should treat unsolicited recovery offers with caution and rely on official channels for updates.

At the time of writing, Bitcoin is trading near $76,700.

#5191. Ethereum added 361,000 ETH to active stake while its total staking balance shrank

CryptoKeyhole’s Oct. 1–30 data show active ETH stake growing while withdrawals exceeded deposits by about 111,000 ETH.

Ethereum staking grew on one measure during Oct. 1–30 even as more ETH was paid out through exits and requested withdrawals than arrived in deposits. Active stake counts validators securing the network; deposits and payments record movements across the staking boundary.

CryptoKeyhole, an analytics provider that runs Ethereum nodes, reports 1,400,947 ETH deposited from Oct. 1 through Oct. 30 and 1,511,710 ETH paid out in its principal-withdrawn category. That leaves a net outflow of 110,763 ETH. Across the same closed-day window, active validators' effective stake rose from 35.82% to 36.09% of ETH supply.

The distinction matters for investors reading a higher staked share as evidence of fresh demand or tightening liquid supply. More ETH can count toward securing Ethereum while the wider pool of ETH held on its consensus layer gets smaller.

The comparison uses CryptoKeyhole's daily staking-share series and supply data for the 30 complete UTC days from Oct. 1 through Oct. 30, measured from Sept. 30 close to Oct. 30 close.

Active stake is the effective balance of validators participating in consensus: the amount that counts toward their network weight. The broader consensus-layer measure also includes queued deposits, validators waiting to activate or be paid after exiting, and balances above the effective amount.

Multiplying the provider's published daily shares by its corresponding supply figures gives the following approximate balances:

These approximate balances are calculated from CryptoKeyhole's series and rounded to whole ETH. They inherit its supply methodology, which can include modeled rewards for days whose closing state has not been read. The final row combines several categories; it does not identify each one's contribution.

About 361,000 ETH more counted as active effective stake while total consensus holdings fell by about 80,000 ETH. The combined balance outside the active effective tally contracted enough to accommodate both changes.

A shrinking total ETH supply does not explain the higher active share in this dataset. CryptoKeyhole's supply series increased by about 86,137 ETH across those closes. Its broader consensus-layer share fell from 37.336% to 37.245%, even as active share increased.

The flow measure counts deposit requests in each day's blocks, including deposits for new validators and top-ups to existing ones. A request does not immediately become active effective stake. Ethereum's pending-deposit processing and activation rules impose separate steps before that capital can contribute to consensus.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Previously deposited ETH can become active during October without another deposit or contemporaneous spot purchase. Conversely, an October deposit can remain queued beyond the month.

Withdrawals have their own timing difference. A validator stops contributing active stake before its remaining balance is eventually paid to its withdrawal address. A payment completed in October can thus reflect an exit that affected active stake earlier.

Rewards can also increase active stake without a deposit. Ethereum's compounding validators can retain rewards and raise effective balances up to 2,048 ETH, while legacy validators have a 32 ETH effective cap and automatically sweep excess rewards. Whole-ETH balance adjustments and a buffer further separate rewards earned from effective stake added.

CryptoKeyhole excludes automatic excess-balance reward sweeps from its principal-withdrawn category. It includes full balances paid to exited validators and partial withdrawals requested from the execution layer. Those payments can contain accumulated rewards, making “principal” a category label rather than a calculation of original deposits alone.

Consolidations also require separate accounting. They move existing stake into fewer validators instead of constituting new external deposits. Intermediate changes in active balances and any residual later withdrawal can affect the relevant tallies without representing fresh buying.

Activation and payment timing therefore help explain how the measures can diverge. The aggregate balance changes do not allocate the 360,945 ETH increase among activation, reward compounding, consolidation effects and other effective-balance adjustments.

The 110,763 ETH flow deficit also measures different components from the change in the full consensus ledger. The latter includes rewards net of penalties and automatic payouts, so these aggregates alone do not fully reconcile the two.

The entry backlog provides another stock of ETH that must be separated from new deposits. CryptoKeyhole's closing queue fell from 1,578,359 ETH on Sept. 30 to 1,226,570 ETH on Oct. 30, a decline of 351,789 ETH.

That decline is not a direct measure of activations. New requests, processing and internal queue movements can change the balance along the way. Equally, the exit queue's fall from 604,304 ETH to zero does not mean that amount was paid out during the month.

At the finalized epoch timestamped Nov. 2 at 09:52 UTC, CryptoKeyhole reported 1,104,862 ETH awaiting entry and estimated 19.7 days for a new deposit to be credited. This later snapshot provides context for the backlog; the October comparison uses the closed-day balances above.

A zero exit queue also leaves later payout steps intact. CryptoKeyhole reported a 27.3-hour withdrawability delay after exiting, followed by payment on a withdrawal sweep. Its last complete sweep cycle took 7.1 days; that historical duration is not a guaranteed waiting time for every validator.

CryptoSlate's October coverage of staking delays distinguished the wait to earn from the stages required to get ETH back. The month-end figures add a separate lesson: a queue, an active balance and a completed payment describe different points in the same process.

At the other end, Ethereum's withdrawal rules credit the specified execution-layer address. That payment alone establishes neither an exchange deposit nor a sale.

#5190. 4M Bitcoin Bought Above $85K Could Cap Recovery: Glassnode

4M Bitcoin Bought Above $85K Could Cap Recovery: Glassnode

Bitcoin's long-term holders are sitting on losses on about 4 million BTC bought between $85,000 and $118,000, and many may sell if the price climbs back toward that level, according to a note from on-chain analytics firm Glassnode.

Glassnode, which defines long-term holders as those holding coins for more than 155 days, said most of that block was bought in 2025. More than half of all long-term holder coins were bought above the current price, according to the note, published Wednesday ahead of the Federal Reserve's rate decision.

$85K keeps turning $BTC back. Since January, Bitcoin has closed above it on only five days, never more than three in a row. It's a truly tough level to break. More in depth analysis in our latest Week on Chain report https://t.co/xy8lE3ilt3

— glassnode (@glassnode) October 28, 2026

That block is worth about $326 billion at Bitcoin's current price of around $81,400, up 1.2% over the past 24 hours but about 35% below its all-time high of $126,080.

Glassnode said a band between $84,000 and $85,000 has turned Bitcoin back four times this year, and that the asset has closed above it on only five days since January. Bitcoin last broke above it in September, peaking at $87,100.

ETF holders are near break-even too. Glassnode puts the average price U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs paid for their coins at $81,500. Bloomberg Intelligence ETF analyst James Seyffart estimated the figure at just under $82,000 in a tweet on Wednesday, adding that Bitcoin has hovered around that level since the end of August.

Seyffart's chart put ETF holders' average unrealized loss at about $2.63 billion as of Monday. In replies, he said the estimate tracks only fund flows, not what secondary-market buyers paid.

The Fed held its benchmark rate at 3.75% to 4.00% on Wednesday in a 10-2 vote, with Governor Lisa Cook and Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack dissenting in favor of a hike. Traders had put the odds of another quarter-point hike in December above 70% before the decision.

Tim Sun, senior researcher at HashKey, argued that a December hike had been priced in to a certain extent. "What truly constrains Bitcoin's performance," he told Decrypt, "is the persistently high U.S. Treasury yields and real interest rates, which are significantly raising the opportunity cost for investors holding risk assets."

The 10-year Treasury yield stood just under 5% at midday on Thursday, according to CNBC, down from 5.05% at Wednesday's close and a 52-week high of 5.37% on October 7.

Sun added that lower funding rates on Bitcoin perpetual futures have squeezed returns on cash-and-carry arbitrage, which pairs spot Bitcoin with short futures. Galaxy Research found that nine hedge funds and banks cut 68,000 BTC worth of spot ETF exposure as that trade dried up, while wealth platforms and sovereign funds added.

ETF flows have picked up this week. U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs took in about $424 million from Monday to Wednesday, per SoSoValue data, after $635 million left the funds on October 22.

"Any rally above $85,000 could face selling pressure from long-term holders seeking to break even," Sun said, echoing Glassnode.

Both pointed to light selling, however. Glassnode said holders have realized smaller losses with each dip since August: at the August low, realized losses outweighed profits by about $630 million in a day, while around a dip on October 24 the largest daily net loss was about $27 million.

"The core issue facing the market right now is not panic selling, but rather insufficient new buying interest to support a sustained recovery," Sun said.

On the downside, Glassnode pointed to about $42 million in bids between $78,000 and $79,000 on Binance, and the average cost basis of newer buyers at $75,800.

Sun expects Bitcoin to keep trading between $75,000 and $85,000 in the short term until macro liquidity improves and ETFs resume continuous net inflows. Whether selling pressure near $85,000 can be absorbed "will serve as an important signal to determine whether the market can regain strength," he said.

#5187. SEC Charges Two Companies in $130 Million Crypto Trading Scheme

The SEC has charged two companies with defrauding investors of over $130 million through a crypto Ponzi-like scheme.

The Securities and Exchange Commission charged two foreign-operated, Colorado-incorporated companies with running a crypto investment fraud that used false federal filings to raise more than $130 million from tens of thousands of retail investors.

The defendants are BG Wealth Sharing Ltd. and DSJ Exchange Pty Ltd. A complaint filed in the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California alleges that from at least October 2025 through June 2026, the companies induced investors in more than 40 states and several U.S. territories to join a “wealth-sharing program.”

Participants were promised risk-free returns generated by so-called signals for crypto asset trading.

According to the complaint, BG Wealth claimed to be the world’s largest hedge fund, claimed registration with the SEC as an investment adviser, and said it partnered with a licensed and secure crypto trading platform, DSJ Exchange.

The SEC says none of those claims were true. The complaint alleges the companies submitted false Form ADV and Form D filings to the SEC. Those filings were viewable on the agency’s website and shared with investors as evidence that the SEC had licensed or approved the firms.

Laura D’Allaird, chief of the SEC Enforcement Division’s Cyber and Emerging Technologies Unit, described the operation in a statement.

“This alleged scheme was well-planned,” she said, citing social media, encrypted messaging channels, weekly live videoconferences and a mobile app used to engage investors. The complaint alleges the defendants displayed fake certificates bearing the SEC’s seal and made periodic payments to some investors, “a Ponzi-like tactic,” while misappropriating the remaining funds.

The SEC charged both companies with violating the antifraud provisions of the Securities Act of 1933 and the Securities Exchange Act of 1934.

It charged BG Wealth with violating Sections 204(a) and 207 of the Investment Advisers Act of 1940. The agency seeks permanent injunctions, disgorgement with prejudgment interest, and civil penalties against both companies, along with conduct-based injunctions against BG Wealth.

The Form ADV and Form D filings have been removed from the SEC website.

The SEC’s Office of Investor Education and Assistance has issued investor alerts warning that scammers are using exempt reporting adviser filings to create a false impression of legitimacy. The agency encourages investors to check the background of anyone offering or selling a crypto investment through Investor.gov.

The SEC said it received assistance from the Alberta Securities Commission, the Hawaii Department of Commerce and Consumer Affairs, the Texas State Securities Board, the Utah Division of Securities, the Washington State Department of Financial Institutions, the Wisconsin Department of Financial Institutions, and other authorities.

#5185. $11 Billion in Bitcoin Options Expire Tomorrow—Here's What It Means

$11 Billion in Bitcoin Options Expire Tomorrow—Here's What It Means

About $11 billion in Bitcoin options expire on Friday on Deribit, a crypto options exchange, with Bitcoin trading near $81,500 a day after the Federal Reserve held interest rates steady while two of its officials voted to raise them.

Even people who never touch options have a stake, because the traders who sold these contracts must buy or sell real Bitcoin to hedge as the price moves, which can add to swings without any news behind them.

An option is a contract that gives its holder the right, but not the obligation, to buy (a call) or sell (a put) Bitcoin at a fixed price, called the strike, by a set date. Friday’s batch covers about 135,000 Bitcoin in contracts, roughly 88,700 calls and 46,500 puts, per Deribit’s data.

That equals about a third of all Bitcoin options open on the exchange and roughly 70% more than the $6.4 billion that expired in August. The put-to-call ratio, which compares bets on a drop with bets on a rally, is 0.52, so calls outnumber puts nearly two to one, which means there’s optimism in terms of price going up.

The $11 billion is notional value, the face value of the contracts at today’s price, not money changing hands. About 86% of the contracts are out of the money, meaning they would be worthless at current prices, and settling at today’s level would move roughly $130 million, about 1% of the headline figure.

Traders watch the Bitcoin options expiry day as one of the key events for their fundamental analysis. Another catalyst to consider is the Federal Reserve’s policy on rates. The Fed yesterday held firm at 3.75% to 4.00%, with expectations rising for another hike this year. Interest rates impact the whole economy as investment in risky assets and businesses would be less attractive than lending giving your money to the government through Treasuries.

Traders also watch max pain, the price at which the most contracts expire worthless and sellers pay out the least. Deribit’s open interest puts it at $78,000, about $3,500 below the market, though it describes positioning and does not predict where Bitcoin will settle. After all, this is crypto, and wild movements are not a strange occurrence.

The heaviest call bets sit at $95,000 and $100,000, about 16% and 23% above the current price, while the biggest put cluster is at $70,000, about 14% below. Friday’s expiry also saw the most trading of any Bitcoin options expiry on Deribit over the past 24 hours, with more than 6,000 contracts changing hands

Friday’s batch is smaller than the $15 billion expiry in March, but about three times the $3.6 billion expiry from April 2021.

The next test is December 25, when about 135,500 Bitcoin in options are already set to expire, roughly the same size as Friday’s and about two weeks after the Fed meets December 8-9.

Coinbase missed revenue expectations as users declined, despite growth in recurring revenue, while executive share-sale plans and a massive Binance security breach added pressure to crypto markets.

Coinbase had a mixed third quarter. Revenue came in at $1.20 billion, below the $1.233 billion analysts expected, according to company filings. Subscription and services revenue did better, reaching $570 million. The company is still heavily tied to trading activity, but its recurring income is growing.

Monthly Transacting Users fell to 7.4 million, down from 9.3 million a year earlier. During its earnings call, Coinbase blamed overall market conditions. Its share of crypto trading volume did rise to 10.7%, the fourth straight quarter of gains, which suggests it is winning business even as the broader market quiets.

JUST IN: Coinbase disclosed buying 643 Bitcoin worth $1.5 billion for its balance sheet in Q3 🚀 pic.twitter.com/5BYdrwLCJN

Separately, filings reported on October 30 show CEO Brian Armstrong has set up a plan to sell up to 8.17 million Class A shares. He adopted the plan in August under SEC Rule 10b5-1, which lets executives schedule sales ahead of time. Sales could run from November 16 through October 2027, subject to stock price conditions. Co-founder Fred Ehrsam and President Emilie Choi have similar plans, bringing the total to about 13.6 million shares.

This is not new territory for Armstrong. In the past, VanEck analyst Matthew Sigel tracked about $545.7 million in sales over nine months, starting in April 2025. Armstrong has said these sales are part of his risk management, not a signal about the company.

Coinbase shares (COIN) fell about 15% today, but not necessarily due to this earnings report.

Binance disclosed a security breach on October 29 that it estimates at $6.3 billion, making it the largest crypto theft on record. Around $3 billion in assets has been frozen so far, with industry partners assisting in the freezes.

The hackers are allegedly linked to North Korea, though Binance has not attributed the attack to any group. The exchange says it has found no evidence of a leaked wallet private key.

Binance says it has $6.34 billion in reserves available to cover losses. Deposits were unaffected, and the company expects to resume withdrawals within 48 hours. Figures remain preliminary, and estimates vary slightly across outlets.

The market reacted quickly. Bitcoin fell roughly 4.5% to below $77,000. Shares of Strategy (MSTR) fell nearly 10% on the day. Strive (ASST) shares fell over 12% at times. Coinbase’s stock is down over 55% on the year.

#5175. Crypto Spends $176 Million on Midterms, But Only 13% of Voters See It as a Priority

Crypto Spends $176 Million on Midterms, But Only 13% of Voters See It as a Priority

The crypto industry is spending heavily on next week’s midterm elections. Voters aren’t putting it near the top of their lists.

Only 13% of Americans say it’s extremely or very important that a political candidate supports the growth of emerging financial technologies like blockchain, crypto and decentralized finance, according to a survey conducted by Ipsos for the DeFi Education Fund, a crypto policy advocacy group.

A broader pitch fared better. About 35% say it’s very important that candidates ensure those building the next generation of financial technology are encouraged to do so in the U.S., the top-ranked policy position in the survey. Just 26% said the same about supporting new rules that allow innovation in the financial system.

The DeFi Education Fund describes the results as “bipartisan support for U.S. innovation,” and the narrower crypto question shows little partisan gap: 15% of Republicans and 13% of Democrats rate it very important. But on the top-ranked item, Republicans led Democrats 45% to 30%.

The survey polled 1,545 adults from Sept. 8 to 16 on Ipsos’ KnowledgePanel, the probability-based panel the Federal Reserve uses for its household finance survey, with a margin of error of plus or minus 2.8 percentage points. Party identification includes independents who lean toward a party.

The industry’s spending tells a different story. Crypto groups have spent about $176 million across more than 100 midterm races this cycle, according to a Reuters review, with super PAC Fairshake and its affiliates accounting for about $150.8 million through Oct. 20.

One Fairshake affiliate has disclosed more than $46 million in spending against former Sen. Sherrod Brown in Ohio’s Senate race.

The stakes are high for the industry after the Clarity Act, which would have set federal rules for crypto markets, failed a key Senate vote in September amid disputes over ethics provisions covering President Donald Trump’s crypto interests. Since then, the industry has turned to regulators like the SEC and CFTC to write the rules instead.

The survey suggests voters care more about security and control than about crypto itself. Some 63% say the financial system needs an upgrade to combat threats like AI and cybercrime, and 49% say the government shouldn’t decide which products they can invest in.

#5169. Tether’s excess reserves nearly double to $8B as Q3 profit hits $1.5B

The stablecoin issuer’s US Treasury holdings are comparable to those of major foreign holders, while its Bitcoin reserves were valued at $8.37 billion at the end of September.

Tether reported $1.5 billion in third-quarter operating profit as its excess reserves nearly doubled to $7.95 billion, according to figures released alongside its latest attestation prepared by independent accounting firm BDO.

The company held $191.5 billion in assets against $183.5 billion in liabilities at the end of September, leaving a reserve surplus of $7.95 billion, nearly double the $4.1 billion reported in June. Its assets included $132.4 billion in direct and indirect US Treasury bill holdings.

That exposure is comparable to South Korea’s $134.2 billion in US government debt and exceeds the United Arab Emirates’ $116.1 billion, according to August data from the US Treasury Department.

Tether also generated $4.5 billion in net operating profit during the first nine months of 2026, with income largely driven by US Treasury bills and reverse repurchase agreements, according to the company. Its Bitcoin reserves were valued at $8.37 billion, up from approximately $5.8 billion at the end of June.

Tether’s USDT remains the world’s largest stablecoin, accounting for around 60% of the $297.3 billion stablecoin market, according to DefiLlama data. On Friday, Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said in a post on X that USDT’s user base had surpassed 700 million, with adoption concentrated in emerging markets.

The stablecoin issuer has faced years of scrutiny over its reserve transparency. In August, the company announced the completion of its first full independent financial statement audit by KPMG US, although the audited statements were not made public.

#5168. Hackers Publish Work Details of 41,000 Homeland Security Employees

Hackers Publish Work Details of 41,000 Homeland Security Employees

Names, job titles, office locations, and phone numbers for more than 41,000 Department of Homeland Security employees and contractors were published online Wednesday by a transparency nonprofit.

The nonprofit says a hacktivist group, meaning hackers driven by politics rather than profit, gave it the records.

The department oversees border protection, immigration enforcement, and cybersecurity, so the file works like a directory of who does what, who reports to whom and how to reach them.

Transparency groups say the contact details are useful for public-records requests. Security researchers warn the same list could help anyone who wants to impersonate a supervisor over the phone or work out who sits behind a sensitive investigation.

The nonprofit, Distributed Denial of Secrets, publishes leaked documents and calls this dataset Icepick. It says the hackers, who call themselves the Department of Peace, took the records from an Okta single sign-on service used by DHS, a login system that lets employees open many internal tools with one account.

Okta, the company behind that login service, disputes the claim. “Okta is secure, and we have no evidence our data or systems were compromised,” the company told Reuters.

The group says it acted after Homeland Security Secretary Markwayne Mullin pledged stepped-up operations by Immigration and Customs Enforcement, known as ICE. “On July 17th, Markwayne Mullin said he was ‘turning up the heat.’ This is what happens to ICE when you do that,” a spokesperson for the group said. “They have our information, so it’s only fair that we have theirs,” the hackers wrote.

The searchable archive indexes 41,191 people, including 21,329 from Customs and Border Protection and about 10,000 from ICE, a third of them in its investigative arm, Homeland Security Investigations.

It also lists more than 55,000 internal permission groups, which are labels that control who can open which systems, along with each person’s name, title, office, email, phone number and manager. DDoSecrets co-founder Emma Best says the dataset contains no home addresses.

“DHS is aware of a potential breach and is actively working to address the situation swiftly and effectively,” a department spokesperson said.

DHS has also filed a takedown notice through the security firm ZeroFox, asking the domain registrar to stop serving contact and location data of people who did not consent to its publication. The search site built on the data stopped loading Thursday, with its domain showing a registrar hold, and Bluesky is blocking links to the nonprofit hosting the archive.

In September, the cybercrime group ShinyHunters claimed to hold 2 to 3 terabytes of data on FBI agents and job applicants, and the bureau told staff to assume hackers have their personal data.

The newest records in the archive date to August 19, and some entries, including CISA staff who have since left the agency, are already out of date.

#5163. Polymarket Pauses Trading as Prediction Market Investigates Security Incident

Polymarket Pauses Trading as Prediction Market Investigates Security Incident

Polymarket users are reporting that the prediction market’s frontend may be compromised, with assets being withdrawn from accounts without permission.

The platform’s status page shows its trading API in a major outage and the exchange in cancel-only mode, meaning traders can pull existing orders but not place new ones. Relayer actions, which submit users’ signed transactions to the blockchain, are also paused.

Polymarket later confirmed that one its third-party providers was compromised. "We have referred this to relevant law enforcement," the company said on X. "Anyone who has been impacted will be made whole."

We've identified & fixed an issue impacting our international platform, Polymarket⁠.com. One of our 3rd party providers was compromised, and we have referred this to relevant law enforcement.

Anyone who has been impacted will be made whole.

— Polymarket Traders (@PolymarketTrade) October 29, 2026

Arkham data shows that a wallet identified as the suspected attacker’s, 0x3F99139F1C5854bb628c2e576Aaf638e99dFfc18, holds about $2.65 million in POL, Polygon’s native token. It received multiple recent swaps from USDC.e, a bridged version of the USDC stablecoin, into POL through Relay, a cross-chain swapping service.

The first transactions began about an hour ago, and the wallet has shown no activity in the last five minutes.

Researchers at Hudson Rock said a script loaded from DebugBear, a web performance monitoring service, contains code that looks for a logged-in user’s trading session and attempts to sell their positions and transfer the assets.

The firm said the same wallet address is hard-coded in the script as its fallback recipient, and that Polymarket’s production site code records the script’s removal. Hudson Rock said it has not established how the compromise started or how many users were affected.

Polymarket has not yet said what caused the incident. In May, a private key compromise drained roughly $700,000 from an internal top-up wallet. A month later, a compromised third-party vendor let hackers inject malicious code into the frontend and steal about $3 million from fewer than 15 accounts, and Polymarket promised to refund users in full.

#5159. Chrome Can Now Pull Details From Your Gmail to Fill Out Web Forms

Chrome Can Now Pull Details From Your Gmail to Fill Out Web Forms

Google’s Chrome browser can now pull personal details from your Gmail inbox to fill out online forms for you, the company said in its announcement on Wednesday.

The update, called “enhanced autofill,” suggests order numbers, tracking information, and flight bookings when a form asks for them, wrote Amanda Lim, a Chrome product manager, in the announcement. Autofill is the browser tool that types in details like your name and address so you don’t have to.

It runs on Gemini, Google’s AI assistant, through a feature called Personal Intelligence, which lets Gemini draw on connected apps such as Gmail and Google Photos to tailor its answers to your own life. It is rolling out to Gemini in Chrome users who have Personal Intelligence turned on, on desktop, Android and iOS, starting in the U.S.

Google’s post names only those three kinds of data as coming from Gmail. Credit card numbers and addresses are part of Chrome’s standard autofill, which fills them from details you have saved in the browser, and the announcement does not say the new feature pulls them out of your inbox.

“You can manage whether enhanced autofill is enabled and what it can access via your Chrome settings,” Lim wrote. “As always, sensitive data is encrypted and you remain in control.”

Encryption scrambles data so only Google and other authorized parties can read it, though the post does not explain how that applies to the Gmail content Chrome reads or what it scans.

Users choose whether to connect their apps and can switch the connections off at any time, the post says, though Google does train on limited information, such as specific prompts and the model’s responses.

In November, users pushed back on a Smart Features setting that let Gemini analyze emails and calendars unless they opted out, and Google said it had not changed its underlying data-handling practices.

Chrome also dropped a line from its settings saying on-device AI, meaning models that run on your own computer rather than Google’s servers, works without sending data to Google servers. Google said the wording change did not reflect a change in how it handles on-device AI.

The rollout began Wednesday in the U.S. on desktop, Android and iOS, and Google’s announcement gives no date for other countries

#5158. HYPE surges 15% as NYSE parent ICE moves to bring Hyperliquid perps to US

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

ICE will run the exchange and clearing, TradeXYZ will design the markets, and Hyperliquid's HIP-3 will supply the tech, pending regulatory approval.

Intercontinental Exchange (ICE), the parent company of the New York Stock Exchange, has agreed to partner with TradeXYZ to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual futures markets to the U.S. under a regulated framework, subject to regulatory approvals.

ICE plans to operate the markets and establish dedicated exchange and clearing infrastructure for perpetual contracts tied to cryptocurrencies, equities, indices, commodities, and forex.

ICE is also making a strategic investment in TradeXYZ, with the new markets expected to be accessible through participating brokers and platforms. CEO Jeffrey Sprecher previously framed ICE's interest around creating a level regulatory playing field for onchain perpetuals.

"We've seen a clear increase in retail participation in markets, and that participation is becoming far more global, with retail traders increasingly seeking short-duration exposure, often around the clock," Sprecher said Thursday. "We intend to find retail traders where they want to be, through the partners, brokers, and platforms they already use. We see these perp markets as additive to our futures markets, not a replacement for them."

The HYPE token surged 15% on Thursday, trading around $91.21 at publication time. The token's all-time high hit $97.96 last month.

Under the structure, Hyperliquid will serve as the underlying technology infrastructure via HIP-3, TradeXYZ designs and implements the markets, and ICE operates the regulated market and clearing structure.

Billionaire investor Stanley Druckenmiller reportedly posted to X for the first time ever in support of the news.

"I am excited to be an investor in trade(xyz) as they partner with ICE to bring 24/7 perpetual futures on Hyperliquid to the United States," he wrote in a post. "As a market participant, I am looking forward to to using their innovative product to enhance my investment process."

The deal builds on TradeXYZ's rapid growth on Hyperliquid, where its builder-deployed perp markets have driven a growing share of activity in contracts tied to stocks, indices and commodities. Hyperliquid's HIP-3 markets have attracted demand for around-the-clock trading of traditional assets, with open interest reaching $2.38 billion in April.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission in May issued a staff advisory and no-action relief, clearing the way for perps to come to the U.S. Then in September, President Donald Trump namedropped Hyperliquid, mentioning the CFTC's effort to bring the protocol into the U.S. in a "fully compliant and legal fashion."

On Thursday, Arkham data showed that a wallet address identified as belonging to a suspected attacker holds about $2.65 million in POL after revealing multiple recent USDC.e-to-POL swaps through Relay. Since then, they have moved funds to different addresses.

#5152. Coinbase Swings to $719 Million Q3 Profit on Crypto Gains

Coinbase Swings to $719 Million Q3 Profit on Crypto Gains

Coinbase is back in the black, though much of that came from the crypto on its own balance sheet.

The exchange reported third-quarter net income of $719 million, or $2.51 per diluted share, on Thursday, ending a two-quarter losing streak. Analysts had expected a loss of 15 cents per share, according to Zacks. But about $715 million of the profit came from gains on crypto Coinbase holds as an investment. Excluding those and other investment gains, adjusted net income was $78 million.

Total revenue came in at $1.22 billion, in line with expectations, flat from the second quarter and down 35% from a year earlier. Transaction revenue slipped 1% to $590 million but beat the roughly $527 million analysts expected, as crypto volatility sank to multiyear lows and trading volume across the market fell.

The standout was prediction markets, which crossed $200 million in annualized revenue, making it one of the fastest-growing products in Coinbase’s history, the company said.

Daily traders grew more than 60% from the prior quarter, and revenue has compounded 82% per quarter since launch. CEO Brian Armstrong said the product kept “growing even through slower sports months.”

“Prediction markets are adding another revenue stream and are less correlated to crypto volatility,” CFO Alesia Haas said.

Coinbase also cut its 2026 adjusted expense guidance by another $100 million at the midpoint, to between $4.15 billion and $4.3 billion, following cost reductions in May. Adjusted EBITDA jumped 60% from the prior quarter to $333 million. Through Oct. 25, fourth-quarter transaction revenue stood at about $165 million.

The positive results follow two consecutive losing quarters. Coinbase posted a $394 million loss in the first quarter and missed estimates in the second as trading activity slowed, though analysts said at the time they weren’t worried about the stock’s slide.

Shares closed Thursday at $160.59, down 1.1%, before the results came out, and were little changed in after-hours trading. The stock is down more than 55% from its 52-week high of $361.40.

#5151. Binance hackers raced to move billions before it could be frozen

Attackers raced to convert stolen stablecoins into crypto that issuers could not freeze as industry players like Tether and Circle moved to immobilize billions of dollars in assets.

Attackers behind Binance’s estimated $6.3 billion hack still control billions of dollars in stolen cryptocurrency despite efforts by token issuers to freeze the proceeds.

The attack, the largest in crypto history, began at 8:04 pm UTC Thursday and the perpetrators, linked to the same North Korean hackers behind last month’s Bitget hack, immediately began converting assets.

The game plan for the hackers was to convert the assets that could be frozen, like USDT and USDC, into ones that could not, such as Bitcoin (BTC) and Ether (ETH).

CertiK engineer Dickson Wu traced $500 million in USDT that was sent to one address, with $325 million sold to market makers for ETH by 20:59 UTC, less than an hour after the hack began.

They were racing against some of crypto’s most powerful gatekeepers: stablecoin giants like Tether and Circle, who had already begun blacklisting addresses associated with the theft.

Binance said Friday that about $3 billion of the stolen assets were frozen onchain out of an estimated $6.3 billion total, and that it is working with law enforcement, issuers and blockchain analytics firms to trace additional funds.

The scale of the theft is mind-boggling, even in crypto terms, but with around half of the assets already frozen, how much of the stolen crypto can the perpetrators actually spend?

Unlike BTC or ETH, which are decentralized cryptocurrencies with no central issuer and no one to hit the pause button, centralized stablecoins like Tether’s USDT and Circle’s USDC have issuers that can freeze tokens held at specific addresses.

Binance said it is working with law enforcement to trace the funds. Source: Binance

That gives them the power to act swiftly in incidents like these. Tether froze around $1.567 billion in USDT in total, with the first major freeze happening just 37 minutes after the stolen funds reached the attacker’s main Ethereum wallet.

Related: Binance says it holds $5B in matching tokens to cover hack losses

Circle froze around $823 million in USDC three minutes later, with other issuers like Ripple and World Liberty Financial following soon after.

The ability to immobilize crypto is not exclusively a stablecoin feature, and despite not having central issuers, some decentralized protocols like Arbitrum did not sit idly by.

Arbitrum’s Security Council moved around $30.5 million in ARB and ETH into an intermediary frozen wallet, where they can only be moved after further action by the council or a vote by Arbitrum DAO governance.

One important distinction remains — frozen funds don’t necessarily equate to recovered funds. Immobilizing assets prevents the suspected attackers from spending them, but it doesn’t automatically return them to Binance or its users.

Decentralized lending protocol Aave adds another layer of complexity to the equation. Between 20:58 and 21:01 UTC, a wallet associated with the attackers deposited $60 million in USDT into Aave V3 on Ethereum and borrowed $44.6 million of wrapped Ether (WETH), around 40 minutes before the first freeze.

LlamaRisk, an Aave risk service provider, said in its 1:00 am update that the attacker’s position subsequently held around $59.9 million in USDT collateral against $43.8 million in debt. Aave said the position remained overcollateralized and did not put protocol suppliers at risk.

Largest crypto heists of all time. Source: Elliptic

Since the USDT had already been deposited into Aave’s lending pool, blacklisting the attacker’s wallet could not automatically retrieve the collateral held by the protocol, as Aave Chan Initiative founder Marc Zeller explained:

To prevent further deposits and borrowing, Aave temporarily froze markets across its V3 and V4 deployments, although existing users could still withdraw funds, repay loans and liquidate positions.

Aave later lifted its precautionary freeze across V3 and V4 markets, saying its contracts were unaffected and positions remained overcollateralized.

“Just another day in crypto,” Zeller said.

Elliptic estimated that $2.84 billion had been frozen by 7:00 am UTC on Friday, while the attackers still controlled around $3.33 billion in assets that issuers could not immobilize.

And while the freeze almost halved the attackers’ profit pool, that doesn’t mean all the remaining funds are safe to recover.

Bitcoin and Ether don’t have a centralized token issuer that can blacklist individual addresses like Tether or Circle. So once stolen assets are converted into these native cryptocurrencies, transactions can be restricted, but freezing them is a lot harder.

Binance has pledged to cover all losses using its Secure Asset Fund for Users (SAFU) and company funds, and says user assets remain safe; according to the latest update from Binance chief executive Richard Teng, the exchange is already “recovering stolen assets for users.”

#5150. Strategy (MSTR) Adds 811 Bitcoin, Strive (ASST) Follows Suit With 124 as Both Keep Stacking

Strategy bought 811 bitcoin for $65.5 million and Strive added 124 BTC last week, with the two firms together adding 935 coins and Strategy now holding 849,327 BTC.

Strategy bought another 811 bitcoin last week. The Saylor-led firm spent about $65.5 million at an average of $80,846 per coin between October 26 and November 1, according to its 8-K filing on Monday. That brings total holdings to 849,327 BTC, bought for roughly $64.1 billion at an average of $75,451.

The money didn’t come from new preferred stock this time. Strategy sold about 1.18 million shares of MSTR common stock for roughly $164 million, sent $65.5 million into bitcoin, and parked the remaining $98.5 million in USD cash. It also spent around $208 million buying back about 2.1 million shares of its STRC preferred.

Strategy spent much of the year leaning on preferred issuance to fund purchases, and the company is now using the buyback to tidy up its preferred stack while still adding coins.

Saylor teased the purchase Sunday with his usual tracker chart and a line that made the rounds on X: “We found room for even more orange.”

The buy landed a few days after Strategy reported its third-quarter results on Thursday. Net income hit $19.5 billion, up from $2.79 billion a year earlier, driven by a $20.9 billion operating gain tied to the bitcoin price rising during the quarter. That gain is a paper one under fair-value accounting, not cash. Saylor called it the start of the next bitcoin bull market and pointed to bitcoin trading about 21% above its 200-week moving average. The same release said CFO Andrew Kang will step down November 5, with COO Reza Virasteh taking over the role in January.

Strategy shareholders also recently approved a proposal allowing four preferred stocks — STRF, STRC, STRK, and STRD — to accrue dividends daily, including weekends and holidays, with 99.3% of votes cast in favor. The change was intended to improve liquidity, reduce reinvestment delays, and increase price stability, while keeping dividend rates and total obligations unchanged. The new payment schedule remained subject to board approval and regulatory filings, with implementation expected to begin in November 2026 for STRC and January 2027 for the other preferred stocks.

BREAKING: Public companies Strive and Strategy bought a combined 934 Bitcoin worth $72.6 million.Strategy: 810 BTCStrive: 124 BTC pic.twitter.com/ObXGCz7weH

Strive is still in the race for second place among public holders. The Matt Cole-run firm added 124 BTC between October 24 and 30 at about $80,526 each, lifting its stack to 43,124 BTC.

That’s a smaller buy than its previous week, when it added more than 6,000 coins, but Cole has been consistent about his approach. He has said the company is buying “hand over fist” when prices dip, and he has framed the funding model around perpetual preferred equity as a way to avoid forced selling.

Strive’s cash position slipped about $14 million to $490 million over the same stretch, and it still holds 505,000 shares of Strategy’s STRC.

Meanwhile, the biggest story in crypto last week wasn’t a buy. On October 29, Binance was hit by what it now estimates is a $6.3 billion security breach, one that started with a compromised DevOps laptop.

Blockchain analytics firms including Chainalysis and TRM Labs have linked the attack to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group, and the drain reportedly took about 20 minutes. Binance says it has frozen roughly $3 billion of the stolen funds and will cover losses from its SAFU fund and company reserves.

Bitcoin slid toward $76,000 on the news, and it fell about 3.6% over the week.

Strategy’s stock dropped about 9% last week and closed Friday at $130.51. The company still has about $18.6 billion of MSTR available under its ATM program, which means more buys are likely if the market cooperates.

Strive shares were down around 20% last week. At the time of writing, bitcoin is near $78,000.

#5147. Binance Drained of $5.5 Billion in Suspected Hack, Tether and Circle Freeze $1.8 Billion

Binance Drained of $5.5 Billion in Suspected Hack, Tether and Circle Freeze $1.8 Billion

At least $5.57 billion in crypto has left wallets linked to Binance across three blockchains in what appears to be a breach of the world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange.

If confirmed as a hack, it would be the biggest crypto hack on record. Binance has not confirmed its exchange has been hacked but said it is aware of the “potential security issue and is actively investigating.”

“We have not confirmed any impact to user funds,” the company posted on X. “Our security teams are reviewing all relevant information with urgency, and we will provide updates through official Binance channels as soon as we have verified information.”

Our teams are aware of reports of a potential security issue and are actively investigating. At this stage, we have not confirmed any impact to user funds.

Our security teams are reviewing all relevant information with urgency, and we will provide updates through official… pic.twitter.com/dN76yhzYM0

Binance is the world's largest crypto exchange, where millions of people buy, sell and store their coins. The money came from its hot wallets, the internet-connected wallets exchanges use to process customer withdrawals.

The tally counts $3.51 billion on Ethereum, $1.44 billion in 18,270 Bitcoin and $630 million on Tron. The first Ethereum transaction, at 20:24 UTC, sent 167,464 ETH plus 63 token transfers to a brand-new address, including 1.615 billion USDT, a stablecoin built to always trade at $1, according to Etherscan.

Three minutes earlier, an address funded through Tornado Cash, a mixer that scrambles the trail of crypto payments, submitted a delegation for a Binance hot wallet using EIP-7702, an Ethereum feature that lets an ordinary wallet run smart-contract code. “7702 lets a normal wallet execute delegated code. The delegation persists; that code can authorize later transfers without another signature from the wallet,” Odysseas Lamtzidi, founder of Phylax Systems wrote on X.

1/4 EIP-7702 can turn the wallet itself into the sweeper.

A Binance-labeled wallet moved 167,464 ETH + 63 tokens, including 1.615B USDT, just 3m24s after a signed delegation was installed.

This is bad.https://t.co/GrvUiya16D pic.twitter.com/zONu8cwW63

— Odysseus | phylax.systems (@odysseas_eth) October 29, 2026

How the attacker got the required signature is still unknown. Tether froze 975.1 million USDT in the main receiving address, and Circle, issuer of the rival stablecoin USDC, froze 823 million USDC in the same wallet at 21:04 UTC, bringing the total frozen to about $1.8 billion. Both issuers can block specific addresses from moving their tokens, but the attacker is moving funds fast.

"Circle is closely tracking reports of a potential exploit involving Binance," Circle CEO Jeremy Allaire posted on X. "In coordination with law enforcement, we have frozen more than 800M USDC associated with the incident and will continue supporting the investigation."

Total on the hack is at least $5.57B across 3 chains (likely more)

Tether has started freezing and this time even Circle acted and managed to freeze 800M in a single wallet.

Still $800M could be frozen, attacker is moving funds fast https://t.co/km4eyJWEa8 pic.twitter.com/IL6wBfZK2r

Binance lost 7,000 Bitcoin, about $40 million, in a 2019 hack. The $5.57 billion tally would be more than triple the roughly $1.5 billion Bybit heist in February 2025, the previous record.

#5144. Binance investigating hack as suspicious outflows reach up to $5.8 billion

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Onchain data shows about $3.55 billion in incoming transfers to the unlabeled wallet, with nearly $900 million subsequently moved across other wallets.

Binance, the world's largest cryptocurrency exchange, appears to have suffered a major hack.

Onchain data flagged by analyst Spreek shows about $3.55 billion in incoming transfers to the unlabeled wallet, with nearly $900 million subsequently moved across other wallets. Conor Grogan, a senior director and product leader at Coinbase, says the amount is up to $5.8 billion.

While Binance has not publicly confirmed a breach, co-founder and Chief Customer Service Officer Yi He said the firm is actively investigating.

"We are aware of reports of a potential security issue and we are actively investigating." He wrote in a post on X. "Our security teams are reviewing all relevant information with urgency, and we will provide updates through official Binance channels as soon as we have verified information."

Later Thursday night, He said users' funds are safe and that Binance will cover all hacking-related losses.

"Deposits and withdrawals are temporarily suspended while our team conducts security checks, freezes funds, and works with law enforcement," she wrote on X. "We will share a full post-mortem once the investigation is complete."

Former CEO Changpeng Zhao said user funds are safe and that he expects them to be fully covered, while crediting Tether and Circle for quickly freezing the funds.

"I don't manage Binance (.com) anymore, but as a shareholder, I expect all user funds will be fully covered," Zhao wrote on X.

Onchain data showed Tether and Circle froze a combined $1.79 billion in USDT and USDC held in a wallet that received billions of dollars in transfers from Binance hot wallets. Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said the stablecoin firm froze more than 1.567 billion USDT, while Circle's Jeremy Allaire said it has frozen more than 800 million USDC associated with the incident.

"Protecting the industry and our partners is our main priority," Ardoino said. "Stay safe everyone."

Blockaid said it is investigating approximately $3.3 billion in suspicious outflows from Binance hot wallets across Ethereum, Arbitrum, and Avalanche.

Aave froze lending markets to prevent the Binance hacker from borrowing, onchain data shows. Aave said its contracts are unaffected, and positions remain overcollateralized.

"As a precaution, all DAO-operated markets across V3 and V4 have been frozen. The freeze pauses new deposits and borrows, and users can still withdraw and repay their existing positions," the lending protocol said on X. "We will monitor the situation and unfreeze as soon as possible."

The BNB token was down more than 5.5% at publication time.

For its part, Binance.US was not affected by the reported incident. "We are a separate, independent platform, and customer funds and services are secure and operating normally," the firm said. "Rest assured, all user funds are safe."

This story has been updated with additional reporting and comments from Aave, Binance.US, and Yi He.

#5142. Binance $6.3B record hack sparks pre-election political clashes

Some political and administration figures tried to make hay of the biggest-ever crypto heist.

Soon after the $6.3 billion hack of Binance, the biggest in crypto history, some political and administration figures began offering their opinions with a US election approaching on Nov. 3 and voting already having begun in many locations.

President Donald Trump was asked at a news conference on Friday about the hack of Binance. “We don’t know. We’re looking at that, the hack with Binance. They’re saying they’re going to get it back, they’re saying they’re guaranteeing it,” he replied.

The crypto exchange said in a post on X late Friday that it would cover any losses. “We want to be clear about our commitment: Binance will absorb the financial impact of these stolen funds, not our users.” It added that about $3 billion of the stolen funds had already been frozen in various locations. On Saturday, Binance said it had already covered 90% of the impacted amount of the hacked loss.

It was not clear whether there was any immediate political advantage to be gained from the hack, which was widely attributed to a North Korean group, but Republican and Democratic sides began staking out positions almost immediately.

Wyoming Senator Cynthia Lummis, a Republican and longtime crypto advocate, cited the stalled Clarity Act in a Friday post on X. “Yesterday, hackers stole more than $6 billion from Binance in less than 4 hours, the biggest heist in history. The Clarity Act would have given crypto firms legal cover to freeze suspicious transactions the instant law enforcement calls. Every minute lost is money gone for good,” she wrote.

Related: FBI and Secret Service join response to Binance’s $6.3B hack

The Senate on Sept. 15 failed to pass a cloture motion on the Clarity Act that would have established the country’s first regulatory framework for digital assets.

The bill had stalled over proposed ethics provisions that would restrict government officials and their families from issuing or profiting from digital assets while in office.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission then submitted a regulatory action on Sept. 18 covering crypto asset transactions and markets for White House review, as the agency moved forward with its approach to overseeing the digital asset sector.

CFTC Chair Michael Selig referred to crypto market regulation on Friday after the Binance hack, saying in a TV appearance: “This proves our exact point in proposing regulations for crypto markets. … The real issue here is that these are unregulated offshore exchanges and they don’t have the same sorts of safeguards that we have in our financial markets here in the United States.”

Still, Democratic Representative Maxine Waters said in a Friday news release that “The reported $6.3 billion hack of Binance should be a wake-up call to anyone who thinks the cryptocurrency industry can operate without strong accountability. Yet, Trump and Congressional Republicans have been set on pushing a deregulatory agenda that weakens compliance mandates and oversight of the crypto industry, not to mention the Trump family’s own questionable ties to the company.”

Recap: How Binance’s record $6.3B hack unfolded

She also commented on past relations between Binance and the Trump family, saying, “Remember that after Binance’s owner, Changpeng Zhao (CZ), gave over $2 billion to the Trump family’s World Liberty Financial, Trump’s Securities and Exchange Commission’s Chair Paul Atkins dropped the SEC’s slam-dunk fraud case against Binance and CZ, and Trump fully pardoned CZ, allowing him to again get involved with crypto.”

Meanwhile, Binance said it was working with law enforcement, blockchain intelligence firms, token issuers and other exchanges to trace and recover the stolen funds.

The company also promised to publish a postmortem incident report after conducting a full security review.

“We will provide a detailed technical report outlining what happened, the lessons learned and the additional safeguards being implemented,” Binance said.

#5140. Binance Hacker Hunts for Exit Ramps as DeFi Exchanges Pause Markets

Binance Hacker Hunts for Exit Ramps as DeFi Exchanges Pause Markets

The attacker behind the wallets draining Binance is using decentralized exchanges such as Uniswap and Aave as exits, swapping stablecoins that issuers can freeze for Ethereum that nobody can, according to Etherscan records for 10 wallets, eight of them labeled as exploiters.

Earlier today, wallets linked to the world’s largest crypto exchange were drained of at least $5.5 billion, with the total tally still climbing. The company said it was investigating the security incident but did not confirm user funds were affected, though security experts widely believe the exchange was indeed hacked. It stands to be the largest hack in the crypto industry's 18-year history.

Stablecoin issuers Tether and Circle moved quickly to blacklist the suspected attacker’s addresses in order to freeze a portion of the funds and make them inaccessible. As centralized issuers, they each have the ability to blacklist addresses so their tokens cannot move, and more than $2 billion of those two tokens have been frozen so far by different stablecoin providers. ETH has no issuer, so every dollar converted before a freeze lands is out of their reach.

The swaps run through Uniswap, a decentralized exchange where trades settle automatically with no company in the middle.

One relay wallet received 20,940 ETH ($45.58 Million) from Uniswap’s UniswapX contract at 20:34 UTC, another got 3,552 ETH (over $8 million) at 20:47, and two later wallets took in about 18,085 ETH (around $41 million) from Uniswap contracts, in 7,444 ETH and 10,642 ETH batches, and about 16,498 ETH ($37.5 million), in 6,946 ETH and 9,552 ETH batches.

Aave, a lending app where users deposit crypto as collateral to borrow other coins, offered a second exit. Between 20:57 and 21:01 UTC, one wallet deposited $10 million and then $50 million in USDT and borrowed 3,271 ETH ($7.43 million) and then 16,491 ETH ($37.47 million) against it, which prompted cybersecurity experts Odysseas Lamtzidi, founder of Phylax Systems, to warn of “contagion in full effect.”

Contagion in full effect https://t.co/aNz2snt8E6

— Odysseus | phylax.systems (@odysseas_eth) October 29, 2026

Aave has since frozen its V3 markets on Ethereum mainnet, which blocks new deposits and borrowing.

Kamino, a decentralized finance protocol built on the Solana blockchain, also paused its Solana and Bitcoin markets as a precautionary measure.

Kamino can confirm that it is currently unaffected by the ongoing Binance situation

Out of an abundance of caution, we have temporarily paused borrowing in the SOL/BTC Market

We are actively monitoring the situation and are in communication with various partners. Any further…

The ETH is piling up rather than moving on. A wallet labeled Exploiter 8 still held 195,460 ETH ($444.2 million) at 22:03 UTC, and another 57,891.77 ETH ($131.6 million) reached one labeled Exploiter 11, together about 253,000 ETH, or roughly $570 million. That second pile arrived after four hops in 28 minutes, from Exploiter 4 to 5, 5 to 6, 6 to 7 and 7 to 11.

The attacker also swapped 36 million RLUSD, Ripple’s dollar-backed stablecoin, for 15,439 ETH, while 52.54 million RLUSD remained unfrozen at the holding wallet, according to an on-chain update circulating on X. Etherscan showed the main receiving wallet holding about $88.5 million in RLUSD at 20:41 UTC, which matches the two figures combined.

Ripple has since frozen about $200 million, according to Binance co-CEO Yi He. “Thanks to the Ripple team for promptly freezing approximately 200 million. User Funds are SAFU,” a reference to the exchange’s emergency reserve fund, Yi He wrote on X.

“Ripple is closely monitoring reports of a potential exploit involving Binance and its attackers’ RLUSD holdings and we are actively freezing affected funds,” Jack McDonald, Ripple’s senior vice president for stablecoins, said in a statement. “We’re coordinating with relevant parties and stand ready to support any investigation and appropriate action to keep users protected.”

Ripple is closely monitoring reports of a potential exploit involving Binance and its attackers’ RLUSD holdings and we are actively freezing affected funds. We’re coordinating with relevant parties and stand ready to support any investigation and appropriate action to keep users…

— Jack McDonald (@_JackMcDonald_) October 29, 2026

Tornado Cash, a mixer that scrambles the trail of crypto payments, appears so far only as funding for the address that set up the attack.

#5139. Bitcoin slides below $77,000, BNB drops amid Binance hack

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

BNB lost 7.1%, while Binance Co-CEO Yi He pledged that the exchange will cover all user losses resulting from the hack.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell below $77,000 late Thursday, alongside declines in other major cryptocurrencies, as the Binance exploit intensified a market-wide sell-off.

The world's largest cryptocurrency dropped 4.5% over the past 24 hours to trade at $76,664 as of 10:00 p.m. ET Thursday, according to The Block's bitcoin price page.

BNB (BNB), the native cryptocurrency that powers the Binance ecosystem, lost 7.1% to change hands at $698.65. Ethereum slid 5.2% to $2,190, while XRP fell 7% to $1.21.

Crypto liquidations totaled $1.55 billion over the past 24 hours, with 246,482 traders liquidated, according to CoinGlass data. Those included $1.34 billion in long positions. The largest observed liquidation order was on Binance-BTCUSDT, valued at $11.09 million.

The crypto market decline coincided with a major Thursday hack at Binance, the world's largest cryptocurrency exchange. Blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis estimated that roughly $5.9 billion was stolen at the time of the theft.

"The Binance exploit has intensified market-wide selling, with BNB facing the greatest exposure. About $1.5 billion in 24-hour liquidations, predominantly long positions, has amplified pressure on BTC and ETH," Dominick John, analyst of Zeus Research, told The Block.

"Market recovery hinges on Binance restoring confidence in exchange security and customer funds," John added.

However, Rick Maeda, head of Markets at Laevitas, suggested that the market reaction to the Binance hack appears to be "fairly contained" and that the BNB's drop was "quite modest for possibly the largest exchange theft ever."

"The exchange's $144 billion assets under custody, the fact that they paid a $4.3 billion penalty to the U.S. government in 2024 for AML breaches with no issue, as well as the swift responses from stablecoin providers such as Tether and Circle to freeze over $2.4 billion have all contributed to the lack of panic across the industry," said Maeda.

Jeff Ko, chief analyst of ViaBTC, shared similar views. "I don't expect the stolen BTC to add much selling pressure," said Ko. "The broader crypto market was already leaning bearish, which is why the sell-off spread so quickly across the board. That said, I believe Binance can absorb the loss comfortably."

Binance said on X that the currently verified frozen amount from the hack is around $3 billion. Binance Co-CEO Yi He also said that the exchange will cover all user losses resulting from the hack.

Coinbase CEO Brian Armstrong said Coinbase is "doing everything we can to support in the current situation," including blocking transfers to compromised wallets.

"Moments like this call for the industry to work together," Armstrong added.
