#5106. Elon Musk, CZ Trade Blows Over SpaceX Shares After $6.4B Binance Hack

Elon Musk, CZ Trade Blows Over SpaceX Shares After $6.4B Binance Hack

Binance co-founder Changpeng Zhao, better known as CZ, called out Elon Musk on Friday after the billionaire appeared to mock the exchange following a $6.4 billion hack—the largest in history—reminding him that Binance holds SpaceX shares worth more than $1 billion.

"Elon, have a lot of respect for you, but bad time to troll. Please stop," CZ wrote on X. "Our SPCX shares (from our $500m investment in your Twitter takeover) are worth more than a billion. Would appreciate if you show a little support. Thanks!"

The exchange followed Musk's response to a post estimating that, as a lot of the stolen funds have been frozen, Binance's losses could reach around $1 billion, despite holding approximately $144 billion in assets under custody.

Musk had replied to the discussion with a grimacing emoji, prompting another user to share an old post from former FTX CEO Sam Bankman-Fried claiming his exchange had enough money to cover its losses. FTX famously collapsed weeks later.

Elon, have a lot of respect for you, but bad time to troll. Please stop.

Our SPCX shares (from our $500m investment in your Twitter takeover) are worth more than a billion.

Would appreciate if you show a little support. Thanks!

— CZ 🔶 BNB (@cz_binance) October 30, 2026

CZ had initially responded to the loss estimate by saying the damage might be somewhat higher but that Binance would still be able to cover it. Musk's emoji appeared to draw an uncomfortable parallel with the FTX episode, although he did not elaborate on what he meant.

Of course, CZ didn't imply anything else about what Binance may do with its SpaceX shares.

Binance co-CEO Yi He also joined the exchange, addressing CZ in a post that appeared to respond to the suggestion that the company might need to liquidate its SpaceX holdings.

"Reporting to the major shareholder: Our company's revenue for the year is sufficient to cover the losses, so we do not yet need to sell the SpaceX shares you are bullish on," she wrote, adding a shrugging emoji.

CZ subsequently clarified that he had never suggested selling the shares. He said he was simply showing Musk some of the funds Binance had available, hoping to reassure him about the exchange's financial position.

😂 did say anything about selling SPCX shares, was just saying/showing some funds that he might know, so that he can be less worried.

— CZ 🔶 BNB (@cz_binance) October 30, 2026

The argument comes as Binance deals with the largest cryptocurrency theft on record. At least $6.4 billion in crypto was taken from wallets associated with the exchange on Thursday, October 29, across multiple blockchains.

Just yesterday, initial reports showed that at least $5.57 billion had left Binance-linked wallets across Ethereum, Bitcoin, and Tron. The attackers moved funds through several transactions, including a transfer of more than $1.6 billion in USDT to a newly created Ethereum address.

The incident also exposed an important weakness in how cryptocurrency can be recovered after a theft. Tether and Circle, the companies behind the USDT and USDC stablecoins, froze approximately $1.8 billion in assets associated with the attackers in the initial response, then subsequently froze a total of $2.39 billion. Unlike those tokens, Ethereum cannot be frozen by a central issuer, making it harder to stop stolen funds once they are converted.

The attackers subsequently used decentralized exchanges such as Uniswap and lending protocol Aave to move funds into Ethereum. These platforms allow users to trade or borrow cryptocurrency without relying on a traditional intermediary to approve each transaction. Decrypt tracked wallets receiving tens of thousands of ETH through these operations, including 19,762 ETH borrowed against approximately $60 million in USDT.

The suspected attackers have also been linked to North Korea. In a Friday report, blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis attributed the attack to TraderTraitor, a North Korean hacking group previously blamed for the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025. Chainalysis estimated the Binance theft at approximately $6.4 billion.

Binance has since said it has reserved more than $6.34 billion in company funds to cover the incident, including $5 billion already held in the same tokens that were stolen. The exchange said it is acquiring the remaining assets through market and over-the-counter purchases, which can take longer when certain tokens have limited liquidity.

#5103. Inside the largest crypto hack on record: How attackers breached Binance for $6.3 billion

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Onchain investigators suggest the attackers exploited Binance's internal systems and wallet-signing mechanisms to move funds across multiple blockchains.

Binance was hit Thursday by the largest crypto hack to date, with blockchain investigators estimating that more than $6 billion in assets were stolen. Beyond the financial toll, the breach has raised questions about the security of centralized exchange infrastructure, particularly the hot wallets used to custody billions of dollars in customer assets.

Binance said its initial investigation pointed to a compromised DevOps engineer's laptop and unauthorized access to a service container. The exchange has not identified a private-key leak, while co-CEO Richard Teng has described the attack as more complex than a compromise of a single employee's permissions.

Blockchain investigators have attributed the attack to North Korean hackers. Their analysis suggests the attackers exploited Binance's internal systems and wallet-signing mechanisms to move funds across multiple blockchains.

The attacker began staging at least 36 hours before the theft, funding an operational wallet with small Tornado Cash withdrawals, according to TRM Labs. It deployed and tested malicious code on three wallets before executing the attack.

On Ethereum, the attacker used EIP-7702 to turn a Binance hot wallet into a sweeper controlled by the attacker. It prepared 50 pre-signed authorizations to ensure one would remain valid when executed. TRM said coordinated transfers across nine other blockchains suggest a compromise of Binance's broader hot-wallet signing infrastructure.

Elliptic's analysis suggests the attacker retained access beyond the initial exploit, using one compromised wallet's owner rights to withdraw funds from another Binance wallet and repeatedly sweep new deposits. A second Ethereum hot wallet also sent $174 million in assets directly to the attacker, further pointing to access to Binance's broader transaction-signing infrastructure.

Simone Imbrescia, vice president of Immunefi, said the exploit highlights how an attack's sophistication does not necessarily lie in its onchain code, but in gaining access to the infrastructure capable of authorizing transactions.

"One of the striking things about this breach is how little code was actually needed on-chain. The sweeper contract was only around 130 lines," Imbrescia said. "The hard part sometimes is getting access to a system powerful enough to make very simple code devastating."

Chainalysis, Elliptic, and TRM attributed the attack to North Korean hacking groups, with TRM explicitly linking it to the same group connected to the $1.5 billion Bybit hack.

Chainalysis identified onchain links between funds stolen from Binance and proceeds from last month's Bitget exploit, which was also attributed to North Korean hackers. TRM cited similarities to previous North Korean operations, including Tornado Cash funding, advance staging and pre-signed authorizations. Elliptic also cautioned that the onchain overlap with the Bitget incident involved only a small portion of the Binance funds and did not conclusively establish that the same attackers were responsible.

Chainalysis identified thousands of transactions in a short period, with stolen funds repeatedly circulating between addresses. The attacker also attempted to avoid stablecoin freezes by converting $325 million in stablecoins into $285 million in ETH over nearly 20 minutes.

Elliptic said the attacker deposited $59.9 million in stolen USDT into Aave as collateral and borrowed 19,762 ETH. The loan remained open and was close to liquidation as of the firm's latest update. The attacker also swapped hundreds of millions of dollars in USDT for ETH through decentralized exchanges and market makers, accepting losses of as much as 26% against market prices to move funds into assets that issuers cannot freeze.

Aave said Friday that it had unfrozen all markets across its V3 and V4 protocols, adding that its smart contracts were unaffected and user positions remained fully collateralized.

Binance has approximately $5 billion in affected assets, with another $1.3 billion being acquired through market and OTC channels as part of its commitment to cover the losses.

Teng said the exchange was targeting Monday to publish its initial proof-of-reserves report and was working to restore withdrawals.

"We are very hopeful that users will be able to withdraw prior to our Sunday deadline, which is on track to be within 40 hours," Teng said in an X video on Friday.

TD Cowen analysts said the breach highlights the custody and cybersecurity risks associated with centralized exchanges but does not necessarily undermine the broader digital asset industry.

"The evidence available so far points to a compromise of infrastructure at a centralized intermediary, rather than a failure of Bitcoin or its underlying network," the analysts wrote. "Put differently, we do not stop using dollars when a bank is robbed, just as car theft does not invalidate the automobile."

The incident also highlights the need to secure the systems and processes that control access to private keys, Imbrescia said. Crypto security efforts often focus on protecting keys and auditing smart contracts, but the people, infrastructure and workflows capable of instructing those keys are part of the security perimeter, she added.

"If an attacker compromises that operational layer, they may not need to steal a key or discover an exotic smart-contract vulnerability at all," she said. "The broader lesson is that access controls, internal infrastructure and transaction-authorisation processes need to be secured with the same intensity as the keys themselves."

Unpacking Telegram’s evolving crypto ecosystem

Unpacking Telegram’s evolving crypto ecosystem

#5102. Bitcoin held up better than expected after Binance hack, Strive CEO says

Strive CEO Matt Cole says the Binance hack has not changed his long-term case for Bitcoin, but cautions it is too early to call a market bottom.

Bitcoin has held up better than expected following the $6.3 billion Binance hack, according to Strive CEO Matt Cole, who said the breach does not undermine the cryptocurrency’s long-term investment thesis.

Speaking on Cointelegraph’s Chain Reaction on Friday, Cole said the breach had not changed his long-term investment case for Bitcoin. He compared the attack on Binance’s wallets to a bank robbery rather than a failure of Bitcoin’s network.

Bitcoin traded near $77,400 at 4:00 pm on Saturday, about 4% below its price shortly before the attack began Thursday, according to CoinGecko data.

Strive, a publicly traded company that holds Bitcoin on its balance sheet, reported owning 43,000 BTC as of Oct. 23.

“I do have conviction that over any reasonable time frame, this does not change the investment thesis on Bitcoin,” Cole said.

Cole said Bitcoin’s decline was less severe than he expected given the scale of the breach and broader market weakness, although he acknowledged that it was too early to determine whether the cryptocurrency had reached a price bottom.

Cole described the market as being in an “early spring” recovery phase that could last another six to nine months, with prices gradually rising rather than surging to new highs.

Paul Chapman, financial crime director at Wirex, also distinguished the attack on Binance from a compromise of Bitcoin’s network. “It’s important to be clear that Bitcoin itself hasn’t been hacked — this was an attack on one company’s systems, not the network,” he told Cointelegraph.

Related: Strive closes $630M SATS IPO, leaves Bitcoin allocation unspecified

According to Glassnode, Thursday’s Binance breach was the largest cryptocurrency hack on record, with around $6.3 billion affected, exceeding the combined losses from the next nine largest hacks. The previous record was the $1.5 billion Bybit exploit in February 2025.

Glassnode said three of the 10 largest crypto hacks have occurred since the beginning of 2025, all involving exchanges.

Binance hack exceeds the next nine largest crypto exploits combined. Source: Glassnode

By comparison, following the Bybit hack in 2025, Bitcoin fell roughly 5% from nearly $100,000 to around $95,000 amid the breach and broader market volatility, according to a Bitfinex market report.

Anchorage Digital data cited by CNBC put Bitcoin’s average decline in the first 24 hours after previous crypto exchange exploits at 8.3%.

Blockaid CEO Ido Ben-Natan said the attackers appeared to have compromised Binance’s transaction signing process. Binance said Friday that it had found no evidence of leaked wallet private keys, though its investigation remained ongoing.

Blockaid’s analysis identified an Ethereum transaction containing 50 signed authorizations covering different transaction counts. A matching authorization allowed attacker-controlled code to operate through the wallet. Ben-Natan argued that checks before signing could have blocked the malicious authorization.

#5099. Binance Sets Aside $6.34B to Cover Hack Losses, CEO Says 'Your Assets Are Safe'

Binance Sets Aside $6.34B to Cover Hack Losses, CEO Says 'Your Assets Are Safe'

Binance will cover every dollar lost in this week's hack with its own money, CEO Richard Teng said in a video address on Friday, adding that customer funds were never touched.

"We are committed to covering all hacked losses through our company funds. Your assets are safe," Teng said, according to a transcript he tweeted.

An update on the security incident. pic.twitter.com/qn2804fO5z

— Richard Teng (@_RichardTeng) October 30, 2026

Binance has reserved more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover the stolen assets, $5 billion of which is already held in the exact tokens taken, including USDC, USDT, BTC, ETH and BNB, he said. The exchange is buying the rest through market and over-the-counter channels.

Teng said the hack hit Binance's own hot wallet, not customer funds. "We never use any customer funds, so they are not impacted," he said, adding that most user assets are held in cold wallets and only moved into a hot wallet when a user withdraws. Binance's ability to absorb a loss of this size comes from keeping much of its operating earnings in crypto, he said.

Binance had earlier said it would cover all losses from its SAFU emergency fund and company funds.

Teng said the hack "was not carried out by one person." Binance initially traced it to a compromised laptop but has since found additional points of breach. The attacker also used a self-destruct mechanism that destroyed tooling and traces along the way, making the attack harder to investigate, Teng said.

The attacker used EIP-7702, an Ethereum feature that lets standard wallets run smart contract code, to obtain onchain execution approval over a hot wallet, according to Teng. "That delegation did not pass through our normal approval controls," he said, which is why Binance paused withdrawals to rebuild and retest its infrastructure. Blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis have attributed the theft to North Korean hacking group TraderTraitor.

Binance hopes to restore withdrawals before its Sunday deadline, Teng said, and is carrying out a full security review covering endpoints, cloud, key management and signing. It plans to publish a full proof of reserves report by Monday, with an initial view before then.

The attacker is still moving funds. Between 11:37 a.m. and 12:12 p.m. ET on Friday, a Tron wallet that Arkham attributes to the hacker sent about 5.19 million TRX, worth roughly $1.73 million, to 12 addresses, according to Arkham data.

All markets are now unfrozen across Aave V3 and V4.

They were frozen as a precaution after initial reports, which paused new deposits and borrows while withdrawals and repayments continued as normal.

Decentralized lending protocol Aave, where the attacker borrowed ETH against USDT while racing stablecoin freezes, according to TRM Labs, said on Friday that it had unfrozen all markets on its V3 and V4 deployments. It had frozen them as a precaution after the first reports of the hack, pausing new deposits and borrowing while withdrawals and repayments continued. "Aave was unaffected, and all positions remain overcollateralized," founder Stani Kulechov wrote.

Binance co-founder Changpeng "CZ" Zhao said that while the $6.3 billion theft is the largest in dollar terms, it is not the largest in Bitcoin terms, pointing to Mt. Gox's loss of 650,000 BTC, more than 5% of the supply. Unlike at Mt. Gox and FTX, where nearly all users were affected, Binance is covering the losses itself, he said. "It hurts, but we survive," Zhao wrote.

#5098. Chainalysis says hacker using ‘offensive agentic techniques’ accessed its customers’ names and emails

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

In the midst of tracking the fallout from the multibillion-dollar Binance exploit, blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis disclosed that its own customer data was accessed by an AI-assisted hacker.

Blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis said an unidentified hacker using "offensive agentic techniques" broke into its systems and internal tools this month and accessed some "non-sensitive" customer data, according to a notice sent to affected customers.

Chainalysis published the full text of the notice in a blog post on Friday evening. The attacker accessed "some non-sensitive customer data, such as user IDs, full names, email addresses, and organization role labels for users provisioned on our platform," the company said, pledging to send a follow-up to each client detailing which exact information was accessed.

"Based on the evidence available to date, we have found no indication that these incidents resulted in the unauthorized access of investigation content (such as graphs, notes, or cases), transaction or query results, passwords in clear text, MFA secrets, or user session tokens," Chainalysis wrote.

Chainalysis did not say when it detected the intrusion, which was first flagged by International Cyber Digest, how many customers were affected, which specific vulnerabilities the attacker exploited, or how AI agents played a role in the breach. Chainalysis declined to answer specific follow-up questions from The Block.

Chainalysis said it has contracted third-party cybersecurity experts and is using large language models to support a broad security review of its platform. The firm advised affected firms to turn on two-factor authentication and to be on the lookout for suspicious activities like unexpected password reset requests or authentication prompts.

Chainalysis said the incident is "not related to the recent crypto theft at Binance." Chainalysis, Elliptic and TRM have each attributed the roughly $6.3 billion Binance hack to North Korean hacking groups, which, according to Binance, managed to compromise an engineer's laptop and access a service container. The exchange's co-CEO Richard Teng said the breach was more complex than the compromise of a single employee's permissions, though.

On Ethereum, the attacker used EIP-7702 to turn a Binance hot wallet into a sweeper, The Block previously reported. The sweeper contract was only around 130 lines, Immunefi Vice President Simone Imbrescia said in a statement shared with The Block. "The hard part sometimes is getting access to a system powerful enough to make very simple code devastating," Imbrescia said.

Chainalysis, which puts the theft at $6.4 billion, has continued to track the stolen funds. On Friday, the firm said it was seeing "never-seen-before laundering patterns," with thousands of transactions in a short period suggesting the attackers were using automation to obscure the trail. Rather than simply forwarding funds to new addresses, the attackers repeatedly circulated the stolen crypto among addresses, often sending funds back to addresses used earlier in the sequence, the firm said, sharing a diagram showing the funds' movements.

On Saturday, Chainalysis said the exploiters have used DeFi lending protocols on the Tron network to stay ahead of stablecoin freezes since the early hours of the attack. The hackers swapped Tron's native TRX token for various stablecoins and deposited them in a lending protocol, identified by onchain analysts Specter and Ian Unsworth as JustLend, in exchange for receipt tokens, which cannot be frozen by stablecoin issuers. The attackers then moved those receipts to clean wallets, redeemed them for stablecoins and bridged the funds off Tron, swapping them for ETH on the other side, according to the firm.

"Now the exploiter is free of worry of a freeze," the firm wrote. Chainalysis' Saturday thread noted that around $2.8 billion in stablecoins have already been frozen by stablecoin firms such as Circle and Tether. "Of course, exchanging stablecoins for ETH does not make the hackers bulletproof," Chainalysis added.

#5096. Secret Service, FBI Join Binance Hack Probe as Circle Freezes $823 Million

Secret Service, FBI Join Binance Hack Probe as Circle Freezes $823 Million

Binance is working with the FBI and U.S. Secret Service after hackers stole $6.4 billion from the cryptocurrency exchange, as Circle joins the recovery effort by freezing more than $800 million in assets connected to the attack, according to Reuters.

A person familiar with the matter told Reuters that Binance was cooperating with law enforcement agencies around the world, including both U.S. agencies. The FBI confirmed it was aware of the incident and was working with public and private partners to assess its scope and coordinate investigative steps. The Secret Service remained silent.

Investigating financial crimes is part of the Secret Service’s job, beyond its better-known responsibility of protecting the president. The agency also investigates computer crimes, unauthorized access to computer systems, and crimes involving digital currencies.

Circle, the company behind USDC, a stablecoin designed to maintain a value of $1, has separately confirmed that it is coordinating with law enforcement. CEO Jeremy Allaire said the company had frozen more than $800 million in USDC associated with the Binance incident and would continue supporting the investigation.

Tether, which issues the competing USDT stablecoin, froze another $1.5 billion in tokens. Both companies can blacklist specific blockchain addresses, preventing the tokens they issue from being transferred out of those wallets. That ability gives stablecoin companies an unusual role in crypto investigations: they can effectively immobilize stolen money before investigators identify the people responsible.

Tether froze more than 1.567B USDT related to a potential Binance exchange hack.

Protecting the industry and our partners is our main priority.

— Paolo Ardoino 🤖 (@paoloardoino) October 29, 2026

This is not the first time the Secret Service has worked alongside crypto companies to investigate digital asset crimes. In April, Operation Atlantic—a joint effort involving the Secret Service, the U.K.'s National Crime Agency, Binance, Coinbase, and other crypto firms— identified more than 20,000 victims of approval phishing scams, traced $45 million in stolen funds and froze $12 million.

The Binance investigation also comes against the backdrop of a growing number of attacks attributed to North Korea. Blockchain intelligence firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis have both linked the $6.3 billion theft to TraderTraitor, a hacking group associated with the country's cyber operations. The same group has been linked to the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025, which the FBI also attributed to North Korean hackers.

North Korean attackers have targeted Binance itself before, including through attempts to infiltrate the exchange by posing as job applicants. In an interview with Decrypt in 2025, Binance Chief Security Officer Jimmy Su said North Korean operatives were contacting employees on LinkedIn every day, sometimes using fake interviews to deliver malware to their devices.

Binance has said it will cover all losses using company funds and its Secure Asset Fund for Users, an emergency reserve designed to protect customers in the event of a security breach. CEO Richard Teng said on Friday that the company had reserved more than $6.34 billion to cover the stolen assets, while approximately $3 billion had already been located or frozen with the help of industry partners.

The exchange initially suspected that a compromised laptop belonging to a DevOps engineer had provided the attackers with access to its systems. It has since identified additional points of breach and said it found no evidence that wallet private keys had been leaked. Withdrawals remain suspended while Binance reviews its security infrastructure, with the company targeting a resumption by Sunday.

#5093. Hackers hijack a dozen DeFi websites, all registered with Name.com, and steal about $138,000

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Blockaid said the sites were rigged with Step Drainer, a malware kit that tricks visitors into signing away their funds. Ten of the domains have since been suspended.

Hackers who took over a dozen DeFi websites late Friday and used them to empty visitors' crypto wallets targeted domains registered through the same company, Name.com, according to public registry records reviewed by The Block. The Block has traced about $138,000 in stolen funds so far using onchain data.

Name.com is listed as the registrar for all 12 hijacked sites that were identified by security firm Blockaid, and 10 of them were suspended at the registrar level at 8:32 a.m. ET on Saturday.

X user bluexxix was among the first to notice the shared registrar, posting at 11:51 p.m. ET on Friday that it "seems @namedotcom is compromised" as more affected sites surfaced. Pseudonymous developer banteg reached the same conclusion on Saturday morning, and X user punkquidity later spotted that 10 of the domains had been switched off within seconds of each other.

Each of the 10 suspended domains now carries a "client hold" status, which stops a domain from resolving. They include Pickle Finance, Origami Finance, FluxBeam, Echelon, StonkFun and StakePoint. Flap's and Kintsu's domains were not suspended and are still operational as of publishing time.

X user liquidated0x flagged that Flap's site had seemingly been compromised at 9:10 p.m. ET on Friday, about an hour before the token launchpad warned its users that its flap.sh frontend had been hit by "a DNS hijacking incident." Blockaid warned about the attack on Flap and liquid staking protocol Kintsu at 10:54 p.m. ET, naming the 10 additional hijacked sites soon after.

Name.com's only public response so far came at 12:27 a.m. ET, when it replied to liquidated0x and bluexxix on X, asking them to fill out its abuse form. Name.com’s listed press address did not accept The Block’s request for comment, and its parent company, Identity Digital, did not immediately respond.

Blockaid said the hijacked sites were rigged with Step Drainer, a malware toolkit that tricks visitors into approving transfers out of their wallets once they connect them to a site. Security firm LevelBlue SpiderLabs described Step Drainer in April as a "Malware-as-a-Service (MaaS)" kit, reportedly sold for about $750 for the full source code or $150 for a version that takes a 20% cut of each theft. The firm said the kit claims support for more than 20 blockchain networks.

Onchain data reviewed by The Block shows the attackers routing proceeds to three wallets in fixed 15%, 55% and 30% cuts. On BNB Chain, a Safe wallet flagged by liquidated0x received about 3.1 BNB, worth roughly $2,200, from four wallets between 9:00 and 9:42 p.m. ET on Friday. It split the first three deposits among the three wallets, and about 2.27 BNB was still sitting in the Safe on Saturday morning.

The same three wallets received identically split payments on Ethereum, Robinhood Chain, and Monad, the network Kintsu runs on. On Monad, two of the wallets redeemed Kintsu's staked MON token and withdrew from lending protocol Neverland before converting the proceeds into MON. On Robinhood Chain, one wallet pulled tokenized stocks including NVDA and AAPL out of Morpho and swapped them for ETH.

Together the three wallets held about $138,000 late Saturday morning, mostly MON and Robinhood Chain ETH, and almost all of it arrived after the attack started. That's only what The Block could trace on EVM chains. Three of the hijacked sites, FluxBeam, StonkFun and StakePoint, are built on Solana, and Echelon runs on Aptos, so any losses there would sit in other wallets and aren't counted.

The hijacked sites don't share a DNS provider, the service that tells internet browsers which server a domain points to. Some run on Cloudflare, some on Vercel and some on Name.com's own nameservers. Punkquidity said they couldn't yet tell "whether Name.com itself or a reseller like Vercel did it."

Kintsu and Origami Finance have both publicly blamed cloud infrastructure firm Vercel. "It was something that happened on Vercel's end that was out of our control," Kintsu wrote in a reply on X, adding that Vercel had not yet responded. Origami first warned of "an active attack on Vercel-associated Nameservers," and later said its vaults would stay paused while it waits for "root cause analysis from Vercel / name[.]com." Flap builder Cedric said shortly after midnight that the team was reaching out to both companies.

Vercel picked Name.com as the registrar for its rebuilt domain service last year, Name.com said in a December 2025 press release. Vercel had not commented publicly as of Saturday morning, and both companies' status pages listed all systems as operational. In April, Vercel disclosed a breach of its internal systems that it traced to a compromised third-party AI tool, The Block previously reported.

No affected project has reported a victim count as of yet, though onchain data shows at least four wallets sent funds to the attackers on BNB Chain alone. FluxBeam said it had "no reports of users being affected," and StonkFun, which moved its site to new infrastructure overnight, said it was "not aware of anyone affected." Kintsu, Origami, FluxBeam and StonkFun all said their onchain contracts were untouched.

Crypto front-ends have been hit by DNS hijacks before. Aerodrome and Velodrome were hijacked in November 2025, and Galxe was hit in October 2023, when attackers got into the project's account at registrar Dynadot.

Vercel did not immediately respond to a request for comment. Blockaid's PR agency said the firm was looking into The Block's questions.

The suspensions on the hijacked domains appear to have been lifted by late Saturday morning, registry records show. The domains resolve again and now carry locks that block transfers and, for most of them, other changes.

Name.com has allegedly told at least one affected project that the breach was not on its end. Cloakd, the developer behind FluxBeam, said on X that its representative at Name.com was "taking the stance that it’s not them but the 3rd party issuers."

Updated at 2:00 p.m. ET with details from the lifted suspension and Cloakd's post.

Update, Oct. 31, 3:37 p.m. ET: Name.com said 15 customer domains were affected, more than the 12 Blockaid identified.

"name.com identified unauthorized activity late Friday, October 30, that redirected a number of domains registered through the platform,” a Name.com spokesperson said in a statement to The Block. "name.com has restored all affected domains and is working directly with the name.com account holders. We’re continuing to investigate and have taken steps to secure the accounts involved."

Name.com also said on X that the domains "were improperly redirected late Friday as part of a plan to access visitors’ crypto wallets," and urged customers to check their DNS records and enable two-factor authentication. Vercel said in a security bulletin that 10 of the affected domains were associated with its platform, that Name.com has restored them and that it is assisting in the investigation.

#5092. Binance says 90% of stolen assets replenished, targets Sunday withdrawals

Less-liquid tokens make up the remaining shortfall as Binance continues buying replacement assets through market and over-the-counter trades.

Binance said Saturday it had replenished 90% of the assets stolen in its $6.3 billion hack, up from 80% a day earlier, and remained on track to resume withdrawals Sunday.

The exchange is using a combination of its corporate treasury, a partial deployment of its Secure Asset Fund for Users (SAFU) and a portion of its futures insurance fund, a spokesperson told Cointelegraph.

“Binance will absorb the financial impact of the stolen funds, not users,” the spokesperson said, adding that the insurance fund would remain above required risk management levels.

The update follows Thursday’s theft, the largest cryptocurrency hack on record, which TRM Labs has since attributed to North Korea’s TraderTraitor hacker group.

Following the hack, Binance said Friday it had set aside $6.34 billion to cover losses, including $5 billion in the affected cryptocurrencies. Industry partners like Tether and Circle froze about $3 billion in affected assets after the hack, but those funds are separate from Binance’s replacement of stolen tokens.

Still, CertiK told Cointelegraph on Friday that it needed more information to verify Binance’s claimed $5 billion in replacement cryptocurrencies. Binance has since published a new reserves snapshot, but says the full cryptographic proof is still being generated. “The full zero-knowledge proof takes approximately 40 to 50 hours to generate due to the computational workload,” the exchange’s update reads.

Binance said in its update on X that customer balances in Bitcoin (BTC), Ether (ETH) and eight other assets were fully backed. The remaining shortfall consists of less liquid tokens that it continues to buy through market and over-the-counter transactions, the update reads.

The spokesperson’s statement did not specify how much each funding source contributed or disclose the insurance fund’s minimum required balance. It also did not quantify how much stolen crypto had been recovered.

#5090. Binance sees $3.64 billion in net outflows after reopening withdrawals following record hack

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The exchange said the outflows equal 2.1% of its assets and that withdrawal volumes have eased after an initial spike. Binance said an updated proof-of-reserves report should be released Monday.

Binance recorded about $3.64 billion in net outflows in the first nine and a half hours after it began restoring withdrawals on Sunday, following Thursday's $6.3 billion hack, the exchange said.

Binance processed $4.65 billion in withdrawals and $1.01 billion in deposits between 1 a.m. and 10:30 a.m. ET, the world's largest crypto exchange said on X. It put the net outflow at 2.1% of its $171 billion in assets under management, based on its latest proof-of-reserves data from Oct. 28.

Withdrawal volumes have declined and are "stabilising as expected" after an initial spike, while deposit volumes "have remained substantial," according to Binance. The exchange began restoring withdrawals in phases at 1 a.m. ET and reopened all 15 networks and assets in its first phase by 7:36 a.m. ET.

Binance is still testing the remaining networks and assets, which include Cardano, Chainlink, Hyperliquid, Litecoin and NEAR, according to its status page. As of 11:22 a.m. ET, the page showed withdrawals suspended for 531 of the 552 coins listed, with 14 others, including bitcoin and ether, partly restored.

Binance saw $4.05 billion in net outflows over 24 hours as of 11:37 a.m. ET, according to DefiLlama, which tracks $131.1 billion in assets across the exchange's wallets. The next 10 largest exchanges by assets that DefiLlama tracks saw about $368 million in combined net inflows over the same period, led by OKX at $316.8 million.

Bitfinex and Gate posted net outflows of $106.3 million and $34.5 million, respectively. DefiLlama does not track Coinbase or Kraken, but bitcoin balances on the two exchanges rose by about 984 BTC and 1,422 BTC over 24 hours, while Binance's fell by about 19,289 BTC, according to CoinGlass.

Attackers drained about $6.3 billion from Binance's hot wallets on Thursday in the largest crypto hack on record, leading the exchange to temporarily suspend withdrawals. The FBI attributed the theft to North Korea's TraderTraitor hacking group on Saturday.

Binance now processes withdrawals from newly generated wallets added to its proof-of-reserves list. It said an updated reserves report, which will be based on an Oct. 31 snapshot and computed with zero-knowledge proofs, is projected to be released on Monday.

Binance co-CEO Richard Teng said on Saturday the exchange had replenished 90% of the stolen assets and would cover the rest. Binance said on Friday it had readied $6.34 billion to cover losses from the hack.

#5088. $8 Million Crypto Kidnapping Lands Texas Brothers 15-Year Prison Terms

$8 Million Crypto Kidnapping Lands Texas Brothers 15-Year Prison Terms

Two Texas brothers have been sentenced to a combined 326 months in prison for a violent home-invasion robbery in which they held a Minnesota family at gunpoint for hours and forced the father to hand over more than $8 million in cryptocurrency.

Raymond Christian Garcia and Isiah Angelo Garcia, both of Waller, Texas, were sentenced on Thursday to 180 months and 186 months respectively, or 15 and 15-and-a-half years, by U.S. District Judge Donovan Frank in St. Paul, according to the Justice Department. Both had pleaded guilty in June.

According to court documents, the brothers traveled from Texas to Grant, Minnesota, and on Sept. 19, 2025, broke into the victim’s home, zip-tied the family and held them at gunpoint for more than nine hours while demanding access to the father’s crypto accounts.

When he failed to transfer the amount they expected, they forced him to drive one of the brothers to the family’s cabin in upstate Minnesota to retrieve additional hardware wallets and move the funds. In total, they compelled him to transfer over $8 million. The pair fled after the victim’s son called 911, and were later arrested near Houston.

“The Garcia brothers committed a brazen and calculated act of violence against the victim and his family,” U.S. Attorney Daniel Rosen said in a statement. The FBI’s Christopher Dotson said the brothers “violated the sanctity of a home” in “a brazen act of violence and greed.”

The case is a stark example of a “wrench attack,” in which criminals use physical force rather than hacking to seize crypto.

Such attacks have surged alongside crypto’s rising prices: Chainalysis counted 46 violent incidents in the first half of 2026 with more than $30 million stolen, while CertiK pegged losses even higher. Recent cases span a French kidnapping in which workers’ children were held hostage and a UK home invasion that ended in prison terms for the attackers.

The sentencing offers one of the clearest signals yet that U.S. courts are treating crypto wrench attacks as serious violent crime.

#5085. North Korean hackers launder $242 million of Binance loot via Tron as FBI presses THORChain: analyst

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Relay and THORChain carried the largest shares of the stolen funds off Tron, while Symbiosis said its screening has "gaps." THORChain has not said whether it will block the addresses the FBI listed.

North Korean hackers have laundered about $242 million of the funds stolen from Binance on the Tron blockchain in two days, onchain analyst and Security Alliance (SEAL) contributor SomaXBT said on X on Sunday, as the FBI presses THORChain and other services to block the transactions.

The attackers took about $628 million from Binance on Tron and had roughly $249 million left to move after Tether froze 378.7 million USDT, SomaXBT said in his thread. Most of the $242 million, about $194 million, left Tron through bridges and swap services. Another $26 million stayed on the network, while about $22 million went to fees, slippage and price moves, according to the analyst.

The funds moved through a variety of crypto bridges: Relay handled $48 million of the outflows, THORChain $44 million, Chainflip $32.1 million and Symbiosis $16 million, together about $140 million, SomaXBT said. Bitget Wallet handled another $41.7 million, but where that money ended up is still unresolved, the analyst said.

Binance lost about $6.3 billion on Thursday in the largest crypto hack on record. The FBI attributed the theft to the TraderTraitor group on Saturday in an alert issued with the U.S. Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation.

The FBI said the hackers were converting stolen assets "primarily into Ethereum" and expects the funds to be further laundered through DeFi protocols and instant swap services. It urged THORChain's THORNode operators, alongside RPC node operators, instant exchanges, DeFi services and other providers, to block transactions tied to the theft. The agency also published a list of 608 Ethereum addresses, 19 bitcoin addresses and one Solana address, but no Tron addresses, that it said were linked to the attack.

A 2022 joint advisory from the FBI, the Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency and the Treasury Department describes TraderTraitor as a North Korean state-sponsored hacking group, active since at least 2020, that steals and launders crypto "to support the North Korean regime." The advisory says the cybersecurity industry tracks the group under various designations, including Lazarus Group, APT38 and BlueNoroff.

The FBI also blamed TraderTraitor for the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025. The agency's recent alert did not disclose the evidence that led it to its attribution, though blockchain analysis firms Chainalysis, Elliptic and TRM Labs had already linked the theft to North Korea through onchain ties to earlier North Korean hacks.

THORChain technical co-founder Chad Barraford wrote on X on Saturday evening that "THORNode operators have zero information that isn't already public and readily available in real time." He added, "No subpoenas required, a best case scenario for investigations," but his posts since the hack have not said whether node operators would block the listed addresses.

Earlier on Saturday, an Ethereum reorg caused THORChain to double-refund a 10 ETH deposit, which triggered an emergency pause, Chainalysis said. The pause, somewhat ironically, stopped all users, including the hackers, from swapping through most of THORChain's bridges, according to the firm.

THORChain trading remained halted on 10 of its 13 connected chains, including Tron, Ethereum and Bitcoin, as of 1:20 p.m. ET Sunday, according to THORChain node data. Trading from Tron has been halted since around midday Saturday.

The attackers have sent 185.1 million TRX and 3.83 million USDT into THORChain in 2,697 deposits, SomaXBT said. THORChain has paid out 17,019 ETH to 170 addresses and 53.25 BTC to 20 addresses.

The attackers lost about 12% to fees and slippage, and front-end interfaces earned about 145,800 RUNE, roughly $95,000, in affiliate fees. North Korea's Lazarus Group also used THORChain to move much of the ether stolen from Bybit's $1.5 billion hack onto bitcoin in February 2025.

Relay received 37.64 million USDT and 39.28 million TRX in 735 deposits, SomaXBT said, adding that the protocol "barely featured" in laundering from past TraderTraitor hacks. Onchain analyst Specter said on Saturday that the hackers bridged $38 million through Relay in seven hours, despite Relay's statement that it screens transaction addresses using third-party risk data, including from Chainalysis, and an internal blocklist.

One Ethereum address received 2,804 ETH and 1.02 million USDT from Relay's router and solvers in 97 payments over about 80 minutes on Saturday, Etherscan data shows, matching SomaXBT's figures. The wallet swapped the USDT for ether on CoW Protocol and sent about 3,257 ETH, roughly $7.4 million, to four new addresses. None of the five addresses is on the FBI's list.

On Sunday, the hackers separately began swapping 970,522 SOL, worth about $95.9 million, for bitcoin through Relay, Lookonchain reported.  The hackers' Solana wallet, the only Solana address on the FBI's list, moved 486,970 SOL ($46.4 million) in the hour before Lookonchain's 5:35 a.m. ET post. The Relay-powered swaps began after the FBI listed the wallet on Saturday. The wallet held about 466,700 SOL at publication time, the same balance as at 9:55 a.m. ET, per Solscan data.

Chainflip received 49.47 million USDT in 571 deposits, but many swaps failed, SomaXBT said. Failed swaps were refunded to fresh Tron wallets and sent back in, 17.91 million USDT in all. Chainflip planned an upgrade last year to block the Bybit hackers from its cross-chain DEX.

Symbiosis said early Sunday it had identified attempts to move Binance hack funds through its protocol and was in contact with Binance. "Our screening is built for normal flows, not for tracking fast-moving, sophisticated attempts so some mechanisms have gaps," Symbiosis said on X. "We're closing them as we go."

The attackers sent 49.71 million TRX into Symbiosis in 1,367 smaller deposits, SomaXBT said. Symbiosis recovered 15 BTC after an exploit of its own bitcoin bridge in September.

The hackers' main Tron distribution wallet paid out every hour for 31 straight hours and never paused for longer than 46 minutes, blockchain analytics firm Elliptic said on Saturday. The same swap amounts kept recurring in THORChain deposits, one of them 19 times.

When a bridge rejected deposits, new wallets automatically re-sent the funds to the same destination, Elliptic said. In the Bybit case, laundering followed human working patterns, according to the firm.

Elliptic co-founder Tom Robinson said in comments shared with The Block that the activity does not appear to involve AI agents. "The pattern is repetitive and static: the same sizes, the same rhythm, the same routes, hour after hour," Robinson said. He added that test transactions and round-number top-ups point to "operators making decisions, with scripts doing the repetitive work."

Elliptic has verified $2.85 billion of the stolen funds as frozen or seized, about 44% of the $6.43 billion it counts as stolen, and 94% of that is stablecoins. No funds have been frozen on Bitcoin, the XRP Ledger, Solana or NEAR. About $3.25 billion, including 18,327 BTC and 233 million XRP, is held in assets that no issuer can freeze, the firm said.

Tether's frozen USDT is split across two attacker wallets, with 312.7 million in one and 65.99 million in the other, per Tronscan data. Mempool.space data checked by The Block shows all 18,270 BTC still sitting in the 19 bitcoin addresses on the FBI's list.

Chainalysis said on Saturday the hackers have been depositing stablecoins into DeFi protocols on Tron for depository receipt tokens, which cannot be frozen, and later redeeming them from new addresses. It did not name the protocol, which other onchain analysts identified as JustLend, and did not address a question from The Block about whether it was JustLend.

TRM Labs said 300 million USDT moved to a Tron wallet that "began supplying it to the JustLend lending protocol." Tronscan data shows that wallet put 10,000 USDT into JustLend, swapped about 234 million USDT for TRX on decentralized exchanges and sent 65.99 million USDT to one of the two frozen wallets.

The Block has reached out to Relay, Barraford, Chainflip, and Bitget Wallet for comment.

#5084. Binance Hack: Why Bitcoin Didn't Crash Like It Did in 2018

Binance Hack: Why Bitcoin Didn't Crash Like It Did in 2018

Bitcoin is down about 4% a day after the biggest crypto hack on record. In March 2018, a failed attack on the same exchange knocked nearly 11% off its price in minutes, and Binance said at the time that no funds had been stolen.

Binance has confirmed yesterday's attack, and Chainalysis and TRM Labs put the theft at roughly $6.4 billion, more than four times the previous record hack: the $1.5 billion Bybit heist of February 2025. Bitcoin dipped to about $76,400 on Friday, around 6% below where it traded after Wednesday’s Federal Reserve decision.

More than $1.3 billion in leveraged bets was liquidated, meaning trades placed with borrowed money that exchanges close automatically when prices move against them. And yet Bitcoin is holding relatively steady, all things considered.

The 2018 incident was a phishing and theft attempt: attackers took over accounts, created API keys (credentials that let trading bots act on an account) and used them to pump the obscure coin Viacoin while 31 accounts that had pre-deposited the coin sold it. Bitcoin fell from $10,740 to $9,690 within minutes and kept dropping to about $6,600 by April 5, though the market was already in a bear run after peaking near $20,000 in December 2017.

To have a grasp of the situation, the entire crypto market was worth just over $400 billion in March 2018 and is worth roughly $2.6 trillion now.

A big reason for that, and why panic doesn't hit markets the same way anymore, is U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs. Funds like BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust let people buy Bitcoin through an ordinary brokerage account, and they now hold roughly 1.3 million BTC, about 6% of the supply.

Their shareholders don’t keep coins on Binance, and sovereign wealth funds such as Mubadala and the Abu Dhabi Investment Council have been buying. BlackRock’s fund alone took in $136 million on Wednesday, per Farside Investors data.

These funds don’t tend to panic sell so easily, and those funds remain locked for long periods of time.

The theft began at 4:05 p.m. ET Thursday, after U.S. markets closed, so Thursday’s preliminary $17 million of ETF inflows predate it and Friday’s flow figures are the first real read on whether those holders are selling.

The attacker still holds more than 420,000 ETH and 18,270 BTC, about $2.4 billion that no stablecoin issuer can freeze. The amount doesn’t account for other tokens, which would increase the holdings to more than $3 billion.

TRM and Chainalysis tie the attack to the North Korean group behind Bybit, which after that heist converted about 84% of the stolen Ethereum into Bitcoin through Thorchain, a cross-chain swap network.

Binance says it has set aside more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover every loss, and it is buying the missing tokens on the open market and over the counter, meaning in private deals off the exchange. About $3 billion is frozen, deposits are operating normally, and withdrawals are on track to resume in 45 hours.

Market sentiment was already soft before the theft and slid further: CoinMarketCap’s Fear and Greed index, a 0-to-100 gauge of market mood, dropped to 36 points from 43 (now in “fear” territory after being “neutral” yesterday), and Bitcoin sits about 38% below its $126,080 record. Back in 2018, the market was well in “extreme fear” territory at 12 points on the scale when the incident happened. Traders were already in panic mode, and the attack just worsened an already ugly situation.

Glassnode data show long-term holders underwater on about 4 million coins bought between $85,000 and $118,000, and HashKey analyst Tim Sun said before the hack that the market’s problem “is not panic selling, but rather insufficient new buying interest.”

So, taken all together, what it all boils down to is this: A more optimistic market, much more liquidity, active long-term investors, a clearer regulatory framework, a sense of safety thanks to that regulatory clarity, quick action by stablecoin providers, and a price trend in recovery likely all played a role in keeping Bitcoin safe from a global panic despite the record-breaking hack.

#5081. Spot bitcoin ETFs see largest daily outflows in four months following record Binance hack

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

US spot bitcoin ETFs shed $643 million on Friday, marking their largest single-day outflow since June 25.

U.S. spot bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) exchange-traded funds recorded their largest daily net outflows in four months on Friday, following Thursday's $6.3 billion hack at Binance.

According to SoSoValue data, spot bitcoin ETFs saw $643.3 million in net outflows on Friday, marking their largest single-day outflow since June 25. Their outflows for the week ending Oct. 30 amounted to $202.6 million.

Spot Ethereum Ethereum (ETH) ETFs saw $155.9 million in net outflows on Friday, their largest since Oct. 7. The ether funds reported $431.5 million in weekly outflows.

The ETF outflows came after Binance was hit by the largest crypto hack to date. Attackers, whom the FBI has identified as North Korean hackers, drained about $6.3 billion from Binance's hot wallets on Thursday.

The crypto exchange said it recorded roughly $3.64 billion in net outflows in the first nine and a half hours after it began restoring withdrawals on Sunday.

Bitcoin rose 1% over the past 24 hours to trade at $78,491 as of 11:20 p.m. ET Sunday, while ether climbed 1.3% to $2,296, according to The Block's crypto price page.

Jeff Ko, chief analyst of ViaBTC, told The Block that he views the ETF outflows as more of a "sentiment shock."

"Spot ETF holders have no direct exposure to Binance, since the underlying BTC is held with regulated custodians. And in context, a roughly $640 million outflow is actually modest against the total ETF assets," said Ko.

Rick Maeda, head of markets at Laevitas, shared similar views, noting that Friday's outflow was relatively small compared with the ETFs' total net assets of $98.14 billion.

"In scale, the $643 million outflow is still under 1% of spot BTC ETF assets so this was simply a de-risking day, and not capitulation or a loss in institutional confidence," said Maeda.

Still, analysts expect investor caution to persist in the near term.

"Taking a look at a similar precedent of Bybit's Q1 2025 hack, I would expect stabilization or modest outflows in the near term rather than a snap-back as long as the hack can be contained at its current situation," Maeda added.

Dominick John, analyst of Zeus Research, also said that the near-term demand is likely to remain cautious, "with a sustained recovery in ETF inflows dependent on improved market stability, restored liquidity and stronger investor confidence."

The Crypto Fear and Greed Index stood at 37 on Sunday in the fear territory.

"The current sentiment in crypto seems to be one of caution rather than fear," said Maeda. "Markets will be headline-sensitive as traders look for updates regarding the hack as well as the macro calendar such as the U.S. midterms."

Ko also pointed to the midterm elections as another key event.

"BTC has absorbed the hack quite resiliently," said Ko. "Attention is now shifting toward U.S. midterm elections, where the balance of power in Congress could have meaningful implications for how crypto market structure legislation moves forward."

#5079. FBI blames North Korea for record $6.3B Binance hack

US agency seeks private-sector help to block further money-laundering of stolen digital assets.

The FBI said that the Democratic People’s Republic of Korea (North Korea) was responsible for the theft of about $6.3 billion in digital assets from cryptocurrency exchange Binance on Thursday.

The FBI said on Saturday in a news release that it refers to this specific North Korean malicious cyber activity as “TraderTraitor,” which it has most recently observed converting stolen assets mostly into Ether (ETH).

It expects these assets to be further converted, using DeFi and instant swap services, as the hackers move to more advanced laundering stages.

The FBI urged private sector entities including THORNode operators, RPC node operators, instant exchanges, DeFi services, blockchain analytics firms and other digital asset service providers to block transactions with or derived from addresses TraderTraitor actors are using to launder these stolen assets. A full list of addresses is provided on the news release.

Cointelegraph reported earlier this year that North Korea-linked hackers stole about 60% of the value lost to crypto hacks in 2025, with proceeds used to help fund the regime’s nuclear and ballistic missile programs, highlighting a growing reliance on digital assets to generate hard currency.

Related: Crypto losses from North Korea hackers in 2025 rose 51% YoY: Report

Those findings came from a CertiK report that attributed more than $2 billion of an estimated $3.4 billion in 2025 crypto security losses to groups tied North Korea, across 79 of 656 incidents documented that year.

While North Korea does not publish credible budget data, according to independent research organization MilitarySpend, mid-range consensus places its actual annual defense spending at as much as $6 billion, using purchasing-power-adjusted GDP estimates, though this figure is uncertain.

MilitarySpend adds that revenue from Russia arms transfers — estimated at as much as $5 billion in munitions exported to Russia from 2022-2025 per South Korean intelligence — supplements domestic weapons programs, as does cryptocurrency theft, which provides additional off-budget financing for WMD development.

As Cointelegraph reported, between 2016 and early 2026, North Korea-linked actors stole an estimated $6.75 billion in cryptocurrency across 263 documented incidents, according to CertiK, citing findings by independent onchain researcher Taylor Monahan.

CertiK’s analysis concluded that North Korea has industrialized crypto theft into a core state revenue mechanism.

#5078. Reddit Stock Plunges 30% as Google's AI Search Hits Traffic and User Growth

Reddit Stock Plunges 30% as Google's AI Search Hits Traffic and User Growth

Reddit shares plunged 30.44% to $90.15 on Friday after CEO Steve Huffman warned that Google's AI-powered search features are cutting referral traffic and could shrink the platform's user base.

The selloff came after the company reported strong third-quarter results but issued a weaker-than-expected revenue forecast for the current quarter.

Reddit built much of its business around people discovering its discussions through Google searches, and those visitors are increasingly finding answers without ever clicking through to the platform. That puts pressure on the advertising business that has helped turn Reddit into one of the internet's fastest-growing publicly traded companies.

Reddit's third-quarter numbers initially looked healthy. Revenue climbed 53% year over year to $895 million, advertising revenue increased 54% to $847 million, and net income reached $283 million. Diluted earnings per share, which measure company profit attributable to each share while accounting for potential dilution, came in at $1.40, up 75% from a year earlier and above the $1.33 analyst consensus.

But the audience figures were less encouraging since Reddit reported 130.3 million daily active unique visitors, up 12% year over year but below the roughly 134 million analysts expected. In the U.S., the number actually fell 2% to 50.7 million, giving investors another reason to question whether the platform can maintain its growth.

Huffman delivered the warning during Thursday's earnings call, explaining that Google search referrals had fallen by more than one million on average every week during October, with the decline accelerating toward the end of the third quarter. If that pace continues, Reddit's total daily active users could fall by a low double-digit percentage in Q4 from the current base of around 130 million.

Huffman pointed directly at Google's AI Overviews and AI Mode, features that generate answers and summaries directly inside search results. Rather than sending users to websites to find information, Google increasingly gives them what they are looking for on its own page, pushing traditional search links further down.

Asked whether the changes were responsible for Reddit's declining search traffic, Huffman said the AI features had expanded aggressively in the U.S. and internationally. "The AI mode does not drive traffic, not for us and not to anybody as far as I know," he told investors.

That creates an awkward situation for Reddit—and arguably every other single media site on the internet. People can still search for Reddit discussions, but Google's results increasingly allow them to consume information from those discussions without visiting Reddit itself. Search referrals also bring back existing users, meaning fewer clicks can affect both new-user acquisition and the platform's ability to keep people engaged.

Reddit is trying to reduce that dependence by moving toward a primarily logged-in experience, which would require people to sign into an account before accessing most of the platform. The company plans to begin testing the change in selected markets outside the U.S. during the fourth quarter.

The strategy also connects to Reddit's increasingly important relationship with the AI industry. In February 2024, Reddit signed a reported $60 million-a-year data licensing deal with Google, giving the search giant access to Reddit discussions to train AI models. A few months later, the company announced a similar partnership with OpenAI, allowing the ChatGPT developer to use Reddit content while giving Reddit access to OpenAI's technology.

Those deals turned Reddit's enormous archive of human conversations into something it could sell to AI developers. But the same AI-powered search experience that benefits from Reddit's content can reduce the number of people who actually visit the site. Reddit also started tightening access to its public data and moving toward a more restricted platform which angered long term users.

The stock has now lost 44% over the past week, with Friday's 30.44% plunge accounting for a significant portion of the decline. At $90.15, Reddit is trading well below the levels it held just days ago.

The technical picture offers little immediate relief. Reddit's 50-day exponential moving average, a trend indicator that gives greater weight to recent prices, sits around $149.80, while its 200-day average is near $162.12. The 50-day average has already fallen below the 200-day average, creating a so-called death cross, a bearish signal commonly associated with a longer-term downward trend.

At $90.15, Reddit would need to climb approximately 66%—almost 70%—just to return to its 50-day average. That would only bring the share price back to the shorter-term trend line, not undo the death cross itself. With both averages sitting far above the current price, a recovery to those levels in the near term looks increasingly unlikely.

#5075. ‘We found room for even more orange’: Michael Saylor’s Strategy scoops up another 811 bitcoin for $65.5 million

#5073. Worried About Crypto Hacks? Buy Bitcoin Proxies in Stock Market Instead, Say Wall Street Firms

Worried About Crypto Hacks? Buy Bitcoin Proxies in Stock Market Instead, Say Wall Street Firms

Wall Street analysts are telling clients the record-breaking hack of Binance is a buying opportunity, not a reason to flee, urging investors to scoop up crypto-linked stocks while prices are depressed.

Analysts at Cantor Fitzgerald, TD Cowen, and Compass Point said in Friday research notes that the roughly $6.3 billion theft, the largest crypto hack ever, is unlikely to derail the broader market’s recovery.

The reassurance came a day after the exploit rattled markets and sent Bitcoin sliding toward $77,000.

Cantor’s Ramsey El-Assal pointed to how fast the industry moved to contain the damage, noting that stablecoin issuers and others froze roughly $3 billion of the stolen funds, with Tether freezing more than $1.5 billion, Circle about $800 million and Ripple around $200 million.

El-Assal expects shares of Coinbase, Robinhood, and Strategy to recover once the panic fades. “We would be buyers on weakness,” he wrote, adding that while the episode is painful, “this is not 2022.”

TD Cowen’s Lance Vitanza struck a similar note, arguing the breach exposed weaknesses in Binance’s own security rather than in the underlying technology behind Bitcoin, and framing the selloff as a chance to add to Bitcoin and the stocks of public companies that hold it.

Compass Point’s Ed Engel and Michael Donovan said Binance has “more than enough liquidity to cover the hole,” while flagging that reimbursing customers could strain stablecoin supply and that rivals like Coinbase and Hyperliquid could pick up business. They are watching the $75,000 to $77,000 Bitcoin range as a key level.

The logic behind buying stocks over tokens is that regulated, exchange-listed proxies like Coinbase, Robinhood, and Strategy offer crypto exposure without the custody risk the hack laid bare, and tend to rebound once sentiment steadies.

Not everyone is sanguine. Cristiano Ventricelli, vice president of digital assets at Moody’s Ratings, said the incident “underscores the heightened cybersecurity risks associated with hot wallets” and “illustrates the risk concentration inherent in large, centralized intermediaries.”

He cautioned that the broader implications will hinge on “the ultimate losses, affected counterparties, asset recovery, and importantly, whether the incident leads to wider disruption or loss of confidence.”

Binance has set aside $6.34 billion to cover losses and told users their assets are safe, while the Secret Service and FBI have opened a probe and firms including TRM and Chainalysis have tied the hack to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group.

#5071. This Woman Lifted Weights 5 Times a Week for 4 Weeks—Here’s How Her Body Changed

Makari Espe swapped two 20-min weights sessions a week for five 30-min sessions—and documented the results.

If ever you’ve wondered exactly what upping the amount of weight training you do could do for your body, let Canadian YouTuber Makari Espe’s story give you an idea. Already accustomed to 20-minute strength sessions twice a week, she committed to five 30-minute workouts every week for four weeks. The aim? To become “harder, better, faster, and stronger.”

To put her results to the test, she recorded her strength before and after the challenge, from how long she could hold a plank to how many chin-ups and push-ups she could do. Along the way, she also documented the reality of training five days a week on her YouTube channel. By the end of those 28 days, she wasn’t just feeling stronger—she could perform more reps, hold exercises for longer, and lift heavier weights. Here’s what happened.

“My current weightlifting routine is 20 minutes a day, two times per week, but I want to be harder, better, faster, and stronger. So, for the next month, I'm going to lift weights five times a week for 30 minutes.”

“Basically, the idea is to do two big muscle groups per workout so that each muscle group is rested by the time you work it out again and we're not all out here hating our lives. For example, it’s 7:00 in the morning and today we're going to be doing back and biceps. Tomorrow I'll be doing legs and shoulders, and then day three is a rest day.”

“I didn't come up with this routine by myself, by the way. I'm not a personal trainer and I'm very lazy when it comes to planning my own workouts. I wish this was a sponsor, but it's not. I'm just here to tell you that I'm getting all of my workouts from the Peloton app. Woo! This week I'll be doing the beginner 5-day program, and for the next 3 weeks I'll be doing these intermediate programs.”

Espe performed a baseline strength test at the start of her 28-day challenge and the same test at the end of the 28 days. She recorded the following results:

Before:1:30 plank / 1-minute wall sit / 2 chin-ups / 10 push-ups / 25 × 10-lb shoulder presses.

After four weeks:2-minute plank / 2-minute wall sit / 4 chin-ups / 15 push-ups / 25 × 15-lb shoulder presses.

As Espe’s body got used to the change in training frequency, she noticed her soreness increased. That post-workout pain has a name: delayed-onset muscle soreness (DOMS), the stiffness and tenderness that can kick in a day or two after a challenging or unfamiliar session as your muscles repair and adapt.

By week two, she said, “My triceps are killing me more so than any other muscle group this whole challenge so far, my body is sore in many places and I'm very tired.” She added that her hamstrings were “so sore” and her “legs hurt so bad.” By week three, she said, “The place where it hurts right now is my shoulder blades; it feels like somebody hit me with a hammer.”

The DOMS might have intensified, but she did work out an effective salve: active recovery. For Espe, that meant gentle cycling, swimming, and stretching, which helped increase blood flow to the sore areas and to ease the stiffness without adding to her training load.

She said: “It's 7:30 a.m. and I just went for a bike ride and now I'm going to go for a swim. I've been wanting to do that every day this week and I finally did it, and it was glorious. Best morning ever. And now I'm going to go stretch. The swim was amazing for many reasons, but the main one was it was so nice on my sore muscles.”

She didn’t set out with aesthetic goals, and it was her performance that really improved. On week three, she said: “I've crossed the threshold and I’ve found the joy in weightlifting again. I'm doing it because I know I can and I'm super strong. I just feel really strong and that's awesome.” A week later, by week four, she added, “I actually feel even stronger than I did as a teenager.”

As her fatigue increased in those initial weeks, by week three, she decided to take stock of what she was putting into her body—and her energy soared: “I'm putting so much more good fuel into my body which is giving me more energy to perform in the gym,” she said.

As the challenge came to an end, she noticed that her mindset had improved as well as her physical strength. “This is going to sound really cheesy, but it's because not only do I feel physically stronger, but I feel stronger in my brain. Like, I feel like I'm more resilient for having been able to push through all of the self-doubt,” she said.

“I will say that roughly 3 and 1/2 hours every week is a lot of free time to be able to put towards working out, but ultimately it makes me feel so much better outside of the gym, and that's kind of why I do all this.”

“So if you decide to do a challenge like this, absolutely take pride in your physical accomplishments, but please be sure to notice the benefits outside of the gym too.”

As Women’s Health UK’s fitness director and a qualified Pilates and yoga instructor, Bridie Wilkins has been passionately reporting on exercise, health and nutrition since the start of her decade-long career in journalism.

After earning a first-class degree in journalism and NCTJ accreditation, she secured her first role at Look Magazine, where she launched the magazine’s health and fitness column, Look Fit, before going on to become Health and Fitness writer at HELLO!

Since, she has written for Stylist, Glamour, Cosmopolitan, Marie Claire, Elle, The Metro, Runner’s World and Red. Today, she oversees all fitness content across Women's Health online and in print, spearheading leading cross-platform franchises, such as ‘Fit At Any Age’, which showcases the women proving that age is no barrier to exercise.

She has also represented the brand on BBC Radio London, plus various podcasts and Substacks – all with the aim to encourage more women to exercise and show them how. Outside of work, find her trying the latest Pilates studio, testing her VO2 max for fun (TY, Oura), or posting workouts on Instagram.

The company reported $14.5 billion in total crypto, cash, and, marketable securities as of Nov. 1.

Bitmine Immersion Technologies increased its Ethereum treasury to 6,060,506 ETH after acquiring 8,573 ETH over the past week, the company said in a statement on Monday.

At $2,261 per ETH, the holdings were worth approximately $13.7 billion as of Nov. The haul represents 4.9% of ether's total supply, according to the statement.

Of the total, Bitmine has staked 5,227,037 ETH worth $11.8 billion, representing approximately 86% of its position. The company projects $358 million in annualized staking revenue at current levels, rising to $415 million if its ETH were fully staked.

He said ETH had declined approximately 4% since the incident was disclosed, compared the decline with an 8% drop on the day the $1.5 billion Bybit theft was announced in February 2025. ETH subsequently fell 20% within a week of the Bybit incident.

"While this is a terrible development, in my view, the relatively muted crypto market reaction is very constructive, particularly compared to the market's response to prior sizable crypto thefts," Lee said.

Lee cited six factors for the muted price reaction, including Binance's ability to cover losses, the absence of decentralized finance protocol failures, and swift industry intervention that limited losses by 50%.

ETH traded at $2,281.41 on Monday, up 0.87% over 24 hours, according to The Block's price page.

Bitmine shares closed Friday at $20.18, down 6.83%.

#5069. Intel Stock Suffers Worst Crash in 50 Years as Weak Forecast Overshadows AI Chip Demand

Intel Stock Suffers Worst Crash in 50 Years as Weak Forecast Overshadows AI Chip Demand

Intel shares plunged nearly 30% to $65.76 on Friday after the chipmaker issued a fourth-quarter forecast that fell well short of Wall Street expectations, overshadowing an otherwise strong earnings report.

The selloff wiped out more than $146 billion in market value, and translated into the worst single-day decline in more than 50 years.

The problem is that Intel finds itself in an unusual position: demand for its processors is booming because of the AI infrastructure buildout, but the company cannot manufacture enough chips to capitalize on it. At the same time, its traditional PC business is struggling, and the factories needed to support its long-term recovery are consuming enormous amounts of money.

Intel's third-quarter numbers were actually quite good. Revenue climbed 21% year over year to $16.5 billion, while adjusted earnings per share reached $0.52, beating the company's guidance of $0.38 and Wall Street's $0.40 estimate. It was the company's eighth consecutive quarter of exceeding its financial expectations, according to CFO Dave Zinsner.

The strongest performer was Intel's Data Center and AI division, which generated $7.3 billion in revenue, up 78% year over year and nearly 80% compared with the same period last year. The division's operating profit reached $3 billion, representing 42% of its revenue.

But investors were looking at what comes next. Intel expects fourth-quarter revenue between $15 billion and $16 billion, meaning its midpoint guidance of $15.5 billion is roughly $1.6 billion below the $17.07 billion analysts had anticipated. Adjusted earnings per share are expected to fall to $0.25, compared with Wall Street's $0.43 estimate.

Gross margins, which measure how much revenue remains after accounting for the direct costs of making products, are also expected to deteriorate. Intel forecast a non-GAAP gross margin of 38% for Q4, down from 44.1% in the third quarter.

Three problems explain much of that deterioration: weaker PC demand, manufacturing constraints affecting server processors, and an accident at one of Intel's factories in Israel.

Intel CFO Dave Zinsner described 2027 as an investment year and warned investors not to expect positive free cash flow. Free cash flow is the money a company has left after paying operating expenses and investments in equipment and infrastructure. Intel expects to sacrifice cash generation in the short term to finance its manufacturing expansion.

"Probably not a dynamic where we are going to have positive free cash flow next year," Zinsner said during the earnings call. He added that Intel expects a return on invested capital of at least 20% on its manufacturing node investments, but acknowledged that several major projects will require substantial spending at the same time.

That spending is part of Intel's broader attempt to rebuild its manufacturing business and compete with Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, or TSMC, the world's leading contract chipmaker. Intel wants to manufacture chips not only for its own products but also for outside customers, a business model known as a foundry.

The turnaround has attracted major partners. In September 2025, Nvidia invested $5 billion in Intel and agreed to collaborate on custom processors for PCs and data centers. The partnership gave Intel a financial boost while connecting its processors to Nvidia's AI ecosystem.

Intel's next-generation 18A process, the manufacturing technology behind chips such as Panther Lake, is central to the company's attempt to regain its technological edge.

More recently, a preliminary agreement with Apple to manufacture some of its chips helped send Intel shares above $130 in May. The potential deal could give Intel a significant new customer as Apple looks to diversify its manufacturing supply chain.

Those developments helped fuel optimism around Intel's recovery, but Friday's selloff shows how much still needs to go right. The company has strong demand in its data center business, yet it must increase manufacturing output, navigate a shrinking PC market and finance an expensive expansion before investors can expect consistent cash generation.
