#5034. FBI Blames North Korea for $6B Binance Hack as Exchange Reopens Withdrawals

FBI Blames North Korea for $6B Binance Hack as Exchange Reopens Withdrawals

The FBI has confirmed that North Korea was behind last week's theft of more than $6 billion from Binance, as the exchange began reopening withdrawals and reported billions of dollars leaving the platform.

In an alert published on Saturday, the FBI said the attack was carried out by North Korean hackers it refers to as "TraderTraitor," confirming the attribution blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis made on Friday.

The FBI is releasing information advising the Democratic People's Republic of Korea (North Korea) was responsible for the theft of more than $6 billion USD in virtual assets from cryptocurrency exchange, Binance, on or about October 29, 2026. The FBI refers to this specific North… pic.twitter.com/TBUvUFln4n

— FBI Cyber Branch (@FBICyberBranch) October 31, 2026

The FBI, together with the Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation, has seen the hackers converting stolen assets mainly into Ethereum, and expects them to launder the funds further through DeFi and instant swap services, the bureau said. It urged node operators, instant exchanges, DeFi services, blockchain analytics firms and other crypto businesses to block transactions involving hundreds of Ethereum addresses, 19 Bitcoin addresses and one Solana address it linked to the theft.

The Secret Service, which is also investigating the theft, said on Sunday that its investigators worked with industry partners to identify and freeze "a significant portion" of the stolen crypto within minutes of the attack. That work continues with the FBI, IRS Criminal Investigation, the U.S. Attorney's Offices for Washington, D.C. and the Central District of California, and the Justice Department's computer crime and national security divisions, the agency said.

The laundering is already well underway. After the attack, the hackers moved more than $500 million on Tron through more than 1,000 addresses, over 3,000 deposits and several Tron-based DeFi protocols, blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis said on Sunday. Most of those staging wallets have now been drained, with funds leaving Tron through cross-chain services including Chainflip, Relay, THORChain and NEAR for dozens of fresh wallets on Ethereum, Bitcoin and other chains, and the launderers have started moving onto Solana, the firm said.

Binance began restoring withdrawals in phases at 1 a.m. ET on Sunday, and all 15 networks and assets in its first phase were available by 7:36 a.m. ET. In the first nine and a half hours, it processed $4.649 billion in withdrawals and $1.01 billion in deposits, a net outflow of about $3.64 billion, the exchange said.

That represents 2.1% of the $171 billion in assets Binance reported in its most recent proof of reserves, from October 28, according to the exchange. "After an initial spike, withdrawal volumes have declined and are stabilising as expected," Binance said. It took a new reserves snapshot on October 31 and expects to publish an updated report on Monday.

As withdrawals began, CEO Richard Teng said Binance had replenished approximately 95% of the affected assets. "Binance will absorb the financial impact of the stolen funds, not our users," he wrote.

Today, we're beginning the phased resumption of withdrawals, taking a careful approach to ensure services return safely and securely.

We've now replenished approximately 95% of the impacted assets, and our commitment remains clear: Binance will absorb the financial impact of the… https://t.co/wKvI7Q4fsT pic.twitter.com/uWFGs3sJJ5

— Richard Teng (@_RichardTeng) November 1, 2026

Binance has since reopened withdrawals on more networks, including Zcash, TON and Sui, and on Monday restored fiat withdrawals in U.S. dollars, UAE dirhams, Vietnamese dong, Philippine pesos and Indonesian rupiah.

"Our top priority right now is to ensure the environment is secure, after which users can withdraw their funds and Binance can resume operations," Binance co-CEO Yi He said on X on Monday, Reuters reported. Binance said it has raised $6 billion to cover the losses from its corporate treasury, an insurance fund and its SAFU emergency fund, according to Reuters, which also reported that Circle had frozen more than $1.1 billion in USDC.

Data from DefiLlama, which tracks the public wallets of exchanges, put Binance's total assets at about $127.6 billion on Monday. More than $6 billion left wallets associated with Binance over 24 hours after withdrawals reopened, CoinDesk reported, citing DefiLlama, though those movements can include transfers between Binance's own wallets. Rival exchanges OKX and Bybit recorded net inflows of about $450 million and $134 million, respectively, during Binance's reopening.

The hack has also rattled institutional investors. Spot Bitcoin ETFs shed $643.2 million on Friday, the first full trading session after the hack, leaving the funds with roughly $516 million in net outflows for October. "A hack at a crypto exchange is bank robbery at the speed of the internet," TRM Labs' Ari Redbord told Reuters.

#5032. Bitcoin ETFs sink into October outflows with $643M exit after Binance hack

US spot Bitcoin ETFs ended October with $515 million in net withdrawals, while Ether funds posted a record $1.61 billion in monthly outflows.

Investors pulled $643 million from US-listed spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) on Friday, pushing October’s net flows into negative territory as crypto markets faced volatility following Binance’s $6.3 billion hack.

Friday’s withdrawals snapped four consecutive days of inflows, leaving the funds with $515 million in net outflows for October, their first negative month since June, according to SoSoValue. The funds still attracted approximately $361 million in net inflows for the year through October.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) led Friday’s outflows with $234 million, followed by Fidelity’s Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC) with $201 million, according to Farside Investors data.

The withdrawals followed Bitcoin’s drop to $77,000 on Thursday. Binance, the world’s largest crypto exchange by trading volume, confirmed Friday that hackers had stolen about $6.3 billion in crypto assets.

Hardware wallet maker OneKey’s founder and CEO, Yishi, warned on X Friday that IBIT could face similar security threats because it relies heavily on Coinbase to hold its Bitcoin.

Investors also withdrew a net $156 million from US spot Ether (ETH) ETFs on Friday, bringing October’s net outflows to a record $1.61 billion. The previous monthly high was $1.42 billion in November 2025.

October’s withdrawals also erased the Ether funds’ net inflows for 2026, leaving them with roughly $45 million in net outflows for the year.

#5031. These Are The 7 Best Fruits to Lower Blood Pressure, According to Experts

Meet the top picks and what experts have to say about each one.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

High blood pressure can creep up on you, leading to symptoms like chest pain, headaches, and dizziness. And since the condition is linked to more serious health complications like heart attack and stroke, it’s important to stay on top of your numbers.

Blood pressure management is complex, and usually involves making a slew of lifestyle changes, including adjusting your exercise routine, tweaking your diet, and even taking medication. But when it comes to those dietary adjustments, consuming fewer packaged and ultra-processed foods and “replacing them with whole foods and antioxidant-rich foods like fruits can be helpful,” says Jessica Cording, RD, author of The Little Book of Game-Changers.

Meet the experts: Scott Keatley, RD, is co-owner of Keatley Medical Nutrition Therapy. Jessica Cording, RD, is the author of The Little Book of Game-Changers. Keri Gans, RDN, is a dietitian and host of The Keri Report podcast.

There's some research to back up these claims too: A 2022 study in the Journal of Clinical Hypertension monitored their blood pressure at home in over 2,200 participants ages 50 to 65. They self-reported how often they ate bananas, apples, pears, and citrus fruit (response options were: not every week, once or twice a week, three to four times per week, and at least five times per week), and those who ate those fruits more frequently had significantly lower systolic home blood pressure.

Ahead, dietitians break down the best fruits that may help lower your blood pressure to regularly add to your plate.

The big draw with bananas is their potassium content; one medium-sized banana delivers about 375 milligrams of potassium, per the USDA, which helps you meet your daily goal of 2,600 milligrams of the nutrient, according to the NIH.

Why does this matter? Potassium “balances sodium in the body and relaxes blood vessel walls, directly lowering [blood] pressure,” explains Scott Keatley, RD, co-owner of Keatley Medical Nutrition Therapy. Having some sodium in the body is important, but too much will cause your body to hold onto water, according to Harvard Health. When that happens, the amount of fluid in your blood vessels increases and raises blood pressure along with it.

Blueberries contain anthocyanins, which are plant compounds that improve blood vessel flexibility and support nitric oxide production, says Keri Gans, RDN, a dietitian and host of The Keri Report podcast. Nitric oxide helps relax the smooth muscles in the blood vessels, helping them widen so blood can flow more freely, decreasing blood pressure, she explains.

This green fruit is loaded with vitamin C—59 milligrams, to be exact. (That's over half the daily recommended goal for women, which is 75 milligrams, per Mayo Clinic.) Vitamin C “enhances nitric oxide availability," Keatley says. Again, nitric oxide helps to relax the smooth muscles in your blood vessels, lowering blood pressure along with it. It also helps to relax the blood vessels, making it easier for blood to flow through and lowering blood pressure as a result.

Like kiwi, grapefruit is rich in vitamin C, as one half of a grapefruit has 39 milligrams of the nutrient, per the USDA. “Compounds [like vitamin C and potassium] in grapefruit can improve the elasticity of blood vessels and reduce oxidative stress, which helps the circulatory system function more efficiently and lowers blood pressure,” Keatley says.

Grapes are loaded with polyphenols, which are naturally-occurring chemical compounds found in plants, Cording explains. These support the elasticity of your blood vessels and reduce oxidative stress in the body, lowering the risk of inflammation—which is also a risk factor for high blood pressure, Gans adds.

Watermelon contains an amino acid called L- citrulline. It boosts the production of nitric acid in your body, helping to widen those blood vessels and lower your blood pressure, Keatley says.

Like bananas, avocados pack a potassium punch—576 milligrams is in 100 grams, or 3/4 of a cup, per the USDA—to help with blood pressure management, Keatley says. They may also help with weight management thanks to the fact the contain healthy fats that will keep you full and satiated—and that can indirectly help to reduce blood pressure, he says.

Remember: High blood pressure is complex, and consuming more fruit alone is unlikely to make it to drop. However, eating more nutritiously can support your other efforts to bring it down. If you have high blood pressure, it’s important to work closely with your healthcare provider to come up with a comprehensive plan to help turn things around.

#5030. Understanding Commitment Uncertainty in Relationships

We’re all holding onto something we’re sure we can’t give up on, but research shows that letting go can boost our happiness a lot more than we might think.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted October 14, 2026 | Reviewed by Monica Vilhauer Ph.D.

Commitment uncertainty is one of the major challenges couples may present with in couples therapy. Commitment is typically a fundamental part of an exclusive romantic relationship. It influences how partners invest in the relationship, identify with one another, and engage with their shared life. Yet, partners may become unsure whether they want to remain committed, whether the relationship will continue, or whether they want to keep investing in it. This is referred to as commitment uncertainty. Understanding this uncertainty is important because a lack of commitment is strongly associated with separation and divorce.

Commitment uncertainty emerges after partners have formed a mutually agreed-upon relationship, such as an exclusive monogamous relationship, and it differs from the uncertainty that can occur during early dating, when the relationship status has not yet been defined.

Several theoretical models describe two broad aspects of commitment. The first concerns wanting to be in the relationship, while the second concerns forces that encourage a person to remain in the relationship.

Research distinguishes these as dedication commitment and constraint commitment. Dedication includes a strong sense of couple identity, a desire for the relationship to continue long term, giving the relationship priority, and a willingness to sacrifice for it. As partners experience their relationship as meaningful and meeting their needs, they tend to pay less attention to alternatives. Constraint commitment refers to difficulties that bind partners together. These can include investments in the relationship, concerns about the well-being of a partner or children, financial circumstances, and social pressure. Constraints can encourage partners to remain together and may also encourage them to work on their relationship. However, constraint commitment alone is unlikely to be sufficient for a functional and healthy relationship in the long term.

Supported by research findings, my experience as a licensed psychologist counselling individuals and couples from all over the world revealed several ways in which commitment uncertainty can emerge in an intimate relationship.

Uncertainty may begin with an event that becomes a

turning point in the relationship. Such an event can lead partners to reevaluate their relationship and its future.

Commitment uncertainty may also develop slowly as connection and engagement between partners deteriorate. At the same time, partners may avoid confronting their uncertainty. Existing constraints, such as living together or having children, can help sustain the relationship and make it possible for partners to resist the growing uncertainty.

In other cases, commitment uncertainty may become apparent without a specific event. Partners may recognise fundamental compatibility problems and realise that critical issues in the relationship were not adequately considered earlier.

For some couples, uncertainty may have been present from the beginning of the relationship. A relationship may begin with low levels of adjustment and remain consistently low in adjustment.

Furthermore, there is a critical factor associated with commitment uncertainty: attachment insecurity, comprised of two dimensions, attachment anxiety and attachment avoidance. Relationship uncertainty is known to mediate the association between both attachment dimensions and commitment.

However, the two attachment dimensions are associated with uncertainty in different ways.

Attachment anxiety involves a strong desire for intimacy and protection alongside concerns about a partner's availability and commitment, fear of abandonment, and concerns about one's own value to the partner. Increasing attachment anxiety is also associated with jealousy and greater emotional ups and downs.

Attachment avoidance, on the other hand, involves discomfort with closeness and dependence, less reliance on relationship partners for care, and a preference for distance. Lower trust in partners and perceptions of others as negative or unreliable are seen as factors associated with increased doubts about the relationship.

The indirect association with commitments was stronger for attachment anxiety than for attachment avoidance.

Both external and internal factors can contribute to the persistence of commitment uncertainty.

External constraints can make it difficult to act on uncertainty. Concern about children, for example, may encourage partners to remain together and attempt to work through their difficulties. Financial constraints may have a similar effect. These pressures can help couples remain together while facing uncertainty, but they can also result in partners staying together without addressing the underlying uncertainty, potentially contributing to increasing negativity in the long term.

Internal processes can also interfere with fully addressing uncertainty. Denial, dissociation, and avoidance may reduce contact with the emotional turmoil associated with uncertainty. Such processes may also be expressed behaviourally, including through an affair.

First of all, commitment uncertainty can concern either a person’s own commitment to the relationship or their perception of their partner’s commitment.

Secondly, it is useful to consider dedication and constraint commitment, as these different aspects can help clarify the nature of the couple's commitment and how it developed.

For example, a feeling of having been trapped by circumstances may indicate that commitment was not fully established initially and may contribute to continuing uncertainty.

During a course of my integrative couples therapy, online or in person, I tend to discuss commitment uncertainty during joint sessions and explore it further in individual sessions, while establishing clear boundaries and explicit confidentiality arrangements from the outset.

A particularly difficult situation arises when couples describe their position as: “I would be more committed if things improved.” The “things” may include communication, intimacy, or other difficulties within the relationship.

This creates a “chicken-and-egg” dilemma. A partner may say that commitment will follow improvement, while the lack of commitment itself reduces motivation to change relationship behaviour.

There may be two possible ways of addressing this dilemma. The couple may directly address whether the relationship should continue, or they may temporarily put the question of commitment aside and work on other relationship difficulties.

Some couples may agree to set aside questions about continuing the relationship for a set period. This can provide stability while they work on the relationship and prevent the possibility of separation from becoming a bargaining tool during everyday disagreements.

If the purpose of couples therapy is instead to clarify the future of the relationship, commitment itself needs to remain central to the work. The process involves examining the factors influencing the decision, considering the consequences of staying or leaving, and distinguishing relationship distress from a desire to end the relationship.

Traditional measures of successful couples therapy often focus on improved relationship functioning or avoiding separation or divorce. However, for couples experiencing commitment uncertainty, remaining together is not necessarily the only possible outcome of therapy. The outcome may include separating well or achieving satisfactory individual well-being.

I find it important to focus couples therapy on reducing avoidance and increasing positive engagement with the question of commitment.

In addition, couples therapy should not rush towards a decision. A demand for certainty may itself represent avoidance; thus, space for thoughtful decision-making needs to be allowed.

To sum up, for couples experiencing commitment uncertainty, therapy may involve exploring how that uncertainty developed, what maintains it, and what they want the outcome of therapy to be.

Şenkal Ertürk, İ., Akyüz, A. N., & Büyükşahin Sunal, A. (2025). Attachment Insecurities and Commitment: The Mediator Role of Relationship Uncertainty. Psychological reports, 332941251351244. Advance online publication. https://doi.org/10.1177/00332941251351244

Owen, J., Rhoades, G., Shuck, B., Fincham, F. D., Stanley, S., Markman, H., & Knopp, K. (2014). Commitment uncertainty: A theoretical overview. Couple and Family Psychology: Research and Practice, 3(4), 207–219. https://doi.org/10.1037/cfp0000028

#5029. OKX draws $445M in net inflows as Binance outflows exceed $7B

OKX led cryptocurrency exchanges with $445 million in daily inflows, while Binance processed about $7 billion in net outflows.

OKX recorded about $445 million in 24-hour net inflows, the largest among tracked crypto exchanges, while Binance continued to register outflows after reopening withdrawals following its $6.3 billion hack.

Crypto exchange Bybit was in second place with $112 million in net inflows, followed by Gate with $91 million and Bitget with $54 million in 24-hour net inflows, according to DefiLlama.

Binance recorded the biggest net outflows among tracked exchanges, with more than $7 billion, up from $5.8 billion earlier Monday. Binance said it began restoring withdrawals in phases at 6:00 a.m. UTC on Sunday.

At the time of writing, Binance held about $127.9 billion in wallets tracked by DefiLlama, more than four times the $29 billion at OKX, the second-largest exchange by that measure.

Binance suspended withdrawals Friday, soon after late Thursday’s $6.3 billion hack, the largest in cryptocurrency history.

By Sunday, Binance said it had replenished approximately 95% of the affected assets, up from 90% on Saturday. The remaining 5% consisted of less liquid tokens that it continued to buy through market and over-the-counter transactions.

The exchange is using its corporate treasury, a partial deployment of its Secure Asset Fund for Users (SAFU) and a portion of its futures insurance fund to replace the stolen assets. Binance has pledged to absorb the losses without passing them on to customers.

About $3 billion in stolen assets frozen by industry partners is separate from Binance’s replacement funds. Binance said Sunday it published a new reserves snapshot and an updated proof-of-reserves report was expected Monday.

OKX’s OKB token rose about 1.4% over the past 24 hours to trade near $113 on Monday, while BNB gained 0.3% to about $708 over the same period, according to CoinMarketCap.

#5028. BTCPay Tells Merchants to Switch Off Servers Amid Hot Wallet Attack Reports

BTCPay Tells Merchants to Switch Off Servers Amid Hot Wallet Attack Reports

BTCPay Server, a free, open-source tool businesses use to accept Bitcoin payments, told operators to switch off their servers on Sunday after reports that attackers were draining the hot wallets connected to them.

"We are receiving reports of hot wallets on BTCPay Servers being swept by unauthorized attackers," the project tweeted at 5:25 p.m. ET. "If you're running an instance, please shut it down (./btcpay-down.sh) until we complete the investigation."

🚨 We are receiving reports of hot wallets on BTCPay Servers being swept by unauthorized attackers.

If you're running an instance, please shut it down (./btcpay-down.sh) until we complete the investigation.

— BTCPay Server (@BtcpayServer) November 1, 2026

About two hours later, BTCPay founder Nicolas Dorier said the reports appeared to involve releases older than version 2.4.2, and that the attacker had logged in to affected servers with administrator access.

Later on Sunday, the project said its investigation had identified an attack vector targeting instances running version 2.4.1, released on July 23, or older. "We have no credible reports of hot wallet drains for users running the latest 2.4.7 release," it said, urging everyone to keep their software updated and asking affected users to send details and logs to its security team.

Some hosting providers, including LunaNode, have shut down BTCPay instances automatically to stop older versions from being exploited, the project said. BTCPay has not said how many servers were affected or how much Bitcoin was taken.

Because BTCPay is self-hosted, each merchant runs the software on their own server, and there is no central operator that can switch instances off. Merchants can choose to run a hot wallet, which stores the wallet's seed on that internet-connected server so it can sign transactions automatically. BTCPay's own documentation labels the feature "DANGER" and warns that it "comes with security implications."

The attacks come days after BTCPay released version 2.4.7 on Thursday as an emergency update fixing one critical and several medium-severity vulnerabilities, which it said were brought to its attention through Anthropic's frontier open-source software scanner. BTCPay has not said whether the attacks exploited any of those flaws.

It is not the first security scare for the project this year. In August, BTCPay warned that a critical vulnerability in all versions before 2.4.2 was being actively exploited, and told users to update or shut down their servers. Supporters of the project later funded a recovery bounty of 10% of any stolen funds returned, capped at 3 BTC.

#5026. BTC heads into US midterm showdown: Three things to know in Bitcoin this week

Bitcoin price action held its 100-day moving average into the monthly close as crypto markets braced for US midterm election and tech earnings volatility.

Bitcoin (BTC) held above its 100-day moving average on Monday ahead of US midterm elections, jobs data and earnings from Apple and Palantir.

Tuesday’s US midterm elections will determine control of Congress, with prediction market traders heavily favoring a Democratic victory in the House of Representatives.

Kalshi put the probability of Democrats winning the House at 93.6% as of Monday.

President Donald Trump said he would use his veto power if Democrats took control of Congress.

“I guess I’ll become Mr Veto. They’ll be sending stuff up. You veto it,” he said, according to the New York Post.

US midterm elections odds (screenshot). Source: Kalshi

Bitcoin ended the fourth quarter lower in each of the past three US midterm election years, falling 17.3% in 2014, 43.5% in 2018 and 14.8% in 2022. All three periods coincided with Bitcoin bear markets.

Benjamin Cowen, founder and CEO of crypto analytics platform IntoTheCryptoverse, suggested that the midterms can function as a cue to increase BTC exposure due to their short-term bearish impact on price.

“Usually the Q4 dump of midterm years just provide opportunities to buy for those who sold long ago,” he said Friday on X.

In July, Cowen referenced Bitcoin bear-market bottoms coinciding with the second half of midterm years.

BTC/USD was down 6.4% for the quarter as of Monday, after its weakest October since 2014, according to CoinGlass.

BTC/USD liquidation heatmap. Source: CoinGlass

September US job openings data are due Tuesday, followed by October’s nonfarm payrolls report on Friday.

Payrolls are expected to rise by 65,000, after the economy added 29,000 jobs in September, below forecasts for 84,000. Such an outcome notionally increases the odds of dovish policy signals from the Federal Reserve, which is in turn bullish for crypto and risk-asset liquidity.

Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack and Fed Governor Michelle Bowman are among the officials due to speak this week.

Data from CME Group’s FedWatch Tool currently shows that markets expect the Fed to raise interest rates by 0.25 percentage points at the December meeting of the Federal Open Market Committee (FOMC). The odds of such an outcome have sat between 67% and 75% for the past month.

Fed target rate probability comparison for December FOMC meeting (screenshot). Source: CME Group

The University of Michigan will also release its preliminary November consumer sentiment survey on Friday, including consumers’ inflation expectations.

Alongside the macro data prints, 20% of S&P 500 companies are due to report earnings in the coming days. Chief among these are tech giants Apple and Palantir, both due to report after the market close on Monday.

The reports follow last week’s tech sell-off, when disappointing guidance renewed concerns about AI spending and profitability.

Economist Mohamed El-Erian described markets as now “acutely sensitive” to tech-sector news headlines in weekend analysis.

“The market is particularly focused on justification for the massive capital expenditures being deployed across the sector, ROI’s, and revenue growth,” he wrote.

After closing October down 7.3%, Bitcoin is holding above a moving average that has provided support since mid-August.

Related: Here’s what happened in crypto today

TradingView data showed BTC/USD staying above last week’s low near $76,300, without retesting it around the monthly close.

That low was close to the 100-day simple moving average (SMA) for BTC/USD, which has since risen to $76,548.

Bitcoin also reclaimed its 100-week exponential moving average (EMA) in time for the monthly close, with this standing at $77,759.

BTC/USD daily chart with 100 SMA, 100 EMA. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Trader and analyst Rekt Capital compared the weekly chart with Bitcoin’s rebound from its 2022 bear market low. During the recovery, BTC/USD produced an inverse head-and-shoulders pattern, retested support at the right shoulder, briefly fell below it and then rallied.

Last week’s drop to $76,300 brought Bitcoin back to what Rekt Capital identified as right-shoulder support on the current chart. He is watching for a brief move below that level as a possible next step in the comparison.

“The question now is whether Bitcoin can produce some sort of downside deviation below here over the next few weeks,” Rekt Capital said.

BTC/USD one-week chart. Source: Rekt Capital on X.com

#5025. The 60 Best White Elephant Gift Ideas of 2026 Are Worth the Steal

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

A White Elephant gift exchange can go so many ways. First, you have to find the perfect present to delight your friends, horrify your family, and prank your colleagues. Then comes the gift-stealing chaos and the excitement of what you'll ultimately take home. If that makes you skeptical, trust that it's FUN.

For those who haven't had the pleasure, White Elephant is a holiday game where everyone brings a gift—usually with a set price limit. The gifts are usually a little bit off-color, though not always. But it's not another game of Secret Santa. Instead, White Elephant kicks off with someone picking a present at random, so whatever you gift has to have pretty wide appeal.

From there, everyone can either steal a gift that's already been opened or pick a new one. Whether you can steal gifts back is a key distinction before things get competitive and you end up with a bunch of Grinches on your hands.

Ready to steal your way to a happy holiday season? We curated a list of the 60 best White Elephant gifts that are all under $50, fun, unique, and—best of all!—in stock. While we don’t advise procrastinating, almost all our picks are available on Amazon, so they’ll be at your doorstep in no time. Honestly, some of these are so good, you’ll be tempted to snag ‘em for yourself.

Bet on everyone to fight for the chance to print their favorite photos and memories instantly with this cute mini Bluetooth sticker printer. Just snap, print, and stick them in journals, scrapbooks, cards, and more. Plus, this printer uses thermal technology and no ink, so you won't have to worry about pesky refills.

Whoever takes this gift home can share it with the whole fam (and hopefully you, too). It's perfect for prepping s'mores without an open flame, thanks to the included flameless heater, which you can use inside on chilly nights or at any outdoor event where a bonfire just isn't in the cards.

Want a gift everyone will fight over? lululemon's editor-loved belt bag will test any parameters your game sets around stealing, thanks to its cult-favorite status. It'll fit a phone, cards, keys, AirPods, and a lip gloss or two, making it a great companion for hot girl walks.

Every good White Elephant game needs a slew of hilarious gag gifts among the practical offerings. We're pretty sure everyone will be crying with laughter over this "Jar of F*cks to Give." Even though every colleague has surely had one of those moments, this gift is maybe most appreciated by that work friend who *really* gets your sense of humor.

Okay, this list had to have one functional idea that was a bit pricier than the others, just in case your White Elephant exchange has a more elevated vibe. Anyone would love displaying this gorgeous, easy-to-use kettle on their kitchen counter. Designed by Drew Barrymore, it's a total win.

Doing puzzles is the ultimate crowd-pleasing cozy hobby, which means bringing one that looks like a work of art to the White Elephant exchange practically guarantees a steal or two. Piecework, a female-owned small business, makes some of the prettiest jigsaw puzzles we’ve ever seen, like this option, which is brimming with stunning florals.

I’m the type of person who always needs to be doing something with my hands while watching TV. That used to mean scrolling on my phone until I discovered these dot-by-letter pads. They don’t take a lot of brain power, but they’ll be so soothing and satisfying while they catch up on their Netflix queue.

Anyone who's often found at their go-to Pilates studio will appreciate this cute pair of grip socks, complete with stylish designs that will peek out of their sneakers and sweet ruffle details.

I dare you to find one person who wouldn’t benefit from an AirTag. Whether they clip it to their keys, stick it in their wallet, or tuck it into their luggage, it’s a small price for peace of mind.

If the friends in your White Elephant exchange love trying new recipes, this electric lunchbox makes it much easier to take leftovers to work the next day. It’ll keep soups, chilis, and warm meals at the perfect temperature. Plus, it has a leakproof lid and is dishwasher-safe!

A Squishmallow is the perfect cozy and cute addition to their bed set-up. For even more fun, throw this mystery box into the White Elephant exchange, so they can have their pick from three adorable stuffies.

I’ve completely ditched carrying around travel-sized hand sanitizer after discovering these viral sprays. Not only are they the perfect size to tuck inside any bag, but they smell so good. Trust me, whoever unwraps these during the gift exchange will rack up compliments once they start using them!

This holiday season, gift them the hair clip to rule them all. These viral hair clips are the perfect size to fit all of their hair, even when they’re in between cuts. Plus, isn’t this cherry-red hue perfect for the holiday season?

Sure, most people have their go-to candle scents, but even the pickiest candle lovers won’t be able to resist this cereal bowl candle. Not only does it look extremely lifelike, but it smells like French vanilla!

Whether they can’t resist murder mysteries or have gotten sucked into the viral “brain stacking” trend on TikTok, Murdle is the perfect answer. Each book comes with 100 logic puzzles in which they’ll use a series of clues to figure out the culprit. Trust me, they’ll become addicted to these fun brain games!

If your White Elephant game is full of beauty lovers, this editor-loved, glossy lip balm is sure to spark friendly competition. "It deeply hydrates and smooths my lips, leaving them soft and supple with a touch of natural shine," skincare expert Mary Berry told Women's Health. If you're playing a game with a slightly higher budget, it's the perfect add-on.

You know that one friend who just can't seem to keep their phone charged? This well-reviewed, pocket-sized portable charger ensures those days are done. It comes complete with three different types of cables—and can charge up to five devices simultaneously—so all of their tech can stay powered on.

Odds are, this will be the most coveted White Elephant gift in the bunch! Not only are Owalas some of the most popular water bottles around, but they're also our editors' overall pick, thanks to their leak-proof design, ability to keep drinks cold for up to 24 hours, and locking mechanism (which doubles as a handle for taking it on the go). Plus, it comes in so many unique colorways!

Give a gift that will help someone have their coziest couch-lounging sesh ever. This sofa tray table can hold a drink, popcorn, remotes, or whatever someone feeling lazy needs to keep nearby. This is especially nice for people who live in small spaces without a ton of room for a coffee table.

If you're playing White Elephant with people who love to travel, this duffle is a must. Not only does it have a shoe compartment and a separate wet bag for dirty clothes, but it even comes with a built-in USB port to keep devices juiced up on the go.
